That the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint himself had leapt into such a situation was his limitation, and a weakness those who called themselves the righteous had no choice but to bear.

Ho Ga-Myung cast a quick glance at Chung Myung. The pair of dark flickering eyes suddenly cooled.

This was no time to be intoxicated with victory. He had to make the next move before that fellow stirred.

“Nothing has changed.”

Everything still existed smoothly within his awareness.

Chung Myung bit his lip, unable to hold back his anger.

‘Ho Ga-Myung.’

He glared fiercely at the distant Ho Ga-Myung. He wanted to rush over and cut that man’s throat, but the wily fox would never allow him such proximity.

The unease Chung Myung had felt toward Ho Ga-Myung began to take shape in this moment.

Chung Myung’s explosive power and unexpected ingenuity were disastrously mismatched with those who, unperturbed by circumstances, did their duty with cold thoroughness.

‘Damn!’

Chung Myung glanced slightly behind him.

Southern Island Sect was resisting desperately, but it was mere endurance.

But withdrawing was difficult. The moment they charged out chasing the enemy, those who hadn’t escaped the gorge would be exposed helplessly to the rain of attacks.

‘There aren’t enough blades!’

Not just holding the line, but blades that could move alone and cleave the enemy...!

Chung Myung grabbed Yu Yiseol by the shoulder and pulled her abruptly.

When Yu Yiseol looked at him with startled eyes, Chung Myung charged back toward the rear and shouted.

“Go quickly and help that side, sago!”

“Sajil!”

“Go!”

Only then did Yu Yiseol fully see the situation. She understood why Chung Myung had shoved her. The side that needed help now was there—more than Chung Myung himself.

But...

She saw Chung Myung’s leg flinch for an instant as he pushed off the ground. It could no longer be fully hidden.

Both were in danger. Neither could be abandoned.

Then what on earth...

“I told you to go!”

Chung Myung barked loudly at the confused Yu Yiseol, even though Gwi Yang might charge again at any moment.

Seeing her sajil’s bloodshot eyes, she clenched her fists until her knuckles bled.

She knew.

She had learned. She had been taught. She had rehearsed it.

She knew, to the point of hating it, exactly where a swordsman must run when faced with a choice where neither side could be taken.

The teachings her sect had given her now felt like a sharp dagger. It pained her that she could never turn away from that learning.

Gritting her teeth, Yu Yiseol turned and, as if shaking off lingering regrets, shot off like a beam of light.

Seeing that, Chung Myung gave the same order to Tang Pae.

“Go there together and help!”

“Yes!”

Tang Pae also ran back without complaint.

After sending the two off, Chung Myung stared at Gwi Yang. Gwi Yang flicked the sword in his hand and advanced slowly.

“I thought you’d at least say thank you?”

“That would be for me to hear, rather.”

“Heh heh heh heh.”

A gleam of smug satisfaction flashed in Gwi Yang’s eyes.

He wasn’t wrong. It was true they had given time to tidy the situation, but that was not necessarily disadvantageous to Gwi Yang.

Why stop a wounded prey that had fallen from the pack and remained alone?

He didn’t care about the others’ situations. The only thing that held his interest was Chung Myung before him.

He could see it: the convulsing wounded leg, the side where hastily applied pressure had split open and bled again.

Not only that. His whole body was riddled with countless small wounds, and his face was sallow as if from internal injury.

He had surely already lost a considerable amount of blood.

In such a state, even the greatest warrior in the land would be unable to display even a fraction of his true power.

A short breath escaped Chung Myung’s lips.

Honestly, this was getting a bit too much.

Perhaps from blood loss, his vision blurred. The hand gripping his sword had lost strength. He was used to pain, but the helplessness that comes after being utterly exhausted never grew familiar.

But he still had to fight.

“A weary business, isn’t it.”

Gwi Yang sneered as he closed the distance.

“To know you must retreat and yet be unable to retreat—”

“...”

“—even though you plainly know it’s the road to death.”

Despite the blatant mockery, Chung Myung instead gave a faint laugh.

“Do you know why you’re weak?”

“What?”

Gwi Yang narrowed his eyes at the unexpected question. Chung Myung mocked him and said:

“Because you ran when you wanted to run.”

“...”

“The reason I’m strong is simple too. When I wanted to run, I couldn’t.”

Be it at any time.

They didn’t understand what it meant to be strong. They didn’t know what he had truly wanted to become.

Chung Myung was still being pursued.

One who had never once retreated. One who had been stronger than anyone in the world.

“Talk your nonsense in hell.”

Gwi Yang, flanked by the Blood Sword Troops members, charged in at once.

In front of Chung Myung, who met the charge without backing down, the back of the one he had always watched flickered dimly.

Chapter 1329: Nothing Has Changed. (4)

Puuuk!

“Aaaaaah!”

An arrow sank into his chest. A Southern Island disciple screamed, unable to bear the pain.

They could, of course, bat away ordinary falling arrows. They hadn’t trained carelessly until now.

But when arrowheads struck the sword barriers the elders had formed, shattered, and sprayed outwards, there was nothing they could do to stop them embedding everywhere.

“Kuh, kuuh...”

A burning pain in his chest drained strength from his entire body. He sank down clutching his sternum.

“Let go!”

At that moment, as if sensing it, Tang Soso dashed over, ripped his hand away, and checked the wound.

“Miss... back...”

Another shard of a broken arrow flew and lodged in Tang Soso’s back as she sat before the fallen man. She didn’t flinch and began to probe his wound.

“Hold on!”

Clenching her teeth, Tang Soso pressed a suture needle to the injured man’s wound and plunged it in without hesitation.

Crunch!

At the same time, the lodged arrowhead burst through his back.

“Waaaah!”

A desperate scream tore out of him, but Tang Soso held him down and stuffed antidote and a styptic into the gaping wound. Her hands were soon stained dark with blood.

To the eye, it was an exceedingly brutal method. But there was no other choice now. Left as is, the arrowheads would sink further, twist through the body, and might reach the heart.

If there wasn’t time to carefully split the wound and remove the arrowhead, this way was actually safer.

Having quickly bound the wound, Tang Soso left the semi-unconscious man and lunged to the side to treat another who groaned with an arrow lodged in his throat.

But before she could reach him, he pulled the arrow from his own throat. Blood gushed out in choked waves.

“Ah, no!”

Tang Soso screamed and desperately clamped the wound with both hands.

“Kk, kkrrk...”

Foamy blood bubbled up in his mouth and the color drained from the man’s face.

“Hold on! Just hold on a bit longer! I’ll save you, so hang on!”

But despite her cry, life drained quickly from his eyes. The light of living faded.

Tang Soso’s fingertips trembled faintly. The blood she felt was still so hot, yet he was no longer among the living.

She squeezed her eyes shut and leapt up. She ran toward another falling figure. She didn’t want to cry, but tears kept coming at every chance. She could barely see ahead.

But there was no time to wipe them or to grieve.

Powerless. Miserably so.

No matter what she did, she couldn’t save the dying. There was no way to stop the light going out of the eyes of those who looked at her, pleading to be saved.

Had she known that being a physician was this torturous, she would never have taken up medicine.

Yet she ignored despair and kept moving. Among all those here, she was the only physician.

Paaahng!

At that moment, a massive iron bolt, loaded with enormous internal qi, hurtled toward her at tremendous speed. Tang Soso’s eyes widened as she sensed it. Before she could react, someone streaked in like lightning and smashed the bolt aside.

“Sasuk!”

It was Baek Cheon. He gritted his teeth and stared toward where the bolt had come from. He wanted to dash over and tear those damned bastards limb from limb, but there was no way he could leave here now.

‘Yoon Jong, Jo Gul! Hurry...!’

He had no choice but to trust those who had gone and hold the line.

“They’re coming again!”

Baek Cheon’s gaze snapped up to the sky. The Southern Island elders who had just held off the bombardment hadn’t yet regained their footing, and more arrows were already pouring down.

‘No!’

Baek Cheon leapt into the air. His eyes were bloodshot. Even without help he had to do something; otherwise, he’d be forced to watch people die with their eyes open again.

He squeezed out the last of his internal qi from his dantian. But before he could push it into his sword, someone beside him soared even higher.

“Samae!”

At that familiar silhouette Baek Cheon couldn’t help but shout. Yu Yiseol, leaping up like a phoenix, swung her sword at the raining arrows.

Sshhhh!

With a sound like steel scraping silk, Yu Yiseol’s sword drew a great circle in the air, like a full moon.

The Moon Maiden Sword at its peak.

The sword qi formed by that arc acted like a huge umbrella in a storm, stopping the deluge of arrows.

“Samae, be careful! There’s bombardment incoming!”

Baek Cheon shouted urgently. At that moment something streaked past him at incredible speed.

‘Dagger?’

A dagger flew like a vortex cutting through the air. Whirling fiercely, it shot straight into the forest ahead.

“Aaaaaah!”

It was distant and faint, but a scream was definitely heard.

That dagger must have achieved something. Tang Pae, who had arrived a bit later than the dagger I fired, rolled to avoid the rain of arrows and shouted.

“I can keep the cannon fire in check! Just stop the arrows, Acting Sect Leader!”

“Yes!”

Baek Cheon landed lightly and felt strength returning to his body.

Only two people had joined, yet the air seemed to clear in an instant. It was a moment that proved how much power a single master could bring on the martial world.

The others weren’t idle either.

“Damn it! Stop pushing forward and stick to the cliff face!”

Im So-Byeong shouted the command.

“Mount Hua people and young lords, don’t waste strength on arrows—deal only with the cannonballs! Elders, while stopping arrows, cling so the person behind has their chest against your back! Then you only need to defend above—idiots!”

A command given in the chaos becomes a landmark by its mere presence. It didn’t matter whether the one issuing it was the leader of the Evil Sect or not. To save themselves, everyone instinctively followed the order.

Paaaah!

Yu Yiseol touched down and charged forward at tremendous speed.

“Samae!”

Baek Cheon called urgently, but Yu Yiseol didn’t even turn her head.

Paaaaaang!

Perhaps perceiving Yu Yiseol’s movement as a threat, a bolt at terrifying speed flew at her. But she, still running, sliced her sword and struck the incoming bolt.

Clang!

Metal clashed with metal in a sharp ringing. The deflected bolt sliced along Yu Yiseol’s shoulder as it passed.

But Yu Yiseol seemed not to care about the wound... rather, she ran as if unaware she’d been cut. She looked unbearably urgent.

Sensing something wrong, Baek Cheon took a step instinctively and then stopped. He bit the inside of his cheek.

Bodies were piling on the ground. The Southern Island disciples’ and elders’ corpses were studded with arrows like hedgehogs.

What he had to do was protect everyone here—reduce the number of people ending up like that by even one.

His hand holding the sword trembled as his internal qi waned, but there was no room for weak words.

Kwaaaang! Kwaaaaang!

At the bombardment roaring at their ears again, Baek Cheon lashed out at the incoming shells as if in a frenzy.

Blood seeped from his bitten lip. But Jo Gul felt no pain.

There were archers ahead firing arrows like rain. Charging there and cutting them down wasn’t that difficult.

But Jo Gul suppressed that urge as if driving a blade into his thigh.

‘Where are you!’

He was definitely nearby. One of the archers firing those brutal bolts! If they didn’t deal with him, the main force couldn’t move.

‘Where are you!’

Jo Gul swiftly scanned the surroundings. The razor-edge sense of Tanger spread rapidly in all directions.

A moment later.

“Found you!”

Jo Gul bent his waist and surged forward like an arrow.

The archer, realizing his position had been exposed, fired arrow after arrow at Jo Gul—a terrifying rapid shot. Dozens of bolts rained down on him at once.

But Jo Gul’s speed was unmatched. His sword moved as if splitting light, knocking every flying arrow aside.

“You bastard!”

Even as the attacks poured in, Jo Gul’s speed didn’t falter, and bewilderment crept into the masked archer’s eyes.

But only for a moment—realizing rapid fire alone wouldn’t be enough, the archer poured his mood and inner energy into his arrows.

Wooooooong!

When internal qi is put into a blade it resonates. The arrows in his hand began to writhe like living carp. Nocking one, he pulled the steel-twisted bowstring to the breaking point.

Kkrrrk!

With the bow drawn to its limit he took aim at Jo Gul.

But Jo Gul, knowing a bolt laden with tremendous internal qi was aimed at him, didn’t dodge; he ran straight toward it.

As the distance closed so their eyes met.

Paang!

The archer released the string. The arrow spun fiercely and flew straight for Jo Gul.

Kwaaaaah!

Air coiled toward the arrowhead, creating a vortex in the air that resembled an enraged dragon.

Jo Gul, resisting it, drew his sword hand back over his left shoulder.

“Haap!”

With a shout, the edge of his Plum Blossom Sword struck the incoming arrow with all his might.

Kaaaaaang!

A metallic sound, almost like an explosion, rang sharply.

Even with all his force in the blade, the internal qi-filled arrow did not bounce off; it advanced as if to bite into Jo Gul’s sword. The arrowhead’s spinning against the blade made a chilling grinding sound.

A normal iron sword would have shattered instantly under that impact. But the Plum Blossom Sword, infused with the Tang Family’s essence, stubbornly withstood the spinning, burrowing arrow.

And at that moment.

“Taaap!”

With a fierce shout, Jo Gul thrust his left hand toward the sword. His hand, stained green with Mount Hua’s Bamboo Leaf Palm technique, slammed hard against the opposite side of the blade where the arrow had struck.

Kwaaaang!

The added shock to the sword twisted the arrow’s trajectory. Still, its remaining force grazed Jo Gul’s side.

Jo Gul’s flank bore a hollowed, semicircular wound, and the left hand that struck the blade was cut so deeply bone showed.

The price bought him only the distance of about three paces to the enemy.

“Ugh!”

The archer quickly leapt back, drew another arrow from his quiver, and nocked it.

Just as he raised the bow to aim at his opponent, a streak of light flew into his sight.

Paaaah!

A swift sword pierced the archer’s throat in an instant.

“Gak...”

The archer gave a strangled sound and crumpled with a look of disbelief.

‘Couldn’t even see it...’

Thud.

Jo Gul, having pulled the sword from the archer’s neck, clenched his blood-soaked left hand and drove his foot into the ground.

‘Where next!’

The mischievous, lively expression that was always on his face had been replaced by a cold, blade-like countenance.

Chapter 1330: Nothing Has Changed. (5)

Bang!

Each step unleashed an explosive boom.

This was not the Yu Yiseol who was always quiet and nimble like a swallow. Urgency she could not hide emanated from her now.

‘Faster!’

Her expressionless face contorted more and more as her anxiety grew.

Whoooosh!

Accelerating so the scenery blurred around her, she ran, slicing off the neck of an Evil Sect member who got in her way in an instant.

There was something about the sword strike she gave now that resembled Chung Myung’s.

‘Faster!’

She kept stamping the ground.

Attached to her sword was not just her own life. It wasn’t only the lives of the Southern Island sect disciples struggling under the rain of arrows flying from behind her.

Chung Myung’s lonely back, standing alone to hold the line after sending her on, kept haunting her. If she failed to clean this up in time and return, that back might be the last image of Chung Myung she would ever see.

‘Too slow!’

Yu Yiseol, seized by impatience, stamped the ground again hard.

Kwang!

Softness and calm were strengths unique to Yu Yiseol that no one in Mount Hua possessed. It was the path she had chosen to avoid falling behind those born with strength.

A sword style not beholden to physical power, one that could be built through bone-grinding effort even if not innate.

That was the blade of the sword ghost Yu Yiseol.

Shriiiing!

A force-infused heavy arrow flew at tremendous speed. She lowered her body fully without slowing. It was almost as if she were skimming the ground.

The fiercely shot arrow sliced through the air in vain.

Wham! Wham!

Arrows came in succession, but Yu Yiseol’s technique would not permit a single arrow to touch her body.

Yu Yiseol cut the throat of the archer nocking the bowstring in one stroke.

Swoosh!

Her blade traced a graceful curve and, in an instant, cut through the archer’s upraised bow and severed the carotid. It was a single, marvelous stroke that drew gasps from anyone.

Yet Yu Yiseol bit her lip so hard it bled.

‘Too slow!’

This was wrong. She had to be faster. More efficient.

I think I understand.

Why Chung Myung’s sword was so often frantic. Why there was no leisure in his blade.

‘Faster!’

She bit the flesh inside her cheek and ran toward another spot where arrows had been fired.

She knew, of course. The people she sought were not there. Those firing the heavy arrows were still hidden to avoid revealing their positions.

But she had no time to hunt them down one by one now.

Then there was only one method.

Instead of her finding them, make them find her.

If she fought most loudly at the center and slaughtered many enemies, those who couldn’t stand it would charge at her.

Then, at the cost of exposing herself to danger, she could find the enemies and lure attacks away from the main force...

Grit!

The thought flashing through her head made Yu Yiseol involuntarily bite her lip hard.

Why push yourself so far? That much?

It was something she had once blurted out thoughtlessly. She hadn’t understood Chung Myung then, and had no intention of trying to understand him; that careless remark finally lodged in her chest now.

It wasn’t that she distrusted the sahyung sajaes. She had never doubted their skill.

Still, she must overextend herself.

Because that way she could save even one more person.

Still, she had to push herself.

So the enemies would target her and not others.

Fighting most brilliantly at the front was simply the best choice. The more she leapt into the enemy center and drew attention with her blade, the safer everyone else became.

She saw enemies arrayed up ahead.

Seeing her rush headlong without looking back or ahead, the archers’ faces went dark.

“Shoot! Fire!”

The archers twisted their upraised bows and aimed at Yu Yiseol. The arrows nocked several per bow rained down on her like a storm.

Instead of slowing, Yu Yiseol drove her sword into the ground. The resilient Plum Blossom Sword bent as if to break and then snapped back. Using that force, she leapt into the air.

Screeeech!

A speeding arrow grazed her pantleg, but unfazed, Yu Yiseol shot up like a raptor and thrust herself into the midst of the enemies.

The Plum Blossom Sword traced a far rougher, fiercer arc than her usual trajectory.

Kwagagagak!

A steel bow was cut off along with a body. As the split bow and torso flew apart, blood splattered above her head.

Bang!

As soon as her foot hit the ground she struck, quickly plunging her sword into the throat of a startled, recoiling archer. There was a gruesome cracking sound as the throat was pierced; the blade protruding from the nape was red.

“Kill, kill her!”

“She’s alone anyway! Kill her!”

There’s a common belief that once an archer’s distance is closed, he can do nothing.

But a well-known saying isn’t always true. An archer with his string released can still wield a powerful short-staff-like weapon, and close distance doesn’t mean he can’t shoot.

In fact, as the distance shortens, arrows can become even more devastating to the target.

The archers surged forward, swinging their short, strung crossbows. Those behind them held their bowstrings taut, ready to fire at any opening.

Kraaang!

Yu Yiseol deflected a steel crossbow aimed at her head in an instant. But before she could brace her blade again, another archer leapt up nearby and fired a force-infused arrow at point-blank range.

Without thinking, Yu Yiseol leaned back. The arrow whistled past right above her shoulder, tearing a few strands of hair, and mercilessly pierced the belly of a Four Sects Alliance man who was charging from behind.

“Kyaaaargh!”

The fired arrow had harmed an ally, but no one cared. Archers who surged up alongside continued to rain swift shots at Yu Yiseol.

Faced with countless arrows pouring in at close range, all Yu Yiseol could do was roll her body and dodge.

Papapapat!

The spot she rolled out of was instantly packed with arrows. If she’d been a moment slower, the outcome would’ve been obvious.

‘Different.’

Yu Yiseol sprang up like lightning and charged the nearest visible enemy. Her body moved more readily than she expected.

“U-uh!”

Shing!

After driving her sword into the startled enemy’s belly, Yu Yiseol grabbed his throat, yanked hard, and spun him. In an instant he rolled and their positions swapped.

“Ah, no...”

Thrown into that forced reversal, he sensed his approaching fate and frantically turned his head.

Kwaduk! Kwadudduk!

Before his head had fully turned, arrows rained and embedded themselves all over his back. Using his body as a shield against the arrows, Yu Yiseol drew her sword and cut down others immediately.

The moment you leap into a pack of wolves, you must be prepared to pay the price. No matter how fierce, one cannot come out of their midst entirely unscathed.

Yu Yiseol knew that well. Doing the impossible out of desperation would be nothing but suicide.

And yet there was only one reason she leapt into the wolf pack’s center.

“Aaaaaaah!”

Several of the group surrounding Yu Yiseol let out screams. They were helplessly struck by a blade from behind. They died without even seeing who had cut them.

“Sago!”

It was Yoon Jong. He had calmly but swiftly broken the enemy lines from the outside.

As harrowing screams kept sounding from outside the encirclement, the archers’ hands trembled slightly.

A bow, unlike a spear or sword, can only aim at one place. There is no bow that can fire at enemies surrounding you front and back at once.

As if to prove that an archer is weakest not when the distance shortens but when the directions to aim become mixed, two Mount Hua people scattered sword qi from inside and outside simultaneously.

The splendidly blooming Plum Blossom Sword qi swept through those who had gathered to encircle Yu Yiseol.

“Aaaaah!”

“Kraaaaah!”

Enemies riddled with holes fell limply like old rag dolls.

Momentum had now shifted to Yu Yiseol. She seized it and began slaughtering enemies in an instant.

Whoooosh!

But then, a whistling sound rose high. A heavy arrow of terrifying speed flew toward Yu Yiseol and Yoon Jong. It was clearly of a different caliber than the arrows the archers here had been shooting.

At last, having found the traces of those she had to chase, Yu Yiseol’s eyes flashed a deadly blue.

But before she could push off the ground, someone was already running in that direction.

“Gul?”

Jo Gul, his face unusually cold, dashed into the forest like a heroic guard. It didn’t take long to learn the result. A death shriek that was clearly not Jo Gul’s was heard.

Once. Again.

A total of three screams burst out. The moment the heavy arrows were fired, Jo Gul had precisely located and memorized their positions, hunted them down, and turned them all into ‘prey’.

It was a calmness and decisiveness hardly recognizable in the Jo Gul everyone knew.

Despair filled the archers’ eyes when they realized even the supporting warriors had been taken out.

Naturally, as the assault slackened, Yu Yiseol staggered and clutched her stomach.

“Sa-Sago!”

Startled, Yoon Jong hurriedly grabbed her.

Blood seeped through the gaps of the hand clutching her belly. When she had used the enemy’s body as a shield to stop an arrow, that arrow which pierced the body had left a wound in Yu Yiseol’s abdomen.

“You bastards!”

A furious curse burst from Yoon Jong’s mouth. He swung his sword violently and sliced the archers apart.

“Aaaaaah!”

The shattered momentum could not be recovered. Judging survival more important than orders, the archers abandoned their comrades and fled without looking back.

“Sago! Are you all right?”

Yoon Jong did not pursue them and instead supported Yu Yiseol. Checking on her mattered far more than cutting down those scoundrels.

“Yoon Jong.”

“Sago, y-you’re wounded! You need treatment at once...”

“There are still cannons.”

“What?”

“Trust me!”

Yu Yiseol shoved Yoon Jong away and stamped the ground at the same time.

“Sago! No, you can’t! Sago! Sago!”

Yoon Jong called out desperately, but Yu Yiseol ran full tilt toward the rear, leaving the front to Yoon Jong and Jo Gul, hoping Chung Myung would not fall before she arrived.

Her throat burned with anxiety.

Wherever she stepped, the ground was stained red with the blood she left behind.

Chapter 1331: I Will Make Sure That Name Is Engraved (1)

“Oh, they’re coming!”

“D-damn it! Fire! Shoot and kill them!”

The gunner who had been aiming the cannon shouted with a pale face.

Of course, even as he shouted, he knew his words made no sense. A cannon is not an anti-personnel weapon. It is devastating against clustered targets, but a cannon cannot hit a single person precisely.

Moreover, the one storming in was no ordinary person; if he was a Mount Hua warrior who had sliced down their protectors like demons, a mere cannon surely wouldn’t work.

“I said fire, now!”

Kraaaang!

The cannon belched fire at Jo Gul, who was charging with gritted teeth. A black shell flew out at terrifying speed.

But Jo Gul didn’t even blink and kept running. The shell skimmed dangerously over his head and struck the large old tree behind him.

Kuuuuuuuuuung!

A tree thicker than a human torso snapped with a crack. It was a sight that showed the cannon’s true power.

But no matter how powerful an attack, it is meaningless if it doesn’t hit.

“A-again! Quickly!”

Hands hurriedly reloaded the cannon like lightning.

But uselessly so; the moment the fuse was relit, Jo Gul had already reached them.

“Th-this...!”

Kwang!

Stepping into his stance, Jo Gul lashed his sword with force.

“You bastards!”

Paaaang!

With a sound like tearing air, a red line was sharply drawn through the void. Those attached to the cannon were split in two and collapsed instantly.

“S-someone save me...”

Puk!

Jo Gul drove his sword into the chest of someone the blade had barely reached, and before he could even feel the heartbeat at the tip, he violently withdrew it.

Sshhh!

Crimson blood sprayed and splattered onto the cannon.

“Huhk! Huhk! Huhk!”

Jo Gul panted harshly and looked around. There was no one alive in sight. Only blood-soaked corpses cooling remained.

Grit.

Biting his lip, he stamped on the still-smoldering cannon. The muzzle jerked up toward the sky.

Kraaaang!

The shell shot up high into the sky.

Kraaaang!

Seeing the shell soaring upward, Baek Cheon instantly realized the situation and shouted loudly.

“Everyone move! Now!”

The situation was dire. More than half the elders had fallen to poisoning and injury, and Southern Island’s disciples were numerous wounded.

Only those too injured to run properly and those whose stamina was utterly spent were barely holding onto their swords.

But even in that terrible state, everyone responded to Baek Cheon’s words. The voice they’d heard constantly from Southern Island’s shore until now had now held absolute power over Southern Island’s disciples.

“This way!”

Baek Cheon shouted and took the lead in running.

For the exhausted, it’s easier to recognize an action and follow than to understand words.

“Follow the Acting Sect Leader!”

“Move, didn’t you hear! Do you want to die here?”

When Namgung Do-Wei and Hae Yeon barked sharply, Geum Yang-Baek shouted loudly.

“What are you doing! Immediately follow the Acting Sect Leader! Elders, carry the wounded disciples on your backs! Now!”

Even though it was remarkable they were still standing, Southern Island’s elders did not forget their duty. The elders who somehow hoisted the immobile wounded followed behind Baek Cheon, led by Geum Yang-Baek.

The Southern Island disciples who could still move reflexively followed and drove their feet into the ground at full speed.

“Damn it! What are you doing!”

Meanwhile, Im So-Byeong raged and grabbed Tang Soso from behind. She was clinging to a dying Southern Island disciple.

“Let me go! I just need to finish this bandage!”

“He’s already dead! He’s already!!”

“Not yet... he’s not dead yet!”

“Snap out of it! If you die, the rest will all die too!”

Tang Soso’s eyes wavered at that. Just seeing them showed how emotionally pushed to the limit she was.

But Im So-Byeong paid no heed to her feelings and forcibly dragged her along.

As everyone ran out, Gwak Hwan-So ran over to those who still couldn’t move.

“What are you doing! Follow immediately!”

“Great sahyung!”

Those who didn’t know what to do looked at Gwak Hwan-So with despair filling their eyes.

“T-the sahyungs’ wounds...”

Gwak Hwan-So’s eyes dropped. A priest lay moaning with a blood-stained bandage wrapped around his heart.

Understanding the situation, Gwak Hwan-So involuntarily clenched his fist.

The other wounded could be moved somehow. They could die together if need be.

But not these ones. They couldn’t be carried. Their wounds were too grave; moving them recklessly would make the wounds burst open, hastening their deaths instead.

Tang Soso had done emergency treatment to keep them alive, but those with no strength left for the forced march remained.

Gwak Hwan-So gritted his teeth. The tendons in his jaw stood out.

“Go!”

“Yes?”

“I said go, now!”

“G-go where? Sahyung!”

Someone who grasped what Gwak Hwan-So meant shouted desperately.

“They’re still alive, still! What kind of nonsense is that? Are you saying we should leave the sahyungs here like this? That’s...!”

“Shut up and go, didn’t I say!”

“Great sahyung!”

When the sajae screamed, Gwak Hwan-So grabbed him by the collar and yanked him roughly. His eyes, bloodshot, glared fiercely at the priest whose eyes were wet with tears.

“Then what? Will you die here together?”

No answer came. He shoved the sajae away as if throwing him off. There was no time for scuffling.

If Chung Myung, who was holding the rear, were pushed back and the gorge reopened, the Ten Thousand People Clan’s hordes would pour out from there like a torrent.

These exhausted people wouldn’t even be able to resist. They’d be cut down without properly swinging their swords.

They had barely escaped a hellish place; they wouldn’t accept such a miserable death!

“Go, now! If you don’t want to die by my hand!”

Whether cowed by Gwak Hwan-So’s ferocity or realizing it was inevitable, the prostrate disciple let out a wailing cry and ran forward.

Those who hesitated until the end also eventually followed him.

Gwak Hwan-So, who had been holding back his tears with his head tilted up, lowered his gaze again.

Even in that brief moment, Gwak Hwan-So was terrified.

They were injured but conscious, so they must have heard everything he said. Their eyes full of resentment, their words like sharp daggers; it was terrifying.

But what Gwak Hwan-So saw was far different from what he’d expected.

“S-sahyung...”

Some pale-faced disciples who had collapsed pointed to themselves with trembling hands.

Gwak Hwan-So was speechless. The moment he understood what it meant, the heavy weight on his shoulders finally collapsed horribly.

“P-please...”

“Ugh...”

He wanted to block his ears. He wanted to bite a blade and fall over. He wanted to run away anywhere.

“S-sahyung...”

“Aaaaaaaaah!”

Gwak Hwan-So let out a howl and swung his sword like lightning. Blood welled from his split lips.

His beloved blade, clearly engraved with the wave pattern symbolizing Southern Island Sect, sliced through the sahyung brothers’ throats in an instant.

Poooot!

Blood spurted from the cut throats, and on the faces of the Southern Island disciples whose eyes were dimming, faint smiles bloomed.

“Th-thank...”

“S-survive...”

Gwak Hwan-So didn’t stay to hear the rest and bolted. No, it’s more accurate to say he couldn’t bear to listen and ran.

Farther, farther away.

Tears no longer came.

He knew there was no choice. They could no longer fight, yet they couldn’t simply die easily.

But if he left them like this, they’d be captured by the enraged Ten Thousand People Clan and suffer every humiliation and die painfully.

In that case, it was better to cut their lives short with his own hands.

So this was a way of helping them. Gwak Hwan-So knew that.

“Aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa!”

But knowing doesn’t erase the heart-wrenching pain. Knowing doesn’t change the fact he had cut the throats of brothers he’d spent his life with.

‘Survive.’

Thoughts of an honorable death no longer remained even slightly in his mind.

He must survive.

He must survive and avenge this. For the number who died here... no, he must shed a hundred, a thousand times their blood to repay their enemies.

‘I will survive by any means! I must!’

As he drove his feet into the ground with all his strength, a murderous poison flared in his eyes.

“Sasuk!”

“Ahead!”

“We haven’t located all the archers! There could be more ambushes ahead!”

Yoon Jong answered as if shouting and quickly joined Baek Cheon at his side.

A moment later, Jo Gul also ran up at full speed from ahead.

“All the cannons are dealt with!”

“Take the lead! There may be more ambushes!”

“Sago! Sago is heading to the rear! She’s been injured!”

Baek Cheon involuntarily turned to the direction Jo Gul had mentioned.

They had already entered a forest path, and in the distance through the undergrowth a deep gorge lay like it had been split by a god’s sword.

You didn’t need more explanation to guess what had happened.

“Chung Myung is probably at his limit too! And the injured Sago can’t manage alone! We have to help!”

For a moment, conflict clouded Baek Cheon’s face.

He must go.

Just hearing Jo Gul made the situation clear. And right now, the only one with the capacity to go back was Baek Cheon.

But if he pulled himself away and the ones left behind ran into the ambush ahead, could they break through on their own?

“...Jo Gul.”

“Yes, sasuk!”

“Take the front. Lead them away like this.”

“Then will the sasuk go?”

“...”

“Sasuk?”

Baek Cheon was silent. For a moment Jo Gul’s eyes trembled.

“Sasuk! S-Sago’s injury is serious! This isn’t the usual behaviour of Sago!”

“I told you to take the front, lead them!”

Baek Cheon’s sudden angry shout made Jo Gul flinch.

But only briefly; Jo Gul glared at Baek Cheon with bloodshot eyes, his face as if he might lunge and throw a punch any second.

“So you’re leaving them behind?”

“They’re not men who can’t take care of their own bodies! They’ll definitely come back!”

“You said you’d run away, even if it meant abandoning everyone else in danger!”

Baek Cheon fell silent as if at a loss for words. The neat corner of his eye trembled.

“You told me! You clearly told me that, sasuk!”

“...”

“Chung Myung will die! Sago will die! Don’t you understand the situation? Has your head gone mad!”

“Shut your mouth!”

The shout came not from Baek Cheon but from Yoon Jong. Yoon Jong glared at Jo Gul with a face twisted like a demon.

“Sahyung. But...”

“Didn’t I tell you to shut up!”

When Yoon Jong shouted again, Jo Gul’s hand gripping his sword trembled faintly.

Jo Gul’s gaze desperately flicked to the Southern Island faction following them. But his eyes were no longer the same as before.

“Who the hell do these people think they are...”

“Jo Gul!”

“Damn Southern Island, why do we have to go this far for them! You’re all crazy! You’re out of your minds, you bastards!”

“You damned bastard!”

As Yoon Jong grabbed for his collar, Jo Gul didn’t hold back and struck his hand away. Leaving a startled Yoon Jong behind, he ran toward the gorge.

“Stop him! Jo Gul, you bastard!”

“Let him go.”

Yoon Jong turned at Baek Cheon’s words. Baek Cheon bit his lips until they bled and cast a terrifying frenzy with his eyes.

“...You take the lead and open the way. Arrive in the Norther Lands alive. No matter what happens.”

“...Yes, sasuk.”

It sounded as if something somewhere was breaking.

Maybe it was a sign of catastrophe.

Baek Cheon’s hand, hidden inside his sleeve where others couldn’t see, trembled pitiably.

Chapter 1332: I Will Make Sure That Name Is Engraved (2)

Seeing the scene spread out before him, Gwi Yang’s face went deathly pale.

He was certainly a man accustomed to blood. Since the day he first took a sword in hand, the days he had seen blood outnumbered those he hadn’t.

Even he felt strange at the sight now before him.

The white cliffs rising on both sides of the gorge were stained red as if purposely painted, and the owners of that blood were none other than the members he had painstakingly raised.

Looking at the gruesome corpses left behind, Gwi Yang involuntarily swallowed hard.

Of the hundred or so Blood Sword Squad members, more than half had vanished here as wandering spirits. In fact, among those who survived, nearly half were barely clinging to life—merely waiting for the death that approached.

There is a simple word for this situation.

Destruction.

A unit of the Ten Thousand People Clan, the Blood Sword Troops, had collapsed here. Only about thirty remained. It was almost laughable to still call it a unit.

He didn’t have warm feelings of cherishing those he had raised, but looking at his subordinates’ mangled bodies stirred an indescribable feeling.

He stared absentmindedly at the figure blocking the gorge’s exit, preventing them from escaping to the wide plains.

This was the one who, alone, held back not only the Blood Sword Squad but the Ten Thousand People Clan’s thousand-strong force.

It was Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.

Perhaps, this scene would one day become legend, even a myth retold in the Jianghu for a long time.

When later generations discussed Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, this might be the first anecdote they brought up.

Even if Mount Hua’s Sword Saint buried his bones here and turned to a wisp of spirit, his heroic stature would not fade.

No, he might even gain a fame greater than if he returned alive.

Sometimes a hero is completed by his death.

Gwi Yang could not deny it. Though an enemy, Chung Myung’s deeds and path were undeniably great.

He had rushed all the way here to rescue the Southern Island Sect trapped behind enemy lines, and even stood alone against the Ten Thousand People Clan’s force which was pursuing Southern Island Sect.

That plain, unembellished fact alone forced acknowledgment of heroic conduct. Perhaps Gwi Yang was witnessing the birth of a legend to be passed down in the Jianghu.

But in the actual appearance of the one before him now, it was hard to find heroic majesty or the bearing of a mythic protagonist.

The once-highly tied hair was disheveled; the youthful face stained dark crimson with his own blood and that of the Four Sects Alliance.

His left thigh was split open to the bone, and a clear mark where a sword had pierced just left of his sternum remained.

A temporarily bound flank had split open again, gushing dark blood, and a long cut ran from his left neck to his armpit.

The wound on his right leg from when he entered here, the sword scar crossing his back almost invisible to the eye, and countless other small injuries were too many to count.

How could anyone find heroic stature in the figure barely supporting himself on the sword he had thrust into the ground, looking as if his breath could leave him at any moment?

Did that unbearably pitiful sight possess the grandeur to adorn a myth?

That’s not a hero.

That’s just a human.

A human flailing, raging, and screaming.

There was no hero here who split mountains with one stroke and made the heavens tremble with his roar. Only a man paying a brutal price for what he had done existed.

Drip.

Blood trickled and fell drop by drop from Chung Myung’s chin. From his slightly parted lips, a faint breath escaped as if it might break at any moment.

To anyone watching, he had already passed his limit.

He looked like a wounded beast, incapable of resistance. Even a common Ten Thousand People Clan warrior, not a Blood Sword Squad member, could likely end his life now without much trouble.

But the Blood Sword Troops members, who took more joy than anything in ending their prey, dared not approach the wounded beast now.

Yet Gwi Yang did not want to blame them. In fact, he couldn’t blame them.

It was at that moment.

Screech.

When Chung Myung clenched the sword hard, a sound of friction leaked out. Gripping the sword planted in the ground, he slowly straightened his bent body, moving like an old cart creaking.

Gwi Yang could not speak; he simply watched the scene.

Blood continued to flow from Chung Myung as he finally straightened. His lips, visible between matted hair, slowly opened.

He was saying something, but it was hard to hear.

It seemed the voice would not come easily even with effort. Chung Myung finally pressed his lips shut. When they met and separated, the parched lips split and bled.

“...on...”

The violet lips, split, were soaked red with blood. Moments later, a hoarse, cracked voice leaked out as if it might break at any second.

“Keep... going?”

The corner of his mouth barely tilted into a crooked smile.

Seeing that smile, Gwi Yang could not smile back. He clenched his fist without realizing it.

At that moment, as if those were not mere bluster, Chung Myung’s sword began to move.

Silence.

The sword planted in the ground was pulled free.

With trembling hands Chung Myung somehow lifted the sword; he wavered briefly but ultimately gripped it with both hands.

Gwi Yang’s eyes shook.

No one would fail to understand what it meant for a swordsman not even to bear the weight of his own sword; at least here, there wasn’t a single person who wouldn’t understand.

Yet he intended to fight. Now Gwi Yang knew as well: this was neither bravado nor a tantrum.

If he could lift the sword, he’d fight. If his life still clung on, he’d resist.

That mode, incomprehensible to some, was perfectly natural to him.

A sharp pain.

Gwi Yang gripped his lower abdomen with his hands as pain surged. The place where Mount Hua’s Sword Saint had stabbed and left a grotesque hole throbbed anew. Other wounds likewise; Gwi Yang had been grievously cut by Chung Myung’s sword.

But Gwi Yang, choosing not to retreat, gripped his sword tighter and stepped forward.

He would cut off Mount Hua’s Sword Saint’s lifeline.

He could not yield this to anyone. It was his right and the recompense for what he had lost.

“...Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

Gwi Yang spoke slowly.

“You... you were truly remarkable.”

There was not a hint of sarcasm—only pure admiration.

Even if their paths diverged and aims differed, Gwi Yang was still a swordsman who had taken up the blade. He could not help but show reverence.

“But... you have far too many needless things.”

If that Mount Hua’s Sword Saint had been alone—able to retreat when he wished and fight when he wished—

Could the Blood Sword Troops have captured Mount Hua’s Sword Saint then?

No. If Mount Hua’s Sword Saint had been alone, the Blood Sword Squad would have been thoroughly hunted by him.

They would have cried out in desperation and died miserably, unable to bridge the crushing gap.

This meant that what drove Mount Hua’s Sword Saint to death were the things he sought to protect—trifles that, even combined, didn’t match his worth pushed him toward death.

“It’s a pity. Perhaps... you too could have become an Alliance lord.”

Chung Myung did not answer.

Perhaps he could not answer; his body was like ashes after burning once.

Only a still-burning spirit supported him.

Gwi Yang stepped forward slightly and signaled with his chin. Two Blood Sword Troops members, faces tense, took positions behind him.

He knew Mount Hua’s Sword Saint had not a finger’s worth of strength left. But he’d seen too much to let that alone breed complacency.

Vrrrrrr.

Gwi Yang poured all his remaining inner strength into his sword.

“At least I’ll kill you without pain.”

Fshhhhh!

At that moment, a sharp cracking sound came from behind Chung Myung. Gwi Yang narrowed his eyes and scanned ahead.

A female swordswoman of Mount Hua who had exited the gorge was desperately running back toward it. Her pleading was so clear it could be felt even at this distance.

But Gwi Yang had no intention of yielding to that plea.

He had painfully learned that not even a small unpredictable factor could be allowed.

“Strike!”

Kwang!

Gwi Yang and the Blood Sword Troops members surged toward Chung Myung at lightning speed. Yu Yiseol squeezed out all her remaining internal qi and stomped the ground, but the enemies reached Chung Myung much faster.

“Ah, no...”

Despair filled her eyes. Following that came hope.

Or perhaps a vague expectation—a baseless certainty that Chung Myung would not be taken like this.

As if not to betray that hope, Chung Myung swung his sword.

But it was neither fierce nor explosive as usual. For Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, it was shockingly weak—the sword faltered in the air and collided with Gwi Yang’s attack.

Kaang!

With a short, high metallic clang, the Dark Fragrant Plum Blossom Sword was pushed back and flicked away from Gwi Yang’s blade.

Then Gwi Yang’s sword thrust precisely toward Chung Myung’s heart.

“Ah...”

All his senses dulled as if drifting in cloudy water.

Yu Yiseol instinctively reached out her hand.

But that hand touched neither Chung Myung nor the thin sword flying toward him; it only clawed at empty air.

In a helpless world where nothing could be done, all she could see was Chung Myung’s unusually bent back and Gwi Yang charging at him.

Chung Myung’s left shoulder flinched at that moment. His slack left hand thrust forward.

Yu Yiseol knew: that hand contained the squeezed-out power of the bamboo-leaf tree.

That even in this desperate moment Chung Myung had not given up.

Puuuu!

But then something hot splattered on Yu Yiseol’s face.

Her vision turned red for a moment. In it she saw something that should never have been on Chung Myung’s weakened back.

It was the thin sword that Gwi Yang had thrust in.

It looked like the tip of the thin sword jutted out from behind Chung Myung’s back.

The smell of blood stung her nostrils; she felt sticky fluid running on her skin and a hot warmth.

With all that combined, Yu Yiseol fully realized the reality.

A tearing scream burst out.

“Chung Myung!!!”

Chapter 1333: I Will Make Sure That Name Is Engraved (3)

For a moment, the world felt crushed and squashed.

Even so, the only thing painfully clear was the narrow, sharp metal protruding from the back of someone she knew well.

Dark red blood slowly ran down the blade jutting out of the back.

Blood slowly flowed along the sword that had pierced the back, staining the blade red; the blood that reached its tip stretched long and fell in drops downward.

The heat of blood splattered on her face, every scene she saw, and above all Chung Myung’s helplessly slumped shoulders were unbearably vivid.

Yet Yu Yiseol felt all of it strangely vague at the same time.

The sensations were undeniably real, but none of it entered the realm of understanding as it should.

It was as if she were looking but could not see, listening but could not hear.

She stopped in her tracks.

Because it was already too late?

No. The reason was that the tip of the sword protruding from Chung Myung’s back emitted a blue sword qi the moment she tried to rush in to save him. It threatened that any closer approach would split Chung Myung in two.

So she had no choice but to stop.

Even as she watched the Blood Sword Troops members’ blades repeatedly strike Chung Myung’s left shoulder and right flank.

Creak! Creak!

In that instant, Yu Yiseol flinched as if she herself had been stabbed.

Her wide-open eyes held not anger or pity, but terror.

Because she had to accept what was real.

If he had any strength left, Chung Myung could have deflected those attacks—but in his current state he could not.

Perhaps the limits of Chung Myung of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint—limits she thought she would never see—were revealed now, in the very way she least wanted to consider.

No sound escaped her parted lips. Rather, it was impossible for one to. If she even moved a finger or made the slightest noise, the sword lodged in that small body might twist and slit the heart.

She could only stare in silence.

Only a few steps. Those few steps felt as distant as an eternal wall.

The unmistakable sensation of a hand piercing.

The satisfying fulfillment one feels only when piercing through a body and ending a life.

Gwi Yang’s eyes, filling with elation, stared at his sword lodged in the center of Chung Myung’s chest; more than half the blade was buried in the chest.

Of course he knew.

A wound punctured by a sword does not bleed as profusely as one might think. If the prey still has qi and the muscles contract, only a few drops of blood trickle out.

But blood was quickly pouring from Chung Myung’s wound enough to soak his chest. That meant he had no strength left to clench around the embedded sword.

Chung Myung’s hand, pierced along with the sword as he tried to stop it, slowly curled and touched the blade. That was all—there was no impact on Gwi Yang’s sword.

Instead, a heavy weight began to press on the sword Gwi Yang held—the weight that comes when the opponent can no longer support their body and surrenders themselves to the blade that will end their life.

It was a weight of death all too familiar to Gwi Yang.

“...Caught you.”

It was a result he could scarcely believe even after achieving it. Yet the outcome was clearly in his hands: Chung Myung of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint. He had finally caught the tremendous monster whose reputation made even the world’s renown seem paltry.

This was an achievement that the word “great feat” could not fully express.

When he finally captured a difficult prey after all manner of hardships, he would feel a thrill that words could not describe.

But what dominated him now was completely different from that thrill.

An incomprehensible, complex emotion surged—utter rapture mingled with an unbearable sorrow took hold of him.

Gwi Yang slowly raised his head.

He wanted to see.

He wondered what expression the monster that had tormented him so terribly and proven a divine might he couldn’t even imagine would wear as it met death.

Would it be contorted with pain? Calm? Or... wearing a hollow smile?

Sadly, he could not see Chung Myung’s expression. All he saw was the crown of his head, head bowed as if all strength had left him.

At that moment, a faint sense of emptiness passed through Gwi Yang’s eyes.

He must have even believed that.

He believed this person would never lower his head even in death—would die proudly, looking up at the sky, even if dozens of swords were stuck in his body.

The faint emptiness passed, and a sense of reality slowly returned.

Gwi Yang, before he knew it, opened his mouth.

“A human... a human, indeed.”

There was no answer.

Of course—there was no strength left to reply. In truth, it was uncertain whether he was even conscious.

Gwi Yang slowly tightened his grip on the sword.

By uttering a single word to his prey, he had effectively broken the rule he had set for himself.

Normally, the thing that ruins a hunt is the complacency that comes at the end. One must never relax until the heart is completely cut and the neck severed.

He was at the moment about to drag the sword he finally gripped and split Chung Myung’s heart.

“...Wuh...”

A faint sound escaped Chung Myung’s lips—perhaps a small death rattle at the end of life.

But Gwi Yang suddenly stopped the sword, for a reason even he found hard to explain.

“...What did you say?”

There was no need to ask again—he could simply ignore it and cut. Yet strangely, he couldn’t.

Chung Myung’s head slowly rose.

Like an old, rusty wheel creaking and clattering as it turns, Chung Myung forced his head up and quietly looked at Gwi Yang.

The eyes revealed between hair matted with blood looked hollow, devoid of any light.

“...Can’t... move...”

Through lips whose state was ambiguous, indistinct words trickled out here and there.

“No... I shouldn’t...”

At that moment Gwi Yang saw it.

Chung Myung’s lips, dark with dried blood, curled into a chilling smile. The moment he saw that smile, Gwi Yang’s blood ran cold as if into an ice cave.

“...My legs.”

Gwi Yang desperately threw his internal qi into driving the sword down.

But despite using all his remaining strength, the sword lodged in Chung Myung’s chest did not budge. It was as if it were stuck between massive rocks—no matter how hard he pressed down, it did not move.

“What?”

A violet qi began to flow from the wound and envelop Gwi Yang’s sword.

At that moment, Chung Myung pushed his hand, pierced by the sword, toward Gwi Yang.

Grrrreek!

With the sound of bone scraping metal, the hand slid along to the sword ring. Grasping the ring, Chung Myung suddenly pulled Gwi Yang toward him. Gwi Yang, whose weight was shifted forward from pressing the sword with full force, could not resist.

Then Gwi Yang saw it: sword qi gathering on Chung Myung’s blade, which had been hanging down.

It was the sword aura of Mount Hua—red like blood and keen as the dawn.

“N-no...”

Bwaaaah!

A blast sound, familiar yet foreign, reverberated through the gorge.

Gwi Yang stared dumbfounded at his sword buried in Chung Myung’s chest. The sword, which had been forcibly pulled, was almost fully embedded in Chung Myung’s chest.

“You...”

Gwi Yang’s mouth hung open. The wordless gape that could not form a phrase finally opened again, and a voice laced with complicated emotion leaked out.

“You... did this on purpose...”

For a moment Gwi Yang’s stunned gaze met Chung Myung’s eyes, which were going slack.

Chung Myung’s voice, slightly clearer than before, came through.

“If I can’t... make it...”

Even with a voice tinged with metal, Chung Myung smiled, revealing teeth stained with blood.

“...You were supposed... to come to me. Right?”

Gwi Yang’s eyes shook.

A moment of silence passed. Then red gore streamed from Gwi Yang’s lips.

Only belatedly did Gwi Yang realize why he had hesitated for an instant at the last moment.

A beast feels instinctive reluctance before taking bait—even if that bait is the life of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, something it cannot ignore.

From the start, the one being hunted was not Chung Myung but Gwi Yang himself.

“You...”

Gwi Yang, who choked up blood from his mouth, slowly collapsed.

The sword he had pulled with his last will came free from Chung Myung’s body. Blood poured from the wound and covered the face of the kneeling Gwi Yang.

“You really...”

Thud.

Gwi Yang’s body, unable to finish the words he uttered, fell to the ground along with the other Blood Sword Troops members who had rushed in.

At the same time, Chung Myung’s body also staggered forward.

“Chung Myung!”

Yu Yiseol hurried forward, catching the falling Chung Myung from behind and supporting him.

“Sajil! Chung Myung!”

“Huh...”

Feeling Yu Yiseol’s firm grip, Chung Myung bared his teeth weakly and smiled.

“You’re... late...”

“You...”

“Ha... anyway...”

As if all the barely intact nerves had been severed, Chung Myung’s head sagged down.

Yu Yiseol’s hand went instinctively to Chung Myung’s neck. She felt a faint pulse at her fingertips. Confirming that his pulse still beat, she tore her long cloak and hurriedly bound Chung Myung’s wound.

“You are alive.”

Though the wound was grave enough that death could come at any moment, he was still alive.

He’s still alive.

That was enough. What more could one need as a reason to risk one’s life?

Looking once at Chung Myung’s unconscious face, she hoisted him up, removed the remaining torn cloak, and tightly bound him to herself.

She felt his heartbeat faint and slow through his back. His body temperature had cooled to the point her hands trembled.

She was afraid—that this heart would slowly stop, that the last faint warmth remaining would go out.

Yu Yiseol lifted her head.

She saw the Ten Thousand People Clan members approaching again, taking in the situation. Their expressions were grim; they would never give up easily.

They surely knew how precious a chance it was to take the life of Chung Myung of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.

Especially Ho Ga-Myung, eyes gleaming from the rear, would try by any means to drive a sword into Chung Myung’s heart to put an end to all of this.

Yu Yiseol reached out and grabbed Chung Myung’s Dark Fragrant Plum Blossom Sword lying on the ground. The unfamiliar hilt still held warmth.

That warmth steadied Yu Yiseol, making her calm and composed. Her trembling eyes grew cold and focused.

With the Ten Thousand People Clan swords advancing and gathering momentum, Yu Yiseol glared at them and slammed her foot hard into the ground without hesitation.

“I will protect him.”

Thud!

The moment she jerked her body back, the Ten Thousand People Clan swords descended on Yu Yiseol like a torrent bursting from a broken dam.

“By all means!”

She instantly severed the throat of the leading Ten Thousand People Clan swordsman charging at her. Resolve blazed fiercely on that blade.

Chapter 1334: I Will Make Sure That Name Is Engraved (4)

“Don’t let her get away!”

“We must kill her!”

Members of the Ten Thousand People Clan chased Yu Yiseol desperately.

The screams that tore their throats and the poison in their eyes were no different from the battle inside the gorge.

But the expressions on their faces and their momentum were clearly different.

More desperate. More frantic. The light on their faces could only be described as desperation and recklessness.

Fwaaah!

The Ten Thousand People Clan members struck the ground with force, determined to chase down Chung Myung, catch up, and drive a sword through his throat, to end his breath completely.

“Get her!”

This would be their last chance. They must not let it slip.

By nature, the Ten Thousand People Clan does not fear enemies. They do not fear war or death. That is what it means to be the Ten Thousand People Clan, a pride each member holds in their heart.

But that pride was shattered to pieces in the face of what they had seen Chung Myung do in the gorge.

So at this point, everyone could only know.

That if they let him go here, they would have to fight him again someday, maybe far in the future, or perhaps sooner than they expected.

They had watched Chung Myung annihilate the Blood Sword Squad with their own eyes. The nightmare of facing an uninjured Chung Myung again was something they didn’t even want to imagine.

They must kill him. They must.

There was no need to discuss or debate. The moment Chung Myung was hoisted onto Yu Yiseol’s back, everyone’s will here naturally united.

By any means, at any cost, they had to kill him!

“Aaaah!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan members surged at Yu Yiseol with overwhelming force. It looked less like people chasing a person and more like a flood swollen by a storm sweeping over the valley.

Thak!

Yu Yiseol drove her foot into the ground desperately.

There was no need to argue that her divine art was Mount Hua’s finest. But her inner strength had been drained by long fighting. And with the unconscious Chung Myung slumped on her back, her steps were inevitably heavier than usual.

“Dieeeee!”

A Ten Thousand People Clan member who had caught up to Yu Yiseol screamed and swung his sword.

At that exact moment, Yu Yiseol realized the battle she was about to endure would be clearly different from anything before.

Blades began to slice through the air with terrifying momentum.

Not toward her head, but toward her back.

Pa-at!

Kaaang!

Her swiftly extended sword struck down the incoming blades.

Grit.

She clenched her teeth.

It was a blade strike she wouldn’t have needed to block if she were alone.

No, she shouldn’t have blocked it.

To forcefully deflect a blade that was off-center from the body’s axis requires more than core strength. The impact on the wrist is greater than one imagines.

But now there was no other choice.

Swaaaak!

The blade coming from the side was the same.

Normally she would avoid such strikes with minimal motion to reduce waste, but now she could not. It wouldn’t end just because her body remained unharmed.

Yu Yiseol twisted and moved as much as possible to dodge the thrusting blades. Her movement, unlike usual, threw off her center and shattered the balance she had always maintained.

Her movements were always supple and graceful.

But this was not innate. It was woven strand by strand through relentless, repeated training, and at its foundation were the two words: balance.

Once that balance collapsed, Yu Yiseol could no longer be fully herself.

“Hah!”

A rare groan burst from her mouth. Her sword then cleaved the air like a ray of light.

When evasion is difficult, there is only one way: strike before you are struck.

“Kraaah!”

Yu Yiseol’s thrust drove without fail into a Ten Thousand People Clan member’s heart.

Though her sword path was different from usual, even that twisted trajectory made her blade powerful.

But the enemies were as desperate as she was, perhaps even more so. Using the man with a pierced heart as a stepping stone, they leapt at Yu Yiseol like earth sliding down in an avalanche.

“Dieeeeeee!”

Screech!

A blade that had fallen toward Chung Myung’s head sliced just barely in front of her shoulder. The blade itself missed, but the blade qi nonetheless cut Yu Yiseol’s skin.

She didn’t even have time to feel pain. Yu Yiseol, flung almost prone, planted her hand on the ground and spun quickly.

Kwahng! Kwahng!

Soon there were explosions where she’d just been; the earth bucked and rose. Had she been a moment slower in moving, her body would by now have been nothing but minced meat.

Flying stones and shards struck her body or left scratches. But Yu Yiseol, unaware of any wounds, was busy swinging her sword with all her strength.

Taaang!

With a whip-like slice through the air, the waists of three Ten Thousand People Clan members pouring down upon her were severed in an instant. Hot blood and bits of entrails spilled out.

Yu Yiseol gripped the ground hard with her hand and hauled her body up in a single motion. Her nails were turned up and broken, but she had no time to notice.

The instant her body was pulled upward, two blades flew straight toward where her legs had been.

Taat!

Yu Yiseol pushed off the ground and turned to stand while looking back. Running forward while looking ahead would have been faster, but she couldn’t do that; there was no way to protect Chung Myung from blades flying at her back that way.

She was fleeing, yet she still had to face the enemy. Blades crowded toward her, and she endured that brutal situation with her whole body.

Yu Yiseol swung her sword like lightning to deflect attacks, but even she found it difficult to block all the blades raining down at once.

Screech!

A blade that grazed her upper arm sliced through muscle in an instant, leaving a wound deep enough to show bone.

Screech!

A blade that brushed her flank also cut the skin, leaving a deep gash.

Kwaaang!

The blade she met head-on didn’t cut her body, but it violently twisted the wrist that held her sword. A blood vessel in her wrist burst, and the bone throbbed as if it would shatter.

She was not the only one who now had freedom of movement. In a narrow gorge, the number who can attack at once is limited. But once outside the gorge, within the reach of blades, attacks can come from anywhere.

It had been impossible from the start to dodge and block all those attacks while carrying someone on her back.

But instead of despairing, Yu Yiseol tightened the sash securing Chung Myung to her back.

‘Protect him.’

This was not done because she could, nor because she wanted to.

Perhaps the one who best understood Chung Myung’s words at Mount Hua was Yu Yiseol.

This was the thing she had to do.

Yu Yiseol had no doubt about that thought. Even if it cost her life, she must ensure Chung Myung survives.

Fwaaah!

Covered in dirt, Yu Yiseol briefly turned ahead and struck the ground with all her strength. With Chung Myung on her back, her body shot forward like an arrow loosed from its bow.

Seizing the opportunity, a blade flew and sliced her calf. Drops of blood scattered into the air.

But the distance between the Ten Thousand People Clan members and Yu Yiseol had widened significantly.

“Pursue them!”

“Don’t bother with the woman! Aim for Mount Hua’s Sword Saint! You must kill him!”

“Don’t let him escape!”

The frantic cries rang out again and again.

They had to kill Mount Hua’s Sword Saint Chung Myung. They would take his life at all costs.

Ho Ga-Myung looked down indifferently at the corpse of Gwi Yang, lying fallen and cooling.

Gwi Yang’s eyes, which had not even been able to close before his breath stopped, still held the terror he felt just before dying.

After giving him a brief glance, Ho Ga-Myung passed by without a word and moved forward.

‘He was a captain.’

A troops and a squad are different. And a captain and a squad leader are even more different.

It is acceptable for a squad leader to die at the enemy’s hands, or for a squad deployed in an operation to be destroyed. Everything requires sacrifice and cost.

But losing a troops, and losing a captain, is another matter.

A dan of the Ten Thousand People Clan is the core of their forces, and if limited to those Ho Ga-Myung can control, that value rises even more.

Such a troops had been annihilated, and Gwi Yang, who led that troops, was dead. It could not be anything less than a major incident.

Yet even this time, Ho Ga-Myung’s face showed not the slightest flicker of agitation.

His gaze was now fixed on Yu Yiseol fleeing ahead, more precisely, on Mount Hua’s Sword Saint on her back. Ho Ga-Myung’s stare at the unconscious Mount Hua’s Sword Saint was chillingly cold.

He had sacrificed Gwi Yang and the Blood Sword Troops to render the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint unconscious.

Others might call it madness if they heard, but Ho Ga-Myung believed this sacrifice had yielded results beyond his expectations.

Of course, Gwi Yang would not have thought so, but from the start Ho Ga-Myung had never once believed someone like Gwi Yang could bring down Chung Myung.

What dragon would be bitten by such a thing?

But fortunately, Gwi Yang had performed beyond his abilities. Or perhaps Chung Myung had been unable to display his full power; that might be true, but the result was the same.

If the dead Gwi Yang could hear, he’d jump out of hell. But Ho Ga-Myung valued Gwi Yang at exactly that level. It was regrettable to lose even one captain who could somewhat be reasoned with, though.

‘The Blood Sword Troops and the like...’

If he could kill Chung Myung, he could offer up any number as sacrifices.

Now all that remained was for him to swallow up those floundering, headless ones as he wished.

The forces led by Mount Hua’s Sword Saint were strong enough to make even Ho Ga-Myung risk his life, but without the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, those men were nothing but rabble.

“Are you ready?”

“We’ve been deployed as you ordered!”

“If even a single thing goes wrong, your life won’t be enough to pay for it.”

The adjutant answered with a bloodless face to the cold voice.

“I will remember.”

A furtive but clear killing intent flashed in Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes.

‘Two moves, no, three moves?’

Now only about three moves. In terms of time, only a moment.

Within that moment, the fate of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint would be decided, exactly according to the steps he’d set.

Ho Ga-Myung lightly curled and opened his fist.

‘Stay calm.’

He could not allow the slightest variable until the end.

This was a strategy in which he had staked everything. From Southern Island Sect to now, he had aimed solely for the life of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, and the fruits of that plan were gradually approaching.

“A headless beast can simply be driven where you want.”

Ho Ga-Myung suddenly wondered what expression Jang Ilso would make when confronted with the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint’s severed head.

‘Perhaps this is my petty rebellion.’

He dismissed the base fantasy that had briefly surfaced in his mind and let out a light sigh. His gaze, however, grew darker and darker.

Chapter 1335: I Will Make Sure That Name Is Engraved (5)

“They’re coming!”

At that shout, Yoon Jong immediately stomped the ground. The Evil Sect ranks flanked them on both sides and swarmed in black masses.

‘Damn it! It never ends!’

They had fought so hard to get out of the gorge, yet instead of thinning, the enemy numbers seemed to have grown.

Of course, those charging now were not the Four Sects Alliance elites but a motley gathered roughly together, ragtag fighters who at best might be second-rate in the martial world.

But even those men in such numbers are a burden. Especially for those exhausted in the gorge, depleted of internal energy and stamina, they were more than a burden; they were a real threat.

“Yoon Jong! Move to the center!”

“Yes!”

Following Baek Cheon’s command, Yoon Jong changed direction and drove into the still-unfinished encirclement of the enemy.

The enemy’s strength was their filthy numbers. But their weakness was also their excessive numbers.

Yoon Jong aimed for the junction where both enemy forces met and trembled his blade. The moment his sword fell from above, the blade-energy that burst forth split the charging men left and right as if parting a sea.

Yoon Jong lunged into the gap forced open and swung his sword again. The sword qi spread in all directions.

Swaaaak!

“Aaaah!”

The enemies fell spraying blood without even getting a chance to act. Not the fastest, not the most intense, but the timely sword strikes were proving decisively effective.

A flicker of astonishment crossed Baek Cheon’s eyes.

‘So steady!’

Even exhausted to the bone and forced to squeeze out the last of his internal qi, there was no wavering in Yoon Jong’s sword.

Perhaps that composure was Yoon Jong’s greatest distinction from Baek Cheon.

But regardless, it didn’t change that Yoon Jong was also pushed to his limits.

“Taaaarp!”

Baek Cheon dove in like a flying tiger into the gap Yoon Jong had made. At the same time his Plum Blossom Sword swept horizontally, instantly bisecting the Evil Sect attackers.

“Ugh, ugh!”

Fear filled the eyes of the Evil Sect men. They were no disciplined warriors of the Ten Thousand People Clan; had it not been for sudden orders, they wouldn’t oppose disciples of Mount Hua. They were only standing their ground out of fear of reprisals.

Baek Cheon had seen and learned, to the point of boredom, how to overwhelm such men with momentum and steer the tide of battle.

Bang!

Baek Cheon stamped his stance and unleashed sword qi in all directions. It looked dazzling, but the energy was hollow, full of empty bursts without significant internal power.

The Justice faction teaches that such hollow displays should not be pursued. Yet sometimes, such tricks can be unexpectedly effective.

“Aaaaaah!”

“P-please spare me!”

Even though the hollow bursts carried no real internal qi, the men who had already been ready to flee screamed and prostrated themselves. Those coming up from behind panicked and backed away rapidly.

Baek Cheon’s eyes flashed.

They now couldn’t bear to charge. But if the moment came when they were forced to meet blades with all these men, even their terrified swings would become threats.

So the best tactic was to break through quickly with minimal collisions.

“Keep running, Yoon Jong!”

“Yes!”

Southern Island Sect’s disciples surged forward along the path Yoon Jong and Baek Cheon had opened.

It’s not easy to move a lake that has stilled. The Evil Sect men who’d been given a path couldn’t throw themselves forward; they hedged, swinging their swords with their backs ready to flee.

“You bastards!”

But Southern Island Sect’s disciples were not men to be felled by such half-hearted attacks.

Swaaaaak!

“Aaaah!”

They simply had the wrong opponents; Southern Island Sect was still a Nine Great Sects force whose skill would be acknowledged anywhere in the martial world.

Moreover, Southern Island Sect’s disciples had gained the missing experience and ferocity on the journey from Southern Island to here. So the ragtag attacks of the Evil Sect could not possibly prevail.

But as always, the fear of the Evil Sect does not come from rare, supreme skill.

“More are coming up ahead!”

“Ugh...”

Baek Cheon’s face twisted for a moment; he saw enemies surging like waves from the front-left.

Judging by their speed, their skill still seemed poor, but their momentum was very threatening. Overwhelming numbers breathe confidence.

Half the battle is momentum. If one hesitated, they’d be swept away in an instant, and then even those who had just been scared stiff would regain courage and charge.

“To the right!”

“Sasuk! That will expose other areas!”

Baek Cheon faltered for an instant. That meant Southern Island Sect, not them, would be fully exposed to attack.

At that moment, a furious voice came from the rear.

“Damn it, don’t stop, keep running!”

“Green Forest King?”

“Don’t stop! If you stop you’ll be encircled! Do you not know what will happen then? I’ll handle the rear somehow, so hurry!”

“Understood! Yoon Jong, to the right!”

“Yes!”

Im So-Byeong’s words were entirely correct. This was open ground. Unlike the gorge, here you cannot hide a lack of numbers.

If they had been in the state before entering the gorge, they might have burst through even if encircled. But now, utterly exhausted, it was impossible. Stamina was drained and carrying the wounded meant mobility was less than half of what it had been.

The moment they were surrounded, there would be no way to break out.

Yet even in this desperate situation, Baek Cheon and Yoon Jong were fully exerting their abilities.

“Sasuk! They’re coming from the left too!”

“Don’t worry about it! Move forward!”

“B-But the gap to the rear is too wide now. We could be split in two!”

“The Green Forest King will hold them!”

“But now...!”

“Go! I’m watching it!”

“Yes!”

Yoon Jong forced the uneasy thought popping into his head away.

Yoon Jong might be better than Baek Cheon at assessing the overall situation and responding calmly; Baek Cheon sometimes tended to be swayed by emotion.

But Baek Cheon’s decisive ability, something Yoon Jong wouldn’t dare imitate, was his decision-making. Yoon Jong trusted that decisiveness utterly, believing Baek Cheon would make the right call.

However, not everyone trusted Baek Cheon as Yoon Jong did.

“Acting Sect Leader!”

Namgung Do-Wei, rushing up from the rear, shouted at Baek Cheon.

“The Taoists behind haven’t arrived yet! We must slow down!”

Baek Cheon bit his lip. Then he spoke with force.

“If you have the strength, help Yoon Jong break the front, Young lord.”

Instead of an answer, he received an order, and Namgung Do-Wei’s face twisted.

“Do you not understand, Acting Sect Leader? The Taoist Chung Myung has fallen behind!”

“I know.”

“You know? Is that what you say as knowing it? What are you thinking? Are you going to use the Taoist Chung Myung as bait and run? Are you sane?”

In an instant Baek Cheon lunged like lightning and grabbed Namgung Do-Wei by the collar roughly.

Baek Cheon’s eyes, full of murderous intent, glared fiercely at Namgung Do-Wei, who stared back in astonishment.

Baek Cheon growled as he spoke.

“If we run back and rescue him, what next?”

“...Acting Sect Leader?”

“What then? What will you do after that?”

Veins bulged on Baek Cheon’s clenched jaw.

“Are you suggesting we all die together nicely? That we’ll be satisfied having done our best to save him and then all drop dead?”

When Namgung Do-Wei couldn’t answer, Baek Cheon let go of his collar as if shoving him away.

It was an exceedingly rude act, but instead of raging, Namgung Do-Wei glared back at Baek Cheon with cold eyes.

“So you mean only we should live? You don’t want to die together?”

Baek Cheon turned his back, ignoring him as if he would no longer engage.

“...If you have breath for words, swing your sword.”

“Acting Sect Leader!”

At Namgung Do-Wei’s shout, Baek Cheon snapped around. The pent-up fury he’d been suppressing burst out.

“Don’t spout off like you know! I’m the one who wants to run back right now even more than you, you bastard!”

Namgung Do-Wei flinched at Baek Cheon’s shout.

It wasn’t the sheer force but the emotion in that voice that struck Namgung Do-Wei hard.

Baek Cheon’s hand trembled as if he could hardly contain the sudden flood of emotion.

“...Help the front, Young lord.”

Baek Cheon said with effort and cast his gaze somewhere. Blood that seeped from his tightly clenched lips dripped down in a thin line.

They’re there.

Chung Myung, Samae, and even Jo Gul.

He wanted to stomp the ground and run right now. He wanted to turn their direction and go rescue them this instant.

But he could not.

Because the one who would despise that choice most would be Chung Myung. That bastard would never allow others to be annihilated because of him, even if it cost him his life.

‘There’s no honorable way to die together. Right?’

Baek Cheon clenched his fist tight, asking that unreachable question inwardly.

Even with a blade at his throat, there’s no giving up. The only thing to think about now is one thing: a path for everyone here to survive.

That was what he’d learned from Chung Myung all this time.

And for that, only one thing was needed.

“...They will definitely come!”

Baek Cheon gritted his teeth and shouted. At that cry, Yoon Jong’s shoulder, swinging his sword, trembled faintly. Baek Cheon felt he knew Yoon Jong’s expression without seeing it.

“Because that damn Jo Gul went in my place.”

Baek Cheon said it with conviction, no, with desperate pleading.

“There’s not a single man among those behind who is less than I am. If it’s those people, they’ll come back for sure!”

His eyes burned red with clenched teeth.

“So we can’t fail to open the way and let them die! Understand?”

Fwaaah!

Yoon Jong answered with action, cutting down the charging men in an instant, as if to show he believed those words without needing to say it.

Namgung Do-Wei, who had been staring at Baek Cheon with a complicated expression, finally nodded. Without more words, he gripped his sword and stepped forward.

Baek Cheon closed his eyes for a moment.

‘Damn bastard.’
