He couldn’t go back. Even if everyone here went to save Chung Myung, he could not. That Chung Myung would never allow that choice.

Even if all these choices proved wrong and they met that bastard in hell, he’d gladly take the insult of being foolish. But he would never accept the reproach of not being trusted.

Baek Cheon forcibly suppressed the gaze that kept wanting to dart backward.

What he had to do now was not doubt, but simply believe.

Stupidly, blindly.

He saw the Evil Sect masses surging like waves as the opened path narrowed again. Almost as soon as he judged Namgung Do-Wei and Yoon Jong alone could not handle it, Baek Cheon stomped the ground.

Red blade-energy poured toward the Evil Sect like a waterfall.

Kwaaaaang!

In the scattering blade-energy, Baek Cheon’s gaze shone terrifyingly.

‘You must come here, you damn bastard.’

He’d take all the curses then, as many as needed.

Chapter 1336: I Will Make Sure That Name Is Engraved (6)

How far have we come? How long have we been fighting?

Swaaaak!

A blade suddenly and chillingly flew in, cutting the foot that was about to touch the ground.

Yu Yiseol lost her balance in an instant and tumbled headlong to the ground. Her muscles seemed severed; she couldn’t put strength into her leg. In fact, she had no feeling at all.

But Yu Yiseol neither despaired nor gave up.

Bang!

She shoved the ground hard with her hands and used the recoil to lurch forward a bit more. A violent shock hit her wrist, as if it might break, but she paid it no mind.

‘More!’

One more step!

“Die, you bitch!”

Members of the Ten Thousand People Clan, their eyes full of poisonous intent, tore their mouths wide and screamed as they swung blades at her. Yu Yiseol immediately swung her sword with full force to meet them.

Kaaang!

Using the force of sword and blade colliding, she rolled back and drove her hand into the ground again.

‘How far have I come?’

No, she mustn’t think.

The moment she thinks, she might despair at how far is still left.

And it doesn’t matter how far she’s come. No matter where this is, what she must do won’t change.

Swaaaak!

A crimson blade flew in fiercely. Realizing she couldn’t block them all, Yu Yiseol’s left hand instinctively covered Chung Myung’s head.

Kaang! Shhk!

The clash of her sword and the blades rang out, and at the same time the sound of flesh being cut by the blade was heard.

Her flank was cut and blood poured, but Yu Yiseol retreated immediately without faltering or staggering.

More. One more step.

“That wretched bitch!”

Even the Ten Thousand People Clan members looked at Yu Yiseol as if they were sickened.

Her face had long since swollen badly from the repeated blows of flying blades.

She couldn’t even open her eyes properly, making it hard to see.

But even in that dire state, she was steadily, desperately extricating herself from the pursuit.

‘More...’

The ground was stained red with the blood she shed.

She no longer felt pain. All that remained was the will to escape.

Swaaaak!

As a blade flew in, Yu Yiseol’s sword moved instinctively.

Kaang!

But her sword no longer had the strength to deflect the blade. The moment blade and sword collided, her wrist buckled. The misdirected blade sliced through her thigh.

Shhk!

Her thigh split open, revealing white bone.

But more troubling than the severity of the wound was that she could no longer use that leg.

‘Must... go...’

She could no longer plant her foot hard or nimbly evade attacks. Her right leg would no longer move at her command.

Screee. Tick. Screee.

But even dragging the limp leg, Yu Yiseol kept retreating while facing the Ten Thousand People Clan members.

Because of the heavy bleeding, her thoughts came and went in fragments.

She no longer clearly remembered when the fight had started or why they were fighting.

What moved her was a faint warmth against her back, nothing more.

She must protect him.

She had always said she’d protect, yet she’d only ever been protected. This time, she had to protect this one.

“Do...n’t... worry...”

Yu Yiseol forced herself to lift her sword. But her wrist, apparently dislocated, kept going limp.

“I’m... still... fine.”

It wasn’t even clear whom she spoke to—Chung Myung, or herself.

No, it didn’t matter.

“...Even in that state...”

The words, spoken in a weary voice as if disgusted by her, reached her muffled ears.

Strangely, she laughed.

They would never understand.

Those who live only for themselves do not know.

Because of this one, plum blossoms bloomed on her sword.

Because of this one, Hyun Jong smiled.

Because of this one, she preserved the home she would live in, and her father’s life and death were not entirely in vain.

Isn’t that enough? Does a person need more reason than that to stake their life on another?

“Sago is...”

Yu Yiseol barely parted her lips.

“Protecting... Sajil...”

She was clearly beyond her limits.

She was at the point where even if the Ten Thousand People Clan didn’t swing any more blades, her breath could simply cease. She was only breathing; she might as well be called a corpse.

But the Ten Thousand People Clan members couldn’t bring themselves to finish her off. They were overwhelmed by something other than fear or inertia.

“...Sago will...”

But that lasted only a moment.

Soon a roaring shout erupted from behind the Ten Thousand People Clan members.

“What are you doing, you idiots! Can’t you finish her now!”

For those who had rushed in from deeper in the gorge and only just joined, it was an utterly bewildering sight.

Startled by the rebuke, some bit their lips and then charged at Yu Yiseol again.

They must resist. They must fight.

But her arms and legs were already limp.

“...Sajil...”

A blade flew toward Yu Yiseol’s throat. She barely lifted her immobilized arm to block, but the limp sword couldn’t reach the blade’s trajectory.

The blade grazed over Yu Yiseol’s wrist and neared her throat.

“Protect...”

Kaaaaang!

At that moment, with a thunderous crack, the blade that had grazed Yu Yiseol’s throat was flung off with a spray of red blood. The shock that exploded right beside her neck collapsed Yu Yiseol and Chung Myung, and someone caught and supported them.

Through her blurred vision, Yu Yiseol saw someone looking down at her.

‘...Jo...Gul?’

“Th...”

Jo Gul’s face was horribly contorted.

“How dare these Evil Sect bastards, less than fucking dogs, lay hands on someone...!”

With one hand supporting Yu Yiseol, Jo Gul thrust his sword forward.

The torrent of his swift sword exploded forth, instantly creating huge wind-holes in the bodies of the Ten Thousand People Clan members who had faltered at the sudden arrival of a new enemy.

“Aaaaaah!”

Jo Gul’s fury did not abate. From his sword tip poured crimson Plum Blossom Sword qi.

The rough, almost violent sword qi swept over and shredded those who hesitated and fell back.

“Sago!”

Jo Gul shouted toward the dazed-looking Yu Yiseol.

When Yu Yiseol faintly opened her eyes, she saw Jo Gul’s face streaming with tears.

“Sago, are you all right?!”

“...Sajil...”

“Damn it, think about yourself for once!”

As Jo Gul shouted, exasperated, a faint smile trembled at Yu Yiseol’s mouth.

Jo Gul then grabbed her firmly as she went limp.

A glance showed no part of their bodies unscathed. Both Chung Myung and Sago had fought to this state.

‘It’s my fault!’

If he’d been just a bit faster, the two wouldn’t have been so badly hurt.

If he’d been a bit stronger, he could have felled those blocking the way sooner and reached here earlier.

The thought that each wound on their bodies stemmed from his weakness made his blood boil.

Clenching his teeth, Jo Gul slung the two over his shoulders.

What he had to do now was not vent his seething rage but rescue them.

Swoosh!

He pushed off the ground and shot forward like an arrow.

“Pursue them! Now!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan members, who had hesitated at Jo Gul’s unexpected appearance, gave chase as soon as his back was turned.

“Get out of the way, you sons of bitches!”

The Evil Sect members, who had listlessly been pursuing Haenam after he had already gone past once, went pale at the sudden shout and the presence of Jo Gul behind them.

Jo Gul mercilessly pierced their backs.

Crunch! Crunch!

Those with fist-sized wind holes in their bodies collapsed on the spot.

“W-what is—”

“From the rear! They’re coming from behind!”

The Evil Sect members flailed their weapons in panic, but the hastily delivered blows had no force and couldn’t stop Jo Gul.

“Move aside!”

“Seize him, you fools!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan pursuers shouted roughly at the tangled Evil Sect members.

When the Ten Thousand People Clan came, the Evil Sect members were also terrified and tried to give way, but those packed in tightly wouldn’t yield easily.

Those who couldn’t get out of the way collided with the oncoming Ten Thousand People Clan, who couldn’t restrain their momentum. The Evil Sect members fell, spraying blood as if hit by a speeding cart.

“Th-this!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan pursuers twisted their faces.

The forces they’d mobilized were hindering them at this moment. Ho Ga-Myung likely hadn’t expected this.

“You miserable bastards!”

Growing frantic, the Ten Thousand People Clan men resorted to punching the Evil Sect members. Some truly brutal ones didn’t even hesitate to cut them down.

“Kraaaah!”

The Evil Sect members, who had never imagined allies would swing blades at them, screamed and tumbled together.

Of course the chaos wouldn’t last long, but for Jo Gul it was a priceless opportunity.

Bang!

“Aaaagh!”

The Evil Sect member Jo Gul stomped on crumpled; using the rebound, he launched himself forward, again and again, like an arrow.

Swaaaak!

A spear rushed up from below, aiming for Jo Gul’s left arm.

But Jo Gul ignored it. If he stopped, he’d die. All he had to do now was advance, and join the main force ahead.

Wham!

“Aaaagh!”

Jo Gul vaulted off another Evil Sect member’s shoulder and arced downward. In his bloodshot eyes, a spear suddenly rose up.

He had to step back or twist his body.

But Jo Gul chose differently. He shoved his sword into its scabbard in an instant and suddenly reached out to grab the spear shaft.

“What?”

Shaaah!

The spear’s tip, laden with internal qi, ripped Jo Gul’s palm in an instant. Yet he did not let go; he pressed down even harder. The sturdy iron spear bent and snapped back like a bow.

Jo Gul used that force to catapult himself forward like a projectile.

Shhk! Shhk!

Blades the Evil Sect men swung in confusion constantly grazed and cut his body.

But Jo Gul didn’t even blink. His gaze fixed solely on the direction he had to go.

They’re there. They must be there! They will be!

And at the moment he finally spotted someone familiar, he couldn’t hold back and shouted as if in a scream.

“Monk Hae Yeon!”

Hae Yeon, who was shaking off the Evil Sect members chasing the group that included Baek Cheon, snapped his gaze up. When he saw Jo Gul bearing Chung Myung and Yu Yiseol over his shoulders, cutting through the distant air toward them, he headed there at once.

“TAOISSSTTTTT!”

Hae Yeon’s body was hardly unscathed. Yet, as if not caring for himself, he leapt without hesitation into the midst of the Evil Sect members.

Kwaaaang!

Those hit by Hae Yeon’s shoulder flew back, spouting blood.

He planted his feet and sent the Evil Sect members flying. Perhaps from overusing his internal qi, blood streamed from his nose and mouth.

But there was no time to tire! He had to clear a path so they could arrive safely!

“A-MI-TA-BUL!”

Hae Yeon lashed out like a madman and struck the Evil Sect members away.

After a while, a clear path opened in the midst of the enemy, carved by his actions.

His internal energy had already been drawn to its limit.

At the moment he hit his limit, Hae Yeon’s eyes flashed and glowed in an almost un-Buddhist way.

“Oooooo!”

Hae Yeon’s fist violently cut through the air.

Kwaaaaaaang!

The pinnacle of Hundred Step Divine Fist. With his current internal qi it shouldn’t be possible to cast it... No, he shouldn’t cast, the apex of Shaolin’s art.

That soul-filled force detonated like fireworks, repelling and blasting the Evil Sect members who stood thickly in their way.

Thud.

In the end Hae Yeon’s knee buckled and he hit the ground. Blood spouting from seven orifices, he steadied himself with a trembling hand on the earth.

‘Ah, not yet...!’

Resolute to risk heart demon, Hae Yeon forced himself up. At that moment, someone burst out from behind with a sharp shout.

“Move aside, monk!”

“T-taoist?”

Suddenly Hae Yeon’s world was covered in red petals.

“Aaaaaah!”

“Kraaaah!”

Tang Soso leapt forward and swung her sword violently and swiftly. Each time Plum Blossom Sword qi poured out, the Evil Sect members’ screams and blood flew.

Tang Soso leapt straight into the air and shouted.

“Sahyung!”

“Yes!”

She leapt over the Evil Sect members’ heads and extended her sword; Jo Gul, as if knowing what she’d do, stomped the blade down with his foot.

The moment the blade’s face met his foot.

“Urrachaah!”

Tang Soso, with a booming shout, swung her sword with all her might.

A sword strike delivered with so much force you could clearly hear the muscles in her shoulders snap.

That strike hurled the three who had put their weight into the sword decisively forward.

“Ugh!”

Thick blood spurted from Jo Gul’s mouth.

It was the price he paid, suppressing even the minimal internal qi that protected his body to fly the farther he could and take the full force of the strike.

A shock so severe the world momentarily went pitch black overwhelmed him.

But even as his vision went dark, he did not forget what he had to do.

“Monk!”

He couldn’t see. But he knew they were there.

Jo Gul hurled the two he’d been carrying over his shoulder forward, firmly believing Hae Yeon would catch them.

When the briefly blackened world regained its color, Jo Gul saw.

Hae Yeon flying through the air, safely catching the two.

“Huh...”

Only then did a small laugh escape him.

The ground surged up toward him.

With a thud Jo Gul was slammed into the earth, bounced a few times, then slammed down again.

“Aah...”

A muffled chuckle escaped Jo Gul’s mouth as he lay sprawled flat.

‘...I did it.’

He had fallen into the very middle of the enemy camp.

Enemies approached the sprawled figure like a pack of jackals.

And he couldn’t resist. He didn’t even have the strength to twitch a finger.

‘What do I care...’

If the price of saving them was his death, he’d die smiling.

Jo Gul grinned grimly and accepted the end.

Paaaaaah!

Then an overwhelming swirl of sword qi roared into his sight.

A red sword qi that seemed driven more by rage than power.

Jo Gul’s eyes widened for a moment.

Those who had lunged at Jo Gul were cleaved apart without even a cry, scattering like rain. Blood poured down over Jo Gul’s fallen face.

And from that cascade of blood someone appeared, grabbed Jo Gul by the collar, and hauled him up.

“You actually did it, you madman!”

“...Sasuk you stayed here?”

“Happy about it?!”

Baek Cheon yanked Jo Gul up and shouted as he brought him fully to his feet.

“Soso!”

“Yes, sasuk!”

Tang Soso rushed over and slung Jo Gul over her shoulders. But Baek Cheon still hadn’t loosened his grip on Jo Gul’s collar.

When Tang Soso looked at him questioningly, Baek Cheon fell silent a moment and then spoke to Jo Gul.

“We’ll talk about the insubordination later.”

“...I wish I’d just die, really.”

“I’m the one who wants to kill you, you damned bastard!”

“...But...”

“What!”

“Why are you crying? It’s embarrassing...”

“Go!”

Baek Cheon shoved Tang Soso forward. He watched her run for a moment, then turned his body without regret.

Finally, everyone had left. Behind him. Now it was his turn to hold them off.

This was the moment he would protect them.

He swung his sword hard. Sword qi burst out explosively, sweeping away those standing around him at once.

“No one gets past here.”

The tip of Baek Cheon’s bloodstained sword gleamed in the sunlight.

“Not until you kill me!”

Chapter 1337: I Will Make Sure That Name Is Engraved (7)

“Green Forest King! Behind you!”

“I know!”

Im So-Byeong, who had glanced back, quickly turned his gaze forward again.

‘Those ruthless bastards!’

They actually pulled off an absurd stunt. This is exactly why he couldn’t shake off the Mount Hua scoundrels.

“Now we just have to get out!”

Those damned Evil Sect men were charging from the front one after another, but they weren’t anything special. A hopeless rabble.

‘No matter if they’re the Ten Thousand People Clan or the Four Sects Alliance, they couldn’t have infinitely recruited troops.’

They could fill numbers, of course, but they couldn’t make up for the qualitative difference. If Jang Ilso himself had acted, it would be different, but the one here was Ho Ga-Myung.

So the problem wasn’t ahead but behind. They had to somehow shake off the pursuit of the Ten Thousand People Clan.

It was Im So-Byeong’s role to come up with that method.

“To the right! Damn Young lord! Don’t just force straight ahead, aim for gaps, you muscle-head!”

“Then hurry up and tell us!”

Namgung Do-Wei shouted but quickly changed direction as Im So-Byeong ordered.

Watching the situation, Im So-Byeong’s eyes flashed coldly.

‘Having many numbers isn’t always an advantage!’

Especially when orders don’t reach properly and troops are mobilized from all directions, the command structure inevitably breaks down.

If exploited properly, it could make them entangle with each other.

‘No, it’s not just possible, it’s necessary!’

“Too far! More to the right!”

“What the...”

“Shut up and do as you’re told!”

Im So-Byeong shouted with an even more brutal force than usual, and Namgung Do-Wei flinched and turned further. Then those charging seemed to avoid him on purpose, opening a path.

Namgung Do-Wei was inwardly alarmed but planted himself without hesitation. A wide path gradually opened where his sword swung.

Seeing that, Im So-Byeong pulled a smirk at one corner of his mouth.

“You were impatient, Ho Ga-Myung.”

He could clearly feel his subordinates watching for cues.

And with good reason.

The Mount Hua’s Sword Saint they’d nearly captured had ultimately rejoined his main force alive, and even those who rushed in to save him had all survived.

They did kill a few more of the Southern Island Sect men in the process, but that was only cutting down minor ranks.

Failure. Considering everything up to now, that’s the only verdict.

Everything sent into this gorge had been for naught.

One versus countless troops, and the sects mobilized, countless were wiped out.

“An accurate assessment.”

“...C-commander?”

Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes calmed as he watched those making a desperate escape.

The Green Forest King’s talent was definitely exceptional. Running his forces with the sole aim of retreat made their speed markedly different from before.

“C-commander! They’re beginning to spread out.”

“I know.”

Those ahead couldn’t stop their escape at all. Moreover, once a breach occurred, they couldn’t even pursue.

All they did was, after being broken through, turn into a wall to block the pursuing Ten Thousand People Clan.

A command failure born of excessive greed.

A strategist would judge it thus: paying the price for blindly throwing uncontrolled men en masse into the battlefield.

But...

“The Green Forest King is certainly an excellent talent. In intellect, he’s better than me.”

“...C-commander?”

“But he lacks experience.”

Questions flashed in the eyes of the officers looking at Ho Ga-Myung. To them, it sounded absurd.

Im So-Byeong had led the Green Forest into countless fights with the Ten Thousand People Clan. To call him inexperienced...

From Ho Ga-Myung’s perspective it might be fair, but by common standards the Green Forest King was more of a veteran for his age.

“How much one has fought isn’t the point. Sometimes too much experience becomes a poison.”

The members held their breath instead of asking questions. That answers were coming without being asked meant Ho Ga-Myung wasn’t talking to them but sorting his own thoughts.

In times like this, one must never interrupt Ho Ga-Myung.

“I hope that certainty doesn’t turn into despair.”

A cruel smile spread across Ho Ga-Myung’s lips.

“Green Forest King!”

Yoon Jong, who had taken Chung Myung from Hae Yeon, ran up and carried him on his back right behind Im So-Byeong.

Im So-Byeong’s face hardened at the sight of Chung Myung.

“Is he even alive?”

“Is that even a question...!”

“...In that state, of all things.”

Im So-Byeong gritted his teeth. Seeing Chung Myung’s gruesome condition, an indescribable fury welled up from deep within him.

“Damn it!”

“Green Forest King! Where are we going now?!”

At the shouted question, Im So-Byeong turned to look back.

Baek Cheon had flown back to hold off the Ten Thousand People Clan pursuing from behind. Those attacking from the front were rabble, and from behind came the Ten Thousand People Clan, his decision was natural.

This meant it was entirely Im So-Byeong’s role to lead everyone ahead and pull them out of danger.

“Did you ask where we’re going?”

“Yes!”

“Asking the obvious stuff now!”

The conditions for where they had to head were fixed.

A place where a few could leverage terrain advantages against many.

And a place where fleeing few could hide themselves with absolute advantage.

There was only one such place nearby. History had proven it; countless had bled to prove its worth. Hence it became cursed ground that no one dared enter.

“Great Mountain!”

Yoon Jong snapped his head up. He saw a wide plain meeting the horizon and jagged peaks stabbing upwards like planted blades.

A shiver ran down his spine.

That mountain range stretching thousands of ri across Guangdong and Guangxi the world called the Ten-Thousand Great Mountains.

“This way!”

Im So-Byeong seized Chung Myung and slung him over his shoulder.

“Help the Young lord! An instant! In an instant we’ll arrive! Then we can lose them!”

“Yes!”

Yoon Jong also ran forward without argument. Among Mount Hua’s disciples, he and Baek Cheon were in the best condition. He had to fulfill his role.

“There’s an ambush on the left! To the right!”

“Yes!”

“After that they’ll charge from the front! The instant you pass the ambush, turn left! Do not overrun your speed! If your waist snaps it’ll be disaster!”

“Understood!”

Yoon Jong swung his sword fiercely.

‘It’s different!’

It didn’t make fighting the enemy easy. But even cutting down one man advanced them significantly more.

‘Is this military strategy?’

He felt in his body what it meant to know the enemy’s flow and strike its grain. A realm he never fathomed; he now, clumsily, understood how strategists view the world.

“Don’t think, just go!”

“Yes!”

Im So-Byeong gritted his teeth.

‘One chance is enough!’

They only needed to hide within the Ten Thousand Great Mountains and buy time once. Then the situation would be incomparably better. They just had to reach Great Mountain.

“Go straight! Break through at full power!”

“Yes!”

As those ahead increased speed, Geum Yang-Baek also shouted himself hoarse.

“Don’t fall behind! You must catch up!”

At the same time, an elder collapsed to the ground. But Geum Yang-Baek squeezed his eyes shut and looked away. There was no time to tend to each person now.

“Run, hurry!”

Im So-Byeong checked the rear.

Hwaaaaak!

From the rear, red plum blossoms were erupting explosively. Even the Ten Thousand People Clan would find it hard to pierce that impossible sword qi.

The divine might Mount Hua’s disciples displayed in this pursuit was, to say the least, oft-praised.

But the sword qi Baek Cheon was showing stood out even among those Mount Hua disciples.

‘Lucky we didn’t meet him as an enemy.’

Baek Cheon would definitely hold the rear. So now they only had to think ahead and run.

“Keep going!”

Im So-Byeong screamed until his throat hurt. At the same time he whirled his fan brilliantly. Those rushing from the side were sliced by the rippling qi he emitted and toppled en masse.

“Press them!”

Gwak Hwan-So charged the hesitating Evil Sect members and drove his sword in hard. The reinvigorated Southern Island Sect disciples also drew on their last strength to swing swords and surge forward.

Their numbers had already been reduced by more than half. The sacrifices made to get this far were immense.

But now was not the time to dwell on sacrifices already made. They had to look only ahead, to where they must go now.

“Ugh, ughhh...”

“G-go away!”

Blood splattered over them, Yoon Jong and Namgung Do-Wei charged like demons, and the Evil Sect men recoiled in terror. The two swung their swords as if to devour the frightened.

Namgung Do-Wei’s explosive sword and Yoon Jong’s perfectly restrained blade combined in a harmony of discord to ravage the enemy.

Swoooooosh!

Slash! Slash! Cut them down again!

It was as Yoon Jong, half out of his mind, cut down the enemy right before him.

“Guh!”

The man whose throat was cut grabbed the wound and fell aside. In that moment Yoon Jong realized there was no one left to stop him.

Before he knew it, a hill-like mountain loomed right ahead of him.

Between the towering peaks a clear path was visible.

“Green Forest King!”

“At the rear! Hold off the pursuers! Send the Southern Island men in first!”

At those words, Yoon Jong felt an indescribable thrill.

They had arrived.

They had crossed the sea, passed Guangdong, pierced through the hellish gorge, and finally reached this place.

“Keep going! We’ll hold the rear!”

“But...!”

“Go, hurry!”

At Yoon Jong’s shout, the Southern Island disciples gritted their teeth and ran. The mountains rising on either side weren’t very tall, but they were so steep they resembled cliffs as the Southern Island disciples dashed between them.

“Aaaah!”

Some collapsed outright from the blows, and others, having reached this spot, collapsed exhausted.

But most of the Southern Island disciples, in the meantime, rushed into the promised land of the Ten Thousand Great Mountains without looking back.

“Monk!”

“Yes!”

Hae Yeon, carrying Yu Yiseol, hurriedly followed. Tang Soso, carrying Jo Gul, also ran at full speed and took the lead.

“S-sahyung...”

“Go ahead, Gul!”

Yoon Jong met Jo Gul’s eyes and nodded deeply.

Yoon Jong quickly checked the rear. At that moment he saw countless plum blossom leaves scattered across this Southern land.

Perfect sword qi, flawless in every bit!

Baek Cheon, who had torn the clinging enemies away at once, was running up.

“Sasuk!”

Baek Cheon’s whole body was stained with blood. Yoon Jong at first thought it was enemy blood, but on closer look much of it was Baek Cheon’s own. Realizing this, a scream involuntarily escaped Yoon Jong’s mouth.

“Run!”

But Baek Cheon urged Yoon Jong on instead.

Yoon Jong, Im So-Byeong, Namgung Do-Wei and Tang Pae passed by Baek Cheon and entered the Ten-Thousand Great Mountains.

“Sasuk!”

“We’ll cut them off before they catch up! Let’s go!”

“Yes!”

Yoon Jong’s voice was slightly uplifted.

They hadn’t completely shaken off the pursuit yet.

But now the situation would be very different. These thornlike mountains rising from the earth would provide places to hide and make the enemy’s pursuit difficult.

Simply being able to ambush would change everything. Wasn’t that what they’d thought upon seeing the Ten-Thousand Great Mountains on the way to Southern Islands?

‘We can do it!’

The sacrifices made to get here, their cost, were not in vain.

As Yoon Jong unconsciously clenched his fist, he saw those gathered up ahead.

“Don’t wait, keep moving, either way!”

At that, the Southern Island Sect’s disciples ahead turned to look back.

The moment the Mount Hua disciples saw those faces, they instinctively knew something was wrong.

The Southern Island Sect’s disciples’ faces were ashen, as if they’d met a ghost.

“W-what’s wrong?”

“...sir.”

“Yes?”

A voice full of despair slipped from Gwak Hwan-So’s mouth.

“The front... the front is blocked, Taoist.”

For a moment everyone fell silent as if speechless. A bleak wind swept through the still space.

Cold sweat formed on Im So-Byeong’s brow.

“N-no way...”

Im So-Byeong hurriedly looked out beyond the mountain valley.

Step. Step. Step. Ho Ga-Myung slowly advanced toward the valley Mount Hua and Southern Island had entered. The satisfied smile at his lips betrayed his intent.

“They were enemies, but excellent. So...”

A voice devoid of feeling slipped out.

“I’ll at least engrave that name. So that everyone will clearly know his end.”

It was a declaration marking the end of the long pursuit.

Chapter 1338: What Is Happening? (1)

Cold sweat ran down Im So-Byeong’s face.

“...We’re trapped.”

A terrain where three mountains meet. The path, naturally, opens both to the left and to the right.

But whichever path they took, the end was the same.

A sheer, dizzying cliff.

A steep rock wall that would better be called ‘despair’ barred the way between the mountains.

Even in perfect condition it didn’t look easy to climb. So with their internal qi depleted and their bodies exhausted, there was no point discussing it now.

They could force themselves to climb, but the outcome was obvious. The pursuing Ten Thousand People Clan would slaughter them before they could resist.

Climbing the mountain wasn’t any better. The steep, rugged slope and dense forest would sap their strength and leave them even more vulnerable to attack.

So this place was basically a jar sealed by cliffs on all sides. Im So-Byeong had led everyone into a terrain closed off on all sides by his own feet.

“How did...”

The Ten-Thousand Great Mountains consist of layer upon layer of peaks, making it very hard to read the terrain. It’s difficult to know what lies beyond a mountain.

They had already confirmed this on the way to the Southern Island, and for that very reason Im So-Byeong had taken this as their escape route.

But even Im So-Byeong hadn’t imagined there’d be such a sheer cliff behind the mountain.

“Ho Ga-Myung...”

Grit.

He bit his lips until they bled.

He had thought he’d broken the encirclement. He thought he’d foiled every ambush. At least in terms of strategy, he believed he was ahead by more than half.

Because that’s how it’s been so far.

But in this moment he felt it: from the moment they entered that canyon until now, they’d been playing into Ho Ga-Myung’s hands.

‘It wasn’t amateurish...’

He had assumed the hastily assembled forces could only hurl themselves in blindly. But not only could they do that—they were enough. The plan had been to open and guide the path visible only to him.

Would it have been different if they’d bypassed the canyon?

No, it wouldn’t have. A different approach doesn’t change the destination.

Only the timing would differ; in the end they’d still have ended up in these Ten-Thousand Great Mountains.

Why hadn’t he suspected it? Why hadn’t he been wary of the Mind-Probing Inquisitor Ho Ga-Myung? Why had he not assumed Ho Ga-Myung could always stay one step ahead if he chose?

Ho Ga-Myung had actually accomplished more than what Im So-Byeong had achieved in leading the Green Forest.

“G-Green Forest King.”

Geum Yang-Baek’s voice was heard. Im So-Byeong’s eyes trembled. For the first time in his life, he realized someone speaking to him could frighten him.

“What should we do now?”

Im So-Byeong gritted his teeth.

What should they do? He wanted to ask instead—what on earth could they do in this situation?

The path they wanted was blocked by a high cliff, and thousands of troops were pressing in from behind.

And the allies who would have to face those frenzied enemies had long since reached their limits. Their strength to fight was already exhausted.

“Green Forest King!”

Geum Yang-Baek raised his voice again, but Im So-Byeong could not answer.

Countless words flashed through his mind, but any of them would only drag morale down in this situation.

And in that moment Im So-Byeong saw—no, felt—something.

The moment he hesitated to answer, he felt something leave the faces of the Southern Island disciples he was watching. The letters ‘hope’ they’d been clinging to vanished futilely.

‘No, not like this...’

What had kept their exhausted bodies moving had been the hope of survival and the will to fight the enemy.

The moment they lost that hope and will, they’d be like rats trapped in a jar.

So he had to somehow prevent them from losing all will.

‘But how?’

Should he urge them to fight to the death to take down at least one more enemy?

Or should he tell them, since they’re doomed anyway, to meet death solemnly and gloriously?

No strategy Im So-Byeong had studied, nor any of his experience, could answer that question. So he merely floundered helplessly in the approaching despair.

Then, someone’s calm voice was heard.

“Surrounded on all sides...”

It was Baek Cheon, who’d been blocking the pursuers from behind and arrived here bloodied at the end.

He looked around without any hurry. After carefully checking the high cliffs and surrounding terrain, he slowly nodded.

“This place will be difficult.”

Im So-Byeong felt his strength drain away in an instant.

Im So-Byeong realized he’d unknowingly relied on that Acting Sect Leader to some degree.

The moment Baek Cheon’s negative words came out, the thread of hope he had been holding onto felt like it snapped.

Baek Cheon turned and stared at the path they had come in by.

“Then there’s no choice. We’ll have to break back out and find another route.”

Im So-Byeong questioned his own ears for a moment.

What did that man just say?

Baek Cheon spoke calmly.

“Green Forest King.”

“Yes?”

“Are there many spots like this here?”

It might have seemed abrupt, but Im So-Byeong grasped Baek Cheon’s intention even then.

“I don’t think so. If there were many places like this, the Demonic Cult couldn’t have waged guerrilla warfare here...”

“Then that’s enough.”

Baek Cheon took a deep breath.

“So we just need to break out here and re-enter by a side path.”

“...”

“Is that right?”

Im So-Byeong couldn’t bring himself to answer.

Of course it was right. Of course it was the correct thing to say.

Except that to get back out and find another route, they’d have to break through the Ten Thousand People Clan’s main force, which was enraged.

“Now, Acting Sect Leader. That...”

“Is there any other way?”

Baek Cheon’s calm question shut Im So-Byeong’s mouth once more.

Another way? There was no such thing. If there were, Im So-Byeong would have used it already.

There was nothing wrong with Baek Cheon’s words. The only option they had was to open that path again. Or else...

“So all we need to do is open the path?”

Yoon Jong’s question from behind was met with Baek Cheon’s nod without even turning.

“It seems so.”

“Good, not complicated. Then, Green Forest King.”

“Yes?”

“Take care of this one for me, please.”

Yoon Jong handed Chung Myung, who he’d been carrying piggyback, to Im So-Byeong.

“Uh...”

“Guard him with your life.”

Im So-Byeong accepted Chung Myung with a dazed expression. Yoon Jong, without another word, stood beside Baek Cheon.

“Monk, take care of our sahyung for us.”

“Taoist...”

“Quickly.”

Tang Soso, too, tried to hand Jo Gul to Hae Yeon and move forward. Jo Gul urgently reached out and grabbed Tang Soso’s sleeve.

“I—I also...”

“Huh?”

“I too... I still want to fight... ugh!”

Tang Soso’s large needle mercilessly struck Jo Gul’s hand that held the sleeve. Jo Gul flinched in surprise as Tang Soso snorted with a smile.

“The bleeding will slow down.”

“...”

“Pull yourself together. We’re about to die for real. Honestly, our sahyungs are hopeless.”

Tang Soso quickly withdrew the needle and stood beside Yoon Jong.

Everyone stared blankly at the three from behind.

“Green Forest King.”

“...Yes?”

“We’ll hold back the oncoming ones. Lead everyone through the opened path. Namgung Young lord and Tang Young lord will help.”

“...”

“We believe in you.”

Im So-Byeong’s pupils trembled tightly.

How could they step forward so plainly even in this situation?

They understood. It was like smashing an egg against a rock. No matter how hard they fought, annihilation was likely.

Yet they gladly stepped forward, unlike him who was seized by despair and at a loss.

Im So-Byeong bit his lip hard.

Logically, there was only one choice here: abandon Southern Island like baggage and have at least themselves climb the cliff.

If they did that, the Southern Island disciples following below would act as shields, allowing the rest to survive climbing the mountain.

Wouldn’t they know this?

No, they know. They would definitely know. No one is as used to climbing cliffs as those from Mount Hua.

But everyone acted as if such an option didn’t exist. They intended to protect Southern Island to the very end.

Foolish and stupid.

But even Im So-Byeong, who’d lived his life as an Evil Sect member for gain, couldn’t bring himself to say that aloud. The same would be true of anyone else.

“Sasuk. Seems like there are a lot of enemies, doesn’t it?”

“You’ll have a hard time.”

“You speak well, that one you do amazingly.”

“How presumptuous toward a sasuk.”

Tang Soso’s hand, laughing and talking with Baek Cheon, trembled slightly. Im So-Byeong saw that hand and understood.

They weren’t calm because they weren’t afraid. They were desperately suppressing the urge to run.

That realization made Im So-Byeong unbearable. Even if others didn’t know, he knew that his arrogance had caused this whole situation.

Had he ever doubted Ho Ga-Myung even once while overpowering him, they wouldn’t have walked into this trap by their own feet.

Just as Im So-Byeong was about to say something.

“Stop this now, Acting Sect Leader.”

A calm voice flowed out. Everyone’s eyes turned that way. Geum Yang-Baek, pale from poison and wounds, was looking at Baek Cheon.

“Do not shame us any further.”

“...Sect leader.”

“The sacrifices you’ve already shown for Southern Island Sect are enough. To ask more would be mere shame.”

“Sect leader, we still...”

“Take the wounded and climb the cliff. Southern Island Sect will hold the rear.”

“S-Sect leader!”

Southern Island Sect’s disciples turned in surprise to Geum Yang-Baek. But Geum Yang-Baek answered calmly.

“Wasn’t that the plan from the start?”

Geum Yang-Baek’s gaze turned to Chung Myung on Im So-Byeong’s back.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint told us at least not to sit and wait for death. To go die where one can die with honor.”

“...”

“I think this place is exactly that place.”

“Sect leader. We can still...”

“Acting Sect Leader.”

Geum Yang-Baek smiled kindly.

“To be honest, I agreed with that statement and yet disagreed it. I couldn’t understand how rushing into the enemy and dying in battle was necessarily honorable.”

“...”

“But now it’s fine. Though it’s hard to find honor in the annihilation that comes from fighting the Ten Thousand People Clan...”

Baek Cheon looked at Chung Myung and spoke.

“Now I think I know. What honor we must protect last. What we must protect last. What our role is.”

Baek Cheon’s shoulders began to tremble faintly.

Southern Island’s disciples, too, couldn’t bring themselves to speak and simply looked at Geum Yang-Baek.

“Until now you have protected us. You shed blood in our stead and bore the burdens we shouldered. So at least... now let us repay that debt.”

Geum Yang-Baek asked as he looked around at everyone.

“Isn’t that right?”

Light returned to Southern Island Sect’s disciples’ eyes.

It’s not that they didn’t understand the situation. They only needed the final resolve.

And Geum Yang-Baek’s words now gave them the courage for that final resolve.

“At least at the end, be true to Southern Island. Wouldn’t it be shameful for Southern Island’s seafolk to die only ever receiving help from those from the land?”

“That’s right, Sect leader!”

“We will do it!”

“Aren’t you tired of only running away?”

Southern Island’s disciples raised their voices as if shouting defiantly.

They had always run away without having a proper chance to fight.

They couldn’t accept fleeing under someone’s protection until the very end only to be stabbed in the back and die. That would tarnish even their final moment.

“Even in the afterlife, we must have words to say to the sahyung who died before us.”

Geum Yang-Baek smiled softly at that.

“True enough.”

They spoke more calmly and composedly than they had so far.

Baek Cheon’s face darkened as he watched.

There’s no desperate resolve on display. No boiling fighting spirit. But precisely because of that, he could tell how firm their decision was.

To insist further would be an insult to them. Even Baek Cheon understood that much.

“Hwan-So, Ja Yang!”

“Yes, Sect leader.”

Gwak Hwan-So and Lee Ja-Yang, their eyes set with firm resolve, rushed out at Geum Yang-Baek’s words.

“You two follow them.”

“...What?”

“Wait, Sect leader. What do you mean by that!”

“If only you two survive, Southern Island won’t be considered as erased. Live and someday raise Southern Island’s name across the world again.”

“Sect leader!”

Gwak Hwan-So prepared to strongly object, but Geum Yang-Baek gripped his shoulder.

“I beg you.”

“...”

Gwak Hwan-So’s shoulders trembled.

“Please, sahyung.”

“Lee Ja-Yang sahyung, I beg you. Don’t let our deaths be meaningless.”

“Survive.”

Under the torrent of voices and gazes, tears began to pour from the two men’s eyes.

They couldn’t refuse. There was no time to stall by saying they wouldn’t go.

So they couldn’t even offer a brief refusal. That fact made it unbearable for Gwak Hwan-So and Lee Ja-Yang.

“Acting Sect Leader. Please take care of them.”

“...Sect leader.”

Geum Yang-Baek smiled gently and stepped forward.

He brushed past Baek Cheon and whispered softly so only he could hear.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint must be kept alive.”

When Baek Cheon couldn’t bring himself to reply and bit his lip.

“...Cough.”

“Chung Myung!”

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint!”

Chung Myung, who had been piggybacked on Im So-Byeong’s back, coughed up blood and opened his eyes. His half-lidded, blurred gaze scanned the surroundings.

“...Where is this...”

Chung Myung’s eyes grew clearer.

“We were toyed with.”

Im So-Byeong bowed his head deeply. But the follow-up was different from what he’d expected.

“...Just as expected.”

Im So-Byeong looked up in surprise.

Even then, Chung Myung’s expression remained ambiguous, revealing nothing of his thoughts.

Chapter 1339: What Is Happening? (2)

Ho Ga-Myung repeatedly clenched his fists tightly and loosened them. His fingertips tingled.

After a moment, he opened his closed eyes.

‘Any variables?’

No matter how many times he checked—ten times, twenty times—there were no remaining variables. Yet he, unfatigued, kept searching for any possibility that a variable might exist.

‘None.’

No matter how he thought about it, nothing came to mind. Even if those trapped inside were Shaolin instead of Mount Hua, there would be no way out.

“At last...”

A long sigh escaped Ho Ga-Myung’s mouth.

After a long hunt from Southern Island to here, they had finally driven them into a dead end. Only after so many sacrifices.

“Commander!”

The aides’ faces were flushed as well.

It was a natural reaction—they knew they had finally put a blade to their throats. Having cast them into a swamp with no escape left, only slaughter remained.

But Ho Ga-Myung’s expression, after doing all this, had not changed at all. He only watched the path between the mountains with calm eyes.

‘Green Forest King.’

The Green Forest King must have thought he could manipulate and toy with the situation.

He had never truly experienced defeat. He may have lost before due to lack of resources or numbers, but he was certain he would never lose a battle of wits.

What Ho Ga-Myung exploited was precisely that arrogance of Im So-Byeong.

‘This is what fighting on enemy ground looks like.’

The Green Forest is used to mountains. Im So-Byeong, in particular, was oddly knowledgeable about mountainous terrain—so much so one might suspect he carried the layouts of all the world’s mountains in his head.

But even he would not have the terrain of the Ten Thousand Great Mountains in his mind. The Green Forest did not set foot there.

The Green Forest is not composed of mountain dwellers. They live where people pass through. So there was no reason for them to care about the uninhabited Ten-Thousand Great Mountains.

But Ho Ga-Myung was not like that.

This place was Guangdong, where the Ten Thousand People Clan had fought countless battles. Even if he did not know the interior of the Ten-Thousand Great Mountains, he knew the routes from Guangdong into the great range as if in the palm of his hand.

So there was only one thing Ho Ga-Myung had to do.

Make them believe they were choosing everything of their own accord.

“Commander! Give the order.”

“Wait.”

“...What...?”

Ho Ga-Myung watched ahead with an emotionless gaze and said.

“We must wait until we are certain everyone has fallen into the trap. If they do not know that fact that they are trapped, it will be no different from a frontal charge and fight.”

Ho Ga-Myung slowly and lightly clenched and unclenched his fist.

“Once they fully realize they’ve fallen into a trap, the situation will change. People typically fall into despair at such times.”

“Ha, but couldn’t they also make a desperate stand?”

“Fighting with your back to a cliff and being unexpectedly driven to a cliff are entirely different. In the end, they won’t be able to handle the rising despair. Any forced fervor cools quickly at the slightest breeze.”

“Ah...”

“Just wait a little. Not a long time.”

The aide’s fingertips trembled slightly.

They had finally driven the enemy—who had slipped through their grasp like a mouse—into that place. Yet even in this situation Ho Ga-Myung showed no excitement and observed the situation coolly.

A composure they would not dare to imitate. This must be why Mind-Probing Inquisitor Ho Ga-Myung had become famous across the land.

“If they try to flee, that would be fine too.”

“Yes.”

Then they only needed to stab the backs of those halfway up the cliff and kill them.

“Could it be... Commander, did you have this place in mind from the start?”

Ho Ga-Myung slightly frowned. He looked displeased by the question. But the aide, elated, did not notice his expression.

Ho Ga-Myung answered with a light sigh.

“...If I were the Alliance lord, I would have.”

“What do you mean by that...”

“The Alliance lord could. Driving them from the Southern Island all the way to here—such an absurd feat.”

“...”

“But I am not the Alliance lord. No, I cannot be the Alliance lord. I am merely a commoner. So not the same process, only the same result.”

The aide tilted his head. He did not fully understand what Ho Ga-Myung meant.

How can the result be the same if the process is different?

“The same result?”

“There are more than ten terrains capable of channeling them off Guangdong’s roads.”

“...”

“This is only one of them. If they had chosen another route, I’d have driven them there instead.”

The aide stared at Ho Ga-Myung blankly.

Ho Ga-Myung said he chose this method because he couldn’t measure up to Jang Ilso, but to the aide it was impossible to tell which was more remarkable.

In truth, this was only natural for Ho Ga-Myung.

If a commoner wanted to enter a contest bearing the great name ‘the world’, he had to struggle until his nails broke and rage relentlessly.

If he could not, he would be unworthy to stand beside Jang Ilso.

‘Im So-Byeong is still too much.’

Of course the Green Forest King was great. But even Im So-Byeong could not escape the realm of prodigy.

Ho Ga-Myung had long admitted he was a commoner, but Im So-Byeong still brimmed with confidence that his intellect would rival anyone’s.

That difference produced the present result. The convinced do not doubt their own victory.

“The time has come.”

Ho Ga-Myung slowly unclenched his tightly held fist.

“Attack. Leave not a single one alive—kill them all.”

“Yes!”

No sooner had Ho Ga-Myung spoken than the aides ordered the attack. The Ten Thousand People Clan members, like hounds waiting only for their master’s word and baring their teeth, started to run. They poured forward like an avalanche of earth.

Ho Ga-Myung smoothed his already parched lips.

There were clearly no variables. But one possibility remained by which he might fail his objective.

The possibility that the men from the Heavenly Friends Alliance would abandon the Southern Island Sect and climb the cliff among themselves to escape.

If they chose that, it would be difficult to end their lives here.

Normally, given the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s conduct, it wasn’t a variable worth considering, but in this moment he had to consider it.

Because it was Mount Hua’s Sword Saint who had lost consciousness.

Mount Hua’s Sword Saint would certainly not back down, but the others were uncertain. At the very end, people reveal their true natures.

But...

‘If that happens, they would no longer be the Heavenly Friends Alliance.’

Ho Ga-Myung knew: sometimes a person’s value comes from something not measurable by quantity.

That applied not only to them but to Jang Ilso as well.

Could Jang Ilso still be Jang Ilso without his dominance? Could a Jang Ilso who abandoned ambition and chose to be content with reality wield the same influence?

Absolutely impossible.

Even if his force remained the same... no, even if his objective strength grew, Jang Ilso would become someone who could never look beyond that.

Jang Ilso’s value lay in his very dominance.

The same went for them.

If, at the final moment, they abandoned what they tried to protect and barely clung to their lives as they fled, could one still call them the Heavenly Friends Alliance?

They would die in that moment.

They might keep their lives, but the Heavenly Friends Alliance that had matched the Nine Great Sects and even pressured the Four Sects Alliance would cease to exist.

Even if it wasn’t a path Mount Hua’s Sword Saint chose himself, the blame would fall upon him. In that moment, the Justice faction would lose its center.

‘I’m curious.’

What will they choose?

Will they honor the grand talks they boast about each time and die to preserve their name? Or will they save their pitiful lives and bury that name in the dung heap?

Ho Ga-Myung stared forward with cold eyes.

The Ten Thousand People Clan members’ shouts and the sound of their footsteps pierced his ears like music. Soon someone’s scream would start to mix into it.

‘Is what remains damage control?’

In any case, the fact that he disobeyed Jang Ilso’s order remained unchanged. And that he suffered heavy losses at the hands of a small enemy force also remained unchanged.

So naturally he would have to take responsibility.

Even if the price was his life, Ho Ga-Myung would never regret it.

If his life could bring Jang Ilso one step closer to his great work, he would gladly smile from the afterlife.

A faint smile bloomed on Ho Ga-Myung’s lips. It seemed the first genuine smile since meeting those men.

But the expected screams did not come. Instead, a strange sight began to appear.

Some of the Ten Thousand People Clan who had rushed into the canyon of the Ten-Thousand Great Mountains were coming back out and running toward them.

‘What is this?’

A situation report? No, that shouldn’t be necessary now.

“Co-Commander!”

Pale-faced men bowed before him.

“What’s wrong?”

“Th-they’ve disappeared.”

For a moment, Ho Ga-Myung could not understand the words. He had considered every move in his head, and this reported situation was not among them, so he became baffled.

“What do you mean by that?”

“Th-they’ve disappeared!”

Ho Ga-Myung looked blankly at those bowing and asked again.

“Disappeared?”

“Yes!”

“You say they’ve disappeared?”

The bowed men held their breath. They knew how absurd the situation was.

“You mean they climbed the cliff?”

“T-there’s no trace! They’ve literally vanished. Like... like a mirage...”

“Don’t talk nonsense!”

Ho Ga-Myung shouted, enraged. Everyone jumped and looked at him. Truly, they had never seen him raise his voice like that.

“Get out of the way!”

Ho Ga-Myung shoved aside the bowing men as if kicking them away and ran toward the place himself.

‘This is impossible!’

If only the Heavenly Friends Alliance members had vanished while the Southern Island Sect remained, one could understand. But for the Southern Island Sect’s disciples to have disappeared entirely is nonsense.

How could such a thing be possible unless they truly became immortals and worked transformations?

Impossible. Such a thing cannot be!

But shortly after, what Ho Ga-Myung encountered was only the stupid faces of Ten Thousand People Clan members, flustered, bewildered, and looking around.

They were truly gone.

The towering cliffs and steep mountains remained, but there was not a trace of the Southern Island Sect’s men. They had vanished as if rising to the sky.

“Th-this...”

Ho Ga-Myung’s hand began to tremble as if seized by fever.

How? How could this happen? How?

Ho Ga-Myung, always cold and composed, frantically looked around like a madman.

“U... Uu...”

After a moment, he began tearing at his hair. Hair that had been neatly arranged fell loose and disheveled.

When something beyond reason occurs, there’s usually a particular culprit behind it.

“Mount... Hua’s Swooooord...”

A beast-like howl burst from Ho Ga-Myung’s mouth.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saaaaaaaaaaaint!”

His scream climbed the cliff and rose into the sky, ringing out miserably.

Chapter 1340: What Is Happening? (3)

Drip. Drip.

The sound of water droplets falling echoed clearly.

Im So-Byeong looked up. Water had gathered on the jagged rock jutting from the ceiling and was falling drop by drop.

‘Where on earth is this...’

His gaze returned downward.

It was so dark he couldn’t see an inch ahead. Unless one had trained in martial arts and developed qi-sense, the darkness was thick enough to stop anyone from taking a step. The only faint thing visible was a narrow, long path stretching into the distance.

‘A cave... no, a grotto?’

The grotto they had come through extended long behind them.

He was still dumbfounded as to how they had come here. His mind was in turmoil.

“Cough.”

At that moment, Chung Myung, walking ahead of him, coughed and hunched over. Every time he moved, flakes of dried blood crumbled away.

Im So-Byeong hardened his face and spoke.

“Taoist...”

“I’m fine.”

But Chung Myung raised his head as if it were nothing and continued forward.

A sigh escaped Im So-Byeong. Even though he had been offered a piggyback, he stubbornly insisted on walking on his own. Who could break that stubbornness?

After following silently for a while, Im So-Byeong could no longer hold back and asked.

“Taoist... where is this place?”

“A cave.”

“Pardon?”

“Can’t you see? It’s a cave.”

Im So-Byeong’s face twisted slightly.

“No, I’m not asking that. What kind of cave is this?”

Even as he spoke, he felt the question itself was absurd. A cave is a cave; how could it have a special purpose?

But in fact, the question was very reasonable.

Im So-Byeong scanned the cave walls.

No matter how he looked, it didn’t seem naturally formed. It was rough, but clearly touched by human hands.

‘Someone dug a cave this size?’

Why? For what reason?

Then Chung Myung’s voice sounded softly.

“Do you know where this is?”

“...I have no idea.”

“The edge of the Ten Thousand Great Mountains.”

Im So-Byeong’s eyes narrowed slightly. What did that mean...

“Most people think the war with the Demonic Cult was fought over single peaks like a scramble, but in reality thousands of battles took place in the south of the Central Plains, over many years.”

“...”

“The place with the most fighting wasn’t the center of the Ten Thousand Great Mountains. It was the approaches leading to it.”

“So this is...”

“Yes.”

Chung Myung answered in a cold voice.

“It’s one of the hideouts they dug.”

Im So-Byeong looked around again with a stiff face.

‘Someplace like this...’

The Ten Thousand Great Mountains themselves are a natural stronghold. Who would have imagined they’d also bother to create grottoes like this in such mountains?

But there was another reason Im So-Byeong was truly shocked.

From a strategist’s point of view, nothing was more chilling than this grotto. The mountain was already hard to predict where enemies might appear.

And the existence of such holes meant the enemy could burst up from underground.

‘How on earth did they deal with such bastards?’

The moment the Demonic Cult people he had seen in Hangzhou merged with this terrain in his mind, a chill ran through him. The past wars, which he’d only vaguely imagined as brutal, unfolded vividly before him.

Moreover, despite the long time passed, this cold grotto still smelled faintly of blood.

“When you say ‘one of them,’ does that mean there are more than this place?”

“Countless.”

A hollow laugh escaped Im So-Byeong.

But before his thoughts could go further, three paths appeared before them.

“Uh...”

Everyone is the same in such a situation. When faced with a fork in an unfamiliar place, one naturally stands and hesitates.

But Chung Myung entered the left path without a moment’s hesitation, as if he already knew the way.

Im So-Byeong narrowed his eyes slightly and voiced his doubt.

“Why this way...”

“The wall.”

“...The wall?”

At the concise reply, Im So-Byeong inspected the wall Chung Myung had mentioned.

“Ah...”

And soon he understood the meaning. Marks that hadn’t been visible before cluttered the left wall, like a giant beast’s sharp claws had raked it.

They were clearly marks left by countless weapons scraping the wall during battle.

‘So a battle took place here?’

Even deep within this cave?

Crunch!

Suddenly Im So-Byeong flinched at the feeling of stepping on something.

When he checked what it was, his color drained slightly. He had stepped on a yellowed human bone.

“...Corpses...”

A continuous groan came from behind him as well.

Bodies were strewn all over the cramped cave floor. Corpses long decayed until only bones remained—similar to sights they’d seen before when breaking through the Ten Thousand Great Mountains and heading south.

“They’re the Pine Pattern Ancient Sword.”

“Of Wudang?”

After that exchange, a brief silence fell.

A narrow cave deep underground where not a single ray of light reached. Even here the Demonic Cult and the Justice faction had fought to the death.

It was clear the Justice faction, unable to endure the enemy’s guerrilla tactics, had risked their lives to enter this dangerous terrain.

“Taoist.”

As Southern Island Sect’s disciples trembled at the horror, Mount Hua’s disciples and Im So-Byeong fixed their gazes solely on Chung Myung.

“Taoist... then did you know there was an entrance there leading to this grotto?”

“Yes.”

“...Th-then did you also know Ho Ga-Myung would herd us toward that entrance?”

Chung Myung nodded slowly.

“Well, he’s that kind of man.”

Im So-Byeong was momentarily stunned.

“So you even knew I would be manipulated by Ho Ga-Myung and driven into that dead end?”

“No.”

“...What? Then...?”

“I was just preparing for that possibility.”

Chung Myung answered without turning his head. Whether he didn’t feel the need to meet their eyes or lacked the strength to turn was unclear.

“If we don’t get trapped, that’s fine. But if we do get trapped, you have to have a way out ready to pick.”

“...”

“The moment we broke through the gorge, the path was already open.”

Im So-Byeong lost his words and stared blankly at Chung Myung’s back.

He had been completely outplayed by Ho Ga-Myung. Perhaps it was the price of arrogance.

He had believed his rival in strategy was Jang Ilso, not Ho Ga-Myung. Thus Ho Ga-Myung hadn’t been in his consideration at all.

Whatever the reason, it was certain he had been utterly defeated by Ho Ga-Myung in a game of wits.

But who won originally didn’t matter at all. Every battle was being played in Chung Myung’s palm.

‘What on earth is this man...’

Only now did Im So-Byeong feel he might understand Ho Ga-Myung’s mindset.

Why Ho Ga-Myung didn’t include a man like Jang Ilso within the reach of his schemes.

Who wouldn’t feel that way if painstakingly devised strategies ended up playing into someone else’s hands?

As Im So-Byeong struggled with rising helplessness and dismay, Baek Cheon spoke up.

“Chung Myung.”

Chung Myung didn’t answer. But Baek Cheon asked without hesitation.

“Then how did you know there was an entrance there leading into this place?”

At those words, several of Mount Hua’s disciples flinched and glanced between Baek Cheon and Chung Myung.

It wasn’t the first time. Chung Myung had repeatedly produced information they couldn’t even guess, startling everyone.

But until now no one had pressed him on it. They assumed anything he did was possible, so they didn’t ask—an unspoken rule regarding Mount Hua’s Chung Myung.

But now Baek Cheon, as if refusing to accept that rule any longer, asked the most sensitive question without hesitation.

“Answer.”

Baek Cheon urged him. This time he didn’t seem willing to let Chung Myung’s silence pass by.

After a moment Chung Myung opened his mouth. But the start of his words was somewhat unexpected.

“Do you know why the Green Forest King got entangled with Ho Ga-Myung?”

“...What are you talking about all of a sudden?”

“Because he didn’t know.”

Baek Cheon looked over at Im So-Byeong with questioning eyes. Im So-Byeong’s face had hardened; the smile gone, he looked cold.

“If he had known such terrain existed there, he would never have entered it of his own accord. Fighting in an opponent’s inner chamber is like that—there are tremendous advantages you can’t guess without experience.”

Baek Cheon gave a slight nod at Chung Myung’s words. Whether it was a satisfactory answer or not aside, his point had been felt intensely throughout this journey.

Put yourself in the other position and it’s clear.

Someone coming to Mount Hua for the first time to attack—more than five deadly terrains immediately come to Baek Cheon’s mind. Natural hazards that would send attackers to hell.

“But the point is this: if you know, you can guess their intentions. Especially if they assume I don’t know that terrain.”

“...”

“The Green Forest King didn’t know this terrain existed, and Ho Ga-Myung didn’t think I knew it. So they were caught in their plan. Because they didn’t know.”

Baek Cheon fixed his stern eyes on Chung Myung’s back.

“War isn’t decided by force alone. More precisely, power isn’t only swinging a blade. Anything that can funnel the opponent where you want is power.”

Chung Myung slowly turned to look at Baek Cheon. His gaze, visible between hair matted with blood, was cold.

“How did I know?”

“...”

“It’s not that I didn’t know what this is. I did know of this. That’s why I went there. No matter how much Ho Ga-Myung thinks he knows Guangdong, I’d know more.”

“...That is...”

“How did I know more?”

Chung Myung twisted the corner of his mouth.

“How do you think I found out?”

Baek Cheon felt a surge of emotion and was about to raise his voice. At that moment someone grabbed his sleeve.

Turning around, he saw Yu Yiseol, only just regaining consciousness, pulling his sleeve.

Bloodied, she slowly shook her head, as if to say don’t ask more.

Baek Cheon was about to say something, but before he could, Chung Myung’s footsteps changed slightly.

The narrow, long end ended. A space opened up so wide it was hard to believe they were still underground.

“It’ll take them at least two days to find the entrance. Rest fully until then.”

With that even-toned instruction, Chung Myung collapsed forward.

“Ch—”

Before anyone could make a sound, Baek Cheon moved swiftly and caught the collapsing Chung Myung. He stared at the unconscious man and let out a deep sigh.

“...Damn bastard.”

His voice slowly reverberated through the vast chamber.

Chapter 1341: I Will Go To Rescue Them (1)

A night deep with darkness. Gentle moonlight still settled on the window of the pavilion where a light remained lit.

Sarak. Sarak.

The sound of an ink-soaked brush sweeping across paper drifted through the window crack. The long-continuing brush sound was halted by a voice coming from outside the door.

“Family Lord.”

“Come in.”

Tang Gunak set down the brush in his hand and lifted his head. Soon the door opened and Dang Ik, the Tang Family’s guardian, cautiously entered.

“You have not gone to your bedchamber yet?”

“It’s still early.”

Tang Gunak shook his head with a blunt expression.

“Well then, what is it?”

“The poisons and covert weapons sent from the main household arrived just a moment ago.”

“That’s fortunate. And the reports that came with them?”

“Yes, Family lord. The refugees sent to Sichuan have all settled in well, they say. I considered reporting it tomorrow but...”

Tang Gunak slowly nodded.

“There aren’t many hands left in the clan; they must have had a hard time.”

Those who remain in the Tang Family are not particularly strong in martial prowess. Of course, within the Tang Family, making poisons and crafting covert arms is no less important than martial skill, so it’s not as if they have been idle.

“What if those left comfortably in Sichuan don’t even do that?”

Tang Gunak let out a soft laugh at Dang Ik’s words.

In the past he would never have laughed at such a joke, but the current Tang Gunak had become noticeably gentler than before.

But Tang Euk did not miss the faint bitterness that colored that smile.

“...Is there still no news from those who went towards the Southern Island?”

“It’s not a place where news can be easily sent. Especially not in the current situation.”

“That may be so, but...”

Dang Euk trailed off and sighed.

Tang Pae, the Tang Family’s Young lord, also accompanied this trip to Southern Island.

As the Tang Family’s guardian, Tang Euk approved of Tang Pae’s trip to the Southern Island. In any case, it was a mission where the major figures from the sects listed under the Heavenly Friends Alliance would all travel together; it was unthinkable for the Tang Family to be absent.

“Do not worry too much, Family lord. The Young lord will do well.”

“Worry...”

“...Yes?”

Tang Gunak gave a short, dry laugh.

“That praise is too much for me right now.”

He rose from his seat after leaving that enigmatic remark.

“Family Lord?”

“You should rest as well. You will have to train again from dawn tomorrow.”

Then he walked out the door. Tang Euk, watching his retreating back, let out a long sigh.

The chirping of insects echoed here and there.

Tang Gunak felt the same peculiar sensation he always did.

Insects typically avoid people. If someone is nearby, they usually quiet down and stop calling.

But where that person is, the insects do not stop calling. It’s not as if that person is hiding his presence.

It is something martial skill cannot explain, perhaps Tang Gunak would never understand it in his lifetime.

Hyun Jong. He stood by the riverbank, gazing toward the distant Southern land.

Tang Gunak, silently watching his back, soon shook his head.

He had sent his son and daughter to enemy territory. But even those worries and concerns could not be said to be greater than that person’s.

For that person had, in effect, sent all that he possessed into enemy land.

“Hm?”

Hyun Jong turned his head, having noticed the insect noise stop. When their eyes met, Tang Gunak bowed his head lightly. Hyun Jong returned the gesture with a slight salute.

“The night is late, Alliance Leader.”

Hyun Jong smiled faintly.

“I simply cannot sleep easily, Family lord.”

Tang Gunak exhaled a quiet sigh. He didn’t know how to answer.

The two stood side by side on the riverbank and looked across. The river, which could be crossed at will if one chose, now seemed endlessly distant and wide.

Only the faint insect calls and the sound of waves rolling into the river resumed.

Just as Tang Gunak opened his mouth to raise a topic, Hyun Jong spoke first.

“Family lord... have you ever regretted taking the position of the Family Lord?”

Tang Gunak’s expression hardened slightly. Depending on how one saw it, it was a delicate question that could be rather rude.

But the question was permitted because it was Hyun Jong asking. Not because Hyun Jong had the right to be disrespectful to Tang Gunak, but because there was no malice in his words.

“Regret...”

Tang Gunak quietly looked at Hyun Jong, pondering how to answer that question.

In truth, there was no meaning in him answering this question. It wasn’t really a question to begin with. So instead he asked back.

“Do you regret becoming the Sect leader of Mount Hua?”

Hyun Jong smiled faintly.

“I used to, quite often.”

“...”

“But not now. I am a lacking man, but becoming the Sect leader of Mount Hua has been the most rewarding thing in my life, I think.”

“Then...”

Hyun Jong’s gaze turned to the river cradling the moon.

“However, from time to time I think: what kind of person was I?”

Tang Gunak frowned slightly.

“What do you mean by that...?”

“You feel the same, do you not, Family Lord? Of course anyone might feel this in life, but especially living as a Sect leader, there are many times one must hide one’s true heart.”

Tang Gunak nodded in agreement.

“It is a position heavy with responsibility.”

He felt the same. Before meeting Chung Myung, Tang Gunak had been under excessive scrutiny from the Elders’ Council and had no choice but to become colder than his nature, because he could not show any weakness to them.

He had regained some composure now, but back then nothing had been easy.

“At first I intended merely to do what I could with my abilities.”

His voice was calm. But it didn’t sound purely peaceful because of the deep, dark gaze that had settled in Hyun Jong’s eyes.

“But it didn’t turn out that way. I am not that grand a person, yet people kept wanting me to be a great man. They said that the Sect leader of Mount Hua ought to be like that.”

Tang Gunak slowly nodded. Anyone who had assumed a position of responsibility would sympathize.

“So I pretended. Pretending to be magnanimous when I was not, pretending to be kind when I was not, pretending to know some Tao when I did not...”

“...”

“I played the image of the ideal Sect leader I had imagined for decades. At first it was hard, but at some point it became a bit easier. Then at another point I grew accustomed to it.”

Tang Gunak closed his eyes.

He seemed to understand. Thoughts govern actions. But sometimes actions govern thoughts. Just as changing thought changes action, changing action can eventually alter thought.

“Now it all became natural. I thought like the Sect leader I wanted to be, acted to appear that way, and thought I had grown somewhat closer to being a man worthy of Mount Hua’s Sect leader.”

Hyun Jong smiled faintly. He turned to Tang Gunak and gave him a gentle look.

“Yet at some point this thought came: what kind of person was I originally? Now I hardly know.”

“Alliance Leader...”

“I think I have become a better person. I have become someone less ashamed to bear the name Sect leader of Mount Hua. But... I sometimes wonder: was that truly a good thing?”

There was an indescribable emotion in Hyun Jong’s voice.

“Was it really only a good thing...?”

Tang Gunak said nothing.

Though it was hard to add anything, he could deeply understand it. He could empathize. That was why his chest ached so.

A brief silence settled. Hyun Jong spoke again.

“The children have grown splendidly. They have become people who can no longer even be compared to me.”

“They are still far from it. How could those brats be compared to you, Alliance Leader?”

Hyun Jong suppressed a bitter smile at Tang Gunak’s words.

“But I also think: did that growth truly make those children happy?”

“...”

“As they bear more responsibilities, are they not losing more things? Just as I once did, are those children not desperately suppressing themselves, striving to become what someone else wants them to be?”

A small sigh escaped Tang Gunak.

“Isn’t that how people live, for the most part?”

“Yes. I suppose so...”

Even knowing that, the regret didn’t vanish, because he realized he too might have been imposing the life he had lived onto those children. Hyun Jong said.

“I think I know one thing.”

“...What is that?”

“What I had to do.”

Tang Gunak stared at Hyun Jong with questioning eyes. But it was not easy to discern his inner thoughts from Hyun Jong’s calm, still-surface expression.

Then, just as Tang Gunak was about to say something, he suddenly turned his gaze sharply.

“Something...”

Something was approaching at high speed from the river. Narrowing his eyes and watching for a moment, the object slicing the surface became clearer. It was white... somehow.

“A weasel?”

Tang Gunak’s eyes widened.

It was surely the weasel that Chung Myung used to carry around. Of course, one cannot easily identify a weasel by appearance alone, but common sense dictated that there could be no other white weasel swimming across the Yangtze river at this hour.

Kiiiiiiii!

Baek Ah, having spotted Tang Gunak and Hyun Jong, let out a sharp cry and increased her speed to nearly fly through the water as it swam.

Tadadad!

Arriving ashore, Baek Ah planted her short paws firmly on the ground in an instant. Without bothering to shake off the water, she immediately climbed onto Hyun Jong’s body and untied the bundle tied to its neck, offering it out.

Kik! Kick!

Ragged breaths and coughs burst out in succession. The little body trembled, making it clear how urgently it had rushed here.

But Baek Ah, as if unconcerned, offered the bundle again, as if urging them to check it immediately.

Hyun Jong untied the bundle Baek Ah offered and took out the letter inside. The corner was a little damp, but it did not prevent reading the contents.

Hyun Jong read it without changing his expression, then calmly handed the letter to Tang Gunak.

“Is this a letter sent by the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint?”

Tang Gunak snatched the letter and read it without waiting for an answer. But unlike Hyun Jong, his eyes trembled greatly while reading.

The letter’s contents were beyond absurd; they were chilling. It briefly contained a declaration to break through the Southern lands together with Southern Island Sect.

It meant Southern land, the stronghold of the Four Sects Alliance.

“W-what is this...”

For a moment a startled Tang Gunak looked at Hyun Jong. But Hyun Jong’s expression did not change. He merely continued to gaze across the river as before.

“Mustn’t we find some way?”

No matter that the place is Gangnam, they could not just fold their hands and watch. What if they all died like this...?

Tang Gunak bit his lip.

But... no.

Invading the Southern Lands now would mean declaring war. No matter how important they were, they could not throw the entire Heavenly Friends Alliance into the blaze of war for the sake of a few lives...

“Family lord.”

“...Yes?”

Hyun Jong asked with an expressionless face.

“Are you not curious what I had to do?”

Tang Gunak frowned at the oddly timed question. Hyun Jong sent him a calm look and spoke.

“I issue an order as Alliance leader. Summon the leaders of all sects affiliated with the Heavenly Friends Alliance to the Alliance Hall immediately.”

Aghast, Tang Gunak widened his eyes.

“W-what do you intend to do...”

Hyun Jong, Sect leader of Mount Hua, looked across the river again, his eyes colder than before.

A steady voice spread out.

“I will go to rescue the children.”

At that moment Tang Gunak realized: the insect calls had stopped some time ago.

Chapter 1342: I Will Go To Rescue Them (2)

Seo Mun-Yu, the finance officer of the Four Sects Alliance’s military, stood before the dauntingly high white steps.

He slowly adjusted his attire, tightening his waist cord and hitching up the drooping hem of his trousers.

“Phew...”

Only after preparing did he slowly begin to climb the steps.

Ttak. Ttak.

The sound of his footsteps on the marble echoed down to the vast plaza below.

Some say the marble staircase simply reveals Jang Ilso’s peculiar tastes: an unnecessary stairway, unnecessary splendor, exactly the ostentatious image of Jang Ilso that everyone knows.

‘They know nothing.’

Anyone who has climbed these steps would never utter such a thing; only those who understand the tension felt when approaching Jang Ilso up these steps would know.

Once one ascends the pure white path, a great hall—too vast to be called merely magnificent—greets the climber.

How could those who only look up from below ever comprehend that overwhelming presence?

“Huuu...”

Another deep sigh escaped him. Whether from the blazing sun or the tension, beads of sweat formed on his forehead.

“I have come to report to the Alliance lord.”

The Red Dogs guarding the hall glared at Seo Mun-Yu with gleaming eyes. These hunting dogs bared their fangs at friend and foe alike at Jang Ilso’s command; even those of the Ten Thousand People Clan found facing them difficult.

The Red Dog’s gaze scanned Seo Mun-Yu from head to toe.

He was nothing but a civilian clerk and could not harm Jang Ilso even if he tried, yet the hounds did not relax in front of him.

“Enter.”

Only after the red hounds opened the doors could he finally enter the hall; a faint incense from an incense burner greeted his nose.

The hall was less a place of business than a venue for audiences. In the barren center sat a large jade throne, and on it Jang Ilso reclined obliquely in a red ceremonial robe embroidered with ornate golden dragons.

This hall was by no means large.

Seo Mun-Yu felt that anew.

The hall that had seemed desolate and vast suddenly felt cramped the moment he saw Jang Ilso; if it had been any smaller, Jang Ilso’s presence would have smothered him.

Though he had escorted Jang Ilso for years, bowing in this way never grew familiar no matter how many times he did it.

Swallowing his dry saliva, Seo Mun-Yu stepped forward cautiously.

It would be less nerve-wracking to crawl forward on his belly, but Jang Ilso did not allow such deferential postures within his sight. So he had no choice but to face him head-on.

“...Alliance lord.”

Jang Ilso, slouched on the throne like a bed, slowly opened his eyes.

When his pale, languid eyes revealed themselves, Seo Mun-Yu shivered as if something had pierced straight through him.

“I-I have a report... the ledger for supply distribution.”

Seo Mun-Yu carefully extended the ledger in his hands.

He resented Ho Ga-Myung’s absence in moments like this. The ledger should have been checked by Ho Ga-Myung without it being presented to Jang Ilso, but because Ho Ga-Myung was away, it had to be brought directly to him.

Jang Ilso’s eyes slowly moved to the ledger in Seo Mun-Yu’s hands. Staring at it, he lifted a hand languidly.

At that, the server, who had been waiting, cautiously brought the large seal from the back. The seal symbolizing the Alliance lord of the Four Sects Alliance was wrapped in a golden dragon carving—more ornate than even an emperor’s seal, it seemed.

“I present it.”

The server held the seal before Jang Ilso’s hand. Jang Ilso nonchalantly took it and tossed it forward toward Seo Mun-Yu.

Clang! Thud!

The precious emblem of the Four Sects Alliance clattered onto the hall floor like a worthless stone. Cold sweat broke out along Seo Mun-Yu’s back as he stared at the fallen seal.

Could he dare to pick that up with his hands? Wasn’t touching it itself an act of sacrilege that could cost him his head?
