But Jang Ilso seemed not to care in the slightest and turned over indifferently.

“Stamp it properly and go.”

“Y-Yes...? Yes, Alliance lord!”

Seo Mun-Yu nodded vigorously.

With trembling hands he opened the ledger and carefully picked up the seal from the floor, as if afraid the golden seal might crumble.

After repeatedly wetting his lashes and wiping the sweat from his eyes with his sleeve, he forced himself to stamp the seal.

It’s over. Now he could finally leave this place.

An involuntary, brief sigh of relief escaped him—truly only a moment.

But after a moment, when he raised his head, Seo Mun-Yu saw that Jang Ilso, who had been turned away, had already returned his gaze to him.

A cold chill poured down Seo Mun-Yu’s spine in an instant.

“Hmm.”

The corner of Jang Ilso’s lips, painted blood-red, lifted in a faint smile. With an unreadable expression, he stared intently at Seo Mun-Yu and spoke slowly.

“Come here.”

“...Yes?”

“Bring that here.”

Seo Mun-Yu’s face turned ashen. Jang Ilso smiled a little softer and said,

“Hurry.”

In the end Seo Mun-Yu approached as if bewitched by the man. He cautiously extended the ledger he held; Jang Ilso took it with slow fingers and skimmed it.

“Hmm.”

A faint, oddly amused sound escaped him.

After a moment, with a soft thud he closed the ledger and looked up at Seo Mun-Yu with a smile.

“You don’t understand, do you.”

“...”

“This sort of thing simply doesn’t suit me; even if I look at it, it doesn’t sink into my head. Do you understand what I mean?”

Seo Mun-Yu nodded repeatedly.

“I wonder how Ga-Myung does these things. People of the clerk breed are impossible to fathom.”

His tone was playful and light.

But Seo Mun-Yu shrank even further; Jang Ilso’s eyes fixed on him showed no amusement at all.

“Tell me.”

“W-What should I say...?”

“Is this ledger accurate?”

“I-it is, Alliance lord!”

Seo Mun-Yu, pale as a sheet, began to babble hastily.

Even answering things Jang Ilso hadn’t asked.

“H-How could there be lies in a report presented to the Alliance lord? Not a single number or particle in the ledger is wrong! I’ve verified one by one that the figures in the ledger match the items currently within the Alliance!”

“Hmm?”

“There has been not the slightest negligence! Please believe me.”

Seo Mun-Yu bowed his head almost as if driven into the ground. Seeing this, Jang Ilso let out a strange smile.

“It matches exactly...”

His once-sharp eyes grew languid again. He nodded slowly, appearing bored by everything.

“Then that’s fortunate.”

Seo Mun-Yu’s body sagged as if all strength left it. His clothes were damp with sweat.

“Ga-Myung being absent must have made things hard for you.”

“I am doing my utmost to fill the commander’s position, even if feebly.”

“Good answer. But that alone is not enough.”

“...Yes?”

Jang Ilso smiled faintly.

“What Ga-Myung does isn’t merely balancing ledgers. Those who come into direct contact with me should know better what kind of person I am.”

Seo Mun-Yu looked at Jang Ilso with baffled eyes. Jang Ilso’s gaze drew a soft curve.

“Do you understand what I’m getting at?”

“I-I don’t really...”

“Oh my, oh my. You mustn’t be this ignorant.”

Jang Ilso reached out. His thin, pale fingertips and long nails touched Seo Mun-Yu’s forehead and slowly traced downward, skimming the eyebrow and down to the cheek.

“Remember this: I do not dislike those who try to deceive me.”

“A-Alliance lord. I...”

“What I dislike is...”

A sharp sting.

A sharp pain flared where Jang Ilso’s nail touched his cheek; the nail had pierced his skin. Yet Seo Mun-Yu could not even blink.

“Those who cannot deceive me.”

Jang Ilso’s smile widened grotesquely bright. It was a truly uncanny laugh.

Snap.

The nail sank deeper and deeper. The sensation made Seo Mun-Yu’s mind reel—perhaps not from the pain but from the terror of what he might suffer next.

“Tell me.”

Jang Ilso’s gaze was utter chaos.

Something indecipherable churned relentlessly, never still for a moment.

Merely standing before those eyes tightened his chest.

“What Ga-Myung is up to...”

Jang Ilso’s blood-red lips parted slightly, revealing white teeth.

“What on earth that guy intends by even deceiving me.”

All Seo Mun-Yu could do was nod frantically.

After a moment.

Jang Ilso leaned back on the throne with his chin propped and tapped his temple with his fingertips as if amused.

“To Southern Island?”

“Y-Yes!”

Seo Mun-Yu bowed so low his head nearly touched the floor.

He knew Jang Ilso disliked formal etiquette, but he had no energy to dwell on that; he could only bow desperately and repeatedly.

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso emitted a faint hum and then fell silent for a while.

Seo Mun-Yu dared not guess what was passing through his mind; he couldn’t even attempt it.

“Ha... hahahah.”

A small laugh finally escaped Jang Ilso—part incredulous, part strangely delighted.

“...So this is the scheme you’ve set in motion.”

His voice was noticeably more animated than before.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, is it.”

Seo Mun-Yu swallowed dryly; his throat, parched from tension, felt as if it would tear.

Before Jang Ilso, one had no choice but to reveal everything—everything he knew.

But this was not strictly his fault; after all, had not the time promised by Ho Ga-Myung not yet passed?

And it was not a time to heed Ho Ga-Myung’s instructions. No matter Ho Ga-Myung’s requests and Seo Mun-Yu’s duty to follow the commander’s orders, the fact remained that Ho Ga-Myung had deceived Jang Ilso.

If Ho Ga-Myung himself had delivered this report, Seo Mun-Yu might have avoided wrath. But not now. If Jang Ilso became angry, all that fury would fall squarely on Seo Mun-Yu.

Seo Mun-Yu’s every nerve was fixated on Jang Ilso; he didn’t even notice the old wound on his cheek reopening and bleeding.

But Jang Ilso, as if sinking deep into closed eyes, remained silent for a long time.

How much time had passed like that?

After what felt like an eternity to Seo Mun-Yu, Jang Ilso opened his eyes.

“Ga-Myung...”

His pale pupils gleamed with a strange light.

“You’ve undertaken quite an audacious affair. Amusing, really.”

Surprisingly, there was no anger toward Ho Ga-Myung in his voice—only a peculiar heat, like a child who had found an entertaining toy.

Jang Ilso slowly rose to his feet.

His blood-red robe trailed long across the floor.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint... Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

Gazing past the prostrate Seo Mun-Yu to some distant place, Jang Ilso laughed softly—a twisted laugh.

“If you’re willing to come out this far, I simply can’t help but welcome you. Right?”

Jang Ilso took a step. Seo Mun-Yu cowered and bowed, but Jang Ilso, as if Seo Mun-Yu meant nothing, strode past him toward the door.

The earlier languor and boredom were gone; each of his steps brimmed with a conqueror’s ferocity.

Bang!

With both hands he flung the doors open, and the waiting red hounds bowed in perfect unison.

Jang Ilso gave an order.

“Summon the Captains. And every Red Dog!”

“Yes, Alliance lord!”

The Red Dogs hurried out. Paying no heed to those who had already departed, Jang Ilso stood at the top of the stairs. Beyond the grand Four Sects Alliance headquarters, the vast land spread out before him.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

A chilling curve formed in Jang Ilso’s eyes.

“I’ll indulge your petulance only to this point.”

A world that looked endlessly peaceful.

But Jang Ilso saw clearly.

He saw the sight of flame, red like blood, rising in the far south and spreading to engulf the world.

Once those flames were kindled, they would not die down until they had consumed the world and reduced it to ashes.

Never.

Chapter 1343: I Will Go To Rescue Them (3)

Chung Myung, who opened his eyes, looks at his chest.

A stark white bandage was wrapped tight around his chest. Chung Myung, staring silently at the wound, slowly pushed himself up.

A sharp ache.

His face twisted at the intense chest pain. As if the barely healed wound had shifted and gaped again, red blood began to spread across the white bandage.

No sooner had he uttered a shallow breath than there was a loud bang and someone stormed into the room.

“Damn it! How many times is this now, you crazy man?”

“...”

“How many times do I have to tell you, if you wake up and something’s off, just lie still and rest! How many times! Are you a goldfish or what? Did you get hit so many times that your memory’s gone?”

Chung Myung turned his head to the person who had entered. The man’s handsome face was twisted with irritation. Watching that expression, Chung Myung spoke.

“...How long was I unconscious?”

“Three days, three days! This time I thought you were gone for good. Hell, that life of yours is stubborn as hell.”

“Three days...”

Chung Myung snorted a laugh in disbelief.

Usually he’d wake in a day at most, but three days, he must have had one foot in the afterlife.

Well, thinking about it, it made sense.

He hadn’t been fighting alone; facing the high priest while protecting the others was never easy. It was fortunate he had at least crawled back with his life.

“Alcohol.”

“...Has that man lost his mind? Do you even know what being a ‘patient’ means? Even three-year-olds in my village know; don’t they teach that in Shaanxi?”

“No more talk, bring the alcohol.”

“There’s nothing left to drink yourself to death with.”

“Bring it.”

“Ugh, damn!”

A man wearing the green robes that mark the Sichuan Tang Family stormed out. A moment later he returned with a white bottle and hurled it at Chung Myung, snapping angrily.

“Drink and die if you must!”

Thud.

Chung Myung snatched the bottle, opened it, and drank straight from it. The harsh spirit washed into his mouth and slightly quelled the metallic smell of blood rising from his gut.

Of course he knew, no amount of alcohol would make that smell vanish.

The blood smell wasn’t coming from the wound in the first place.

The drink only numbingly distracted him for a moment from the sickening, clinging smell that had soaked into him.

Removing the bottle from his lips, Chung Myung stared blankly at the ceiling. After a long silence he spoke.

“How many made it back?”

“...About forty.”

“How many died?”

“...”

“I asked how many died.”

“About twenty.”

Chung Myung nodded without a word.

It was absurd. Men who’d screamed and fought could reach safety and then drop dead. If you have the strength to swing a sword, you should have the strength to live.

Hearing that twenty of the forty he’d saved were dead left him hollow enough to laugh.

“What about the rest?”

“Now’s not the time to worry about them. You saved them, so they’ll manage. Whether arms are severed or tendons cut, at least they’re alive, what’s the problem?”

“...”

“Worry about yourself, worry about yourself. Or did some ghost from a past life who couldn’t fight latch on to you? How many times have I told you not to stake your life on those bastards?”

“...So much nagging.”

“At least fight while I’m there, didn’t I say that! How many times do you get stabbed in the back fighting those who can’t even guard one another? Taoist’s life is, it’s not dozens or hundreds...”

“Shut up. You’re loud.”

Tang Bo’s face contorted.

Chung Myung drank again. He winced at the burning down his throat, then gave a bitter smile.

“I’ve never gambled with my life.”

“...”

“It just happened while I was fighting.”

Tang Bo, staring at him with a fixed expression, sighed.

“You’ll actually die that way.”

“Is that such a big deal?”

Chung Myung’s gaze shifted to the window beside the bed. The world outside was impossibly blue, and impossibly desolate.

“Hundreds, thousands die each day, what difference would adding my life make?”

“So you’re trying to die sooner?”

Chung Myung looked at Tang Bo blankly. Tang Bo didn’t shrink back; instead he raised his voice angrily.

“Why risk your life for people you don’t even get along with! You always complain those damned bastards give you stomach-aches, what, are you two-faced?”

Chung Myung gave a small laugh at that; there was no real argument to give.

“Because blood rushes to my head.”

“...”

“That won’t happen anymore.”

“Yeah, right, sure.”

Tang Bo let out a long, sinking sigh.

Even after knowing him so long, he sometimes couldn’t understand this Taoist.

He wasn’t particularly virtuous, nor was he someone chasing lofty Taoist ideals.

Objectively, he was the kind of Taoist they wondered how on earth ever joined the sect.

So why did he always make those choices at crucial moments?

“Taoist.”

“What?”

“I’m not joking, you’ll actually die that way.”

“...”

“You know it, if even you go down, we can’t win this war. No matter how many others die, you have to stay alive somehow.”

“I know.”

“Hyung.”

“I said I know.”

Tang Bo, hesitating, looked steadily into Chung Myung’s eyes and said.

“Not all those in the Demonic Cult are the same.”

“What do you mean?”

“Not every Demonic Cult member you behead is the one who killed Mount Hua’s Taoists. And besides, killing them won’t bring the dead back...”

“Shut your mouth.”

In that moment Chung Myung’s gaze changed. In the chill of his eyes, Tang Bo could no longer press him.

Tang Bo sighed again, full of complaint.

“You should keep your mouth shut.”

He pulled a medicinal paste from his robe and tossed it at Chung Myung, who caught it reflexively.

“Apply it to the wound. I roughly stitched you up, so if you treat it with the paste it shouldn’t let it reopen. Don’t do anything foolish today, lie still.”

Tang Bo turned and started to leave, but abruptly stopped and, without looking back, said:

“Those, the men you rescued.”

“...What about them?”

“They wanted me to tell you they’re grateful.”

“...”

Thunk.

Tang Bo closed the door and left.

Lying back down, Chung Myung stared at the ceiling. As feeling slowly returned to his body, the nicks and bruises ached. But he was used to that pain now.

‘Has he lost his mind?’

Chung Myung laughed aloud.

Wasn’t that the most obvious thing to say?

In this crazy world, there’s no one left completely sane. Everyone is clutching at a half-lost mind.

Killing them won’t bring the dead back...“

He knew. He knew, and that’s why he acted this way. The dead do not come back.

No matter the regret or struggle, those who die once never return.

He knew all too well how cruel that was.

❀ ❀ ❀

He felt as if he were sinking ever deeper. In that strange but familiar sensation, Chung Myung opened his eyes.

A dark ceiling, the smell of blood, the pain sweeping through his body, an intense déjà vu washed over him.

Pushing up his leaden eyelids, Chung Myung raised his head to look at his body.

A bandage was wrapped around his chest.

It was familiar yet unnerving, the body beneath the bandage looked smaller and frailer than the body he once knew.

Chung Myung’s gaze slowly turned to the side.

Someone slept propped against the unfamiliar cave wall.

The sleeper’s face shifted from one he recognized into a stranger’s.

Staring dazedly at Baek Cheon’s face, neatly resting even in sleep, Chung Myung lifted his gaze again.

‘...Right.’

He was no longer the Plum Blossom Sword Saint.

The Mount Hua he knew was gone.

He had won the war, but lost everything in return.

All he had tried to protect was gone, and all he hoped to leave behind had vanished.

What remained here was only...

Ssshk. Ssshk.

As he drifted back, a small breath reached his ear. Turning his head, he saw Yu Yiseol lying nearby and Tang Soso sleeping on the other side.

They looked as if they’d fallen asleep after exhausting themselves checking on him.

For a long time Chung Myung quietly stared at Tang Soso’s face, as if trying to find traces of the past there.

But it didn’t work.

Tang Bo’s face from his earlier dream felt distant. For some time now, the man’s features often failed to come clearly to his mind.

Sometimes brutally vivid, then fading in an instant, growing dull, bit by bit.

“You’re awake?”

No answer was really necessary; it wasn’t a question that required one.

What needed saying was no different than before.

“...How long has it been?”

“Not exactly sure, but probably about two days.”

“Two days...”

Better than three?

Chung Myung smiled and tried to sit up, but Baek Cheon pressed down hard on his forehead.

“Don’t let the barely-healed wound burst open, lie still and behave.”

“It’s healed.”

Clench.

Baek Cheon silently drew his sword. Chung Myung finally relaxed his neck. Baek Cheon might not be stronger than Tang Bo, but he had already proven himself to be even less sensible.

Keeping his head on the ground, Chung Myung rolled his eyes and spoke.

“What about the others?”

“Gul and Yiseol were badly hurt, but still they are better than you. Don’t worry about others, look after yourself.”

“Southern Island?”

“...You really don’t understand, do you?”

“I asked how the Southern Island is.”

At the persistent question, Baek Cheon sighed deeply.

“Half.”

“...The elders?”

“The same.”

That meant about half dead, counting those who never made it here and those who arrived only to die.

Judging by Baek Cheon’s expression, the real death toll was likely far above half, but either way it was grim.

If that was the cost of breaking through Guangdong, the Ten Thousand People Clan’s stronghold, then those losses weren’t a complete disaster. If anything, it could be called a success.

Of course, no one would put it that way.

Chung Myung pushed himself up again.

“I told you to lie down.”

“I’m really fine now.”

Chung Myung pushed Baek Cheon’s hand off his head and sat up. Baek Cheon tried to stop him but finally shook his head.

“I was the one who put the rear in your hands.”

At that unexpected remark, Chung Myung stared at Baek Cheon.

“So I know I don’t have the right to tell you this.”

“...What are you talking about all of a sudden?”

“But I have to ask, why push yourself that far?”

“...”

“You could have pulled back. Even if more people died because of that choice, no one would have blamed you. You know that, so why force it? Why?”

Baek Cheon’s lips trembled. He appeared calm, but clearly wasn’t.

Watching him, Chung Myung suddenly turned his head toward the still-unconscious Yu Yiseol.

“It wasn’t forcing things.”

“...What?”

“I just believed.”

At least fight while I’m there, didn’t I say that!

“When I fall past my limits, I believed there would always be someone behind me, that’s all.”

Only now, finally, had there been people he could lean his back against.

Chapter 1344: I Will Go To Rescue Them (4)

Ho Ga-Myung glared fiercely at the adjutant standing before him with a gaunt, haggard face.

“The entrance?”

“Not yet…”

The lieutenant’s answering voice trembled.

It was the first time anyone had seen Ho Ga-Myung so disheveled. Therefore, it was impossible not to know how much wrath he was holding back.

The sword at Ho Ga-Myung’s waist seemed as if it might leap out and slit the lieutenant’s throat at any moment.

But what reached out was not a sword but a low, short command, sharper than a blade, more chilling than a dagger.

“Find them.”

“Yes!”

“By any means necessary.”

The lieutenant bowed deeply.

But Ho Ga-Myung had already averted his eyes from him and was staring at the razor-sharp peaks of the Ten Thousand Great Mountains.

Grit.

His tightly clenched lips split and red blood trickled out.

‘Mount Hua’s Sword Saint…’

He had been toyed with by Mount Hua’s Sword Saint from beginning to end. What tormented him most was that, no matter how he thought about it, he could not find any reason for their defeat.

He couldn’t understand how such a thing had happened.

Is it even conceivable that newcomers to Guangdong could know and exploit secret places that even he had failed to discover?

Who could possibly have imagined such a possibility?

‘Impossible…’

It felt as if someone had plunged their hand into his head and was ransacking it.

But denying that possibility was meaningless; the impossible had already happened. No matter how hard he screamed or refused to see it, reality remained reality.

They had poured out everything they could. They had borne tremendous losses. He had even committed the unthinkable, defied Jang Ilso’s orders.

Thus the plan for which he had staked everything had vanished in an instant like foam.

If they managed to escape the Ten Thousand Great Mountains like this?

‘That cannot happen.’

Once they cross this hellish range, the vast lands of Southern Lands open up. No matter how many forces the Four Sects Alliance could muster, it’s impossible to seal off all of Southern Lands.

How could they possibly catch those they couldn’t seize even in Guangdong once they disperse over a much larger land?

“Push all available troops inside.”

“…Sorry?”

The adjutant behind him flinched and looked at Ho Ga-Myung’s back in alarm.

“Wherever they’re hiding, they can’t remain hidden forever. They’ll have to show themselves eventually. Then we catch and kill them.”

“…Troops? Into there?”

When the adjutant asked, Ho Ga-Myung slowly turned. The look in his eyes made the adjutant flinch.

“S-sorry.”

“Don’t make me say it twice.”

“Yes!”

The adjutant bowed his head deeply.

Only after Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze dropped did the adjutant manage to raise his head and look with shaking eyes at the mountain range before him.

A hellish range where one couldn’t even guess what lay just beyond.

‘From here?’

Was he being ordered to face Mount Hua’s Sword Saint? That man?

The very thought made him nauseous and dizzy. But there was no alternative; one could not oppose Ho Ga-Myung now.

So those who followed the order could do nothing but pray: may Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, that devil’s blade, not reach them.

❀ ❀ ❀

Crack. Crack.

Chung Myung tilted his head left and right and lightly opened and closed his hand.

‘He’s different.’

He was by no means the same as he had been. This battle made that clear. Yet he also had strengths different from before.

First was resilience.

The purest qi he had gathered in the land granted him a recovery ability incomparable to before.

That was likely why he could rise quicker now even from injuries more severe than in the past.

His body’s internal state was also improving rapidly.

Tension eased, blood began to flow, and sweat ran down his entire body. The turbid qi accumulated from battle was being expelled in sweat.

If he sweated it out like this, his condition would surely recover to at least seventy percent.

‘So it wasn’t all for nothing.’

He hadn’t yet reached the peak height he once had, but it proved that the summit he now chose to climb was far higher than the one his past self had ascended.

Tududuk.

Chung Myung tore off the bandage wrapped around his chest.

Baek Cheon, who had been about to yell asking what he was doing, shut his mouth. Chung Myung’s chest wound had already healed.

“…Is he even human?”

“Sometimes I doubt it too.”

Chung Myung chuckled softly. But Baek Cheon couldn’t relax his expression at the response.

“A man who can heal like that…”

“What?”

“Then if you’d been a little restrained you wouldn’t have had to go through that mess with that leg, right?”

“Huh? True.”

“What a madman, really…”

Baek Cheon ground his teeth.

He knew it too.

That Chung Myung had no time to rest. That had he not taken the front and fought without pause, the sacrifices would have been far greater.

But even knowing that, his boiling anger couldn’t be helped.

Baek Cheon sighed as if the ground could swallow him. Chung Myung then asked calmly.

“How about Jo Gul sahyung?”

“Yoon Jong is tending to him elsewhere.”

“He wasn’t that badly hurt, but these youngsters are just weak-blooded nowadays.”

“…Have you never thought your constitution is abnormal? Others would have died ten times over.”

“Tsk tsk. Weaklings.”

Chung Myung snorted and smiled despite the grumbling; Baek Cheon’s face twisted.

“You find it funny?”

“What changes if we pretend to be serious?”

“…”

“Sasuk, relax your face too.”

“You…”

“People are watching.”

Baek Cheon’s face took on a complicated expression at Chung Myung’s seemingly light remark.

“Everyone’s staring only at the sasuk’s face. Their expressions reveal exactly what situation we’re in. If the sasuk keeps scowling and making an already ugly face uglier, everyone gets more anxious.”

“Have you ever heard of dependency in your life?”

“At this point you are a peerless handsome man.”

“…Don’t even say that.”

Baek Cheon let out a deep sigh. His heart was heavy.

He wasn’t unaware of that. He knew one must not show weakness in any circumstance.

But it wasn’t as easy as saying it.

He had almost killed Chung Myung. He had nearly driven Yu Yiseol and Jo Gul to death. Because his judgment had been flawed. If only he had been a bit more…

Clack.

At that moment, Chung Myung lightly tapped Baek Cheon’s shoulder.

“…What is it?”

“I wouldn’t have been able to do that.”

“…”

“The sasuk’s judgment was correct. I couldn’t even imitate it.”

“…Don’t give me needless flattery.”

“Still, you have some sense.”

“What, you bastard?”

While spouting nonsense, Chung Myung’s gaze checked Yu Yiseol’s condition. There were deep wounds here and there, but it seemed unlikely any aftereffects would remain. Taoist cultivation skills exert great power in such cases.

But the absence of aftereffects did not erase the suffering she had endured.

This time Chung Myung’s gaze fell on Tang Soso, who lay asleep from exhaustion. The small wounds at her fingertips hinted at how fiercely she had struggled.

Those who fight with swords finish their role the moment battle ends; the healer’s fight begins then. She had probably saved countless lives and had to send away countless more.

Chung Myung’s gaze sank coldly.

Step.

He took slow steps.

Across the wide communal hall lay the Southern Island disciples scattered about. Any semblance of noble bearing was long gone; they lay like corpses in the shape of defeated soldiers.

“Understand?”

Baek Cheon, who had quietly followed him, spoke in a firm voice.

“Everyone fought to their limits. Two days of rest won’t be enough to recover. If we go out now we’ll only be driving the name to its doom.”

“Hm.”

Chung Myung nodded.

“There’s reason in that, but it’s not entirely right. By now the crazed Mind-Probing Inquisitor is probably clawing desperately to find the entrance.”

“…”

“And with his power he’ll soon find the entrance. He’s only half-blinded; that’s the only reason he hasn’t found it yet.”

Baek Cheon pressed his lips together.

Chung Myung was right. This place could not remain safe forever.

At that moment, those who noticed Chung Myung waking swarmed over.

“Taoist!”

“Are you all right?”

“Your wounds are severe, shouldn’t you rest more?”

Seeing Namgung Do-Wei, Tang Pae, and Hae Yeon rushing in, Chung Myung snorted. Each bore worry heavily on their faces.

‘I’m done for.’

Causing concern to such greenhouse plants.

“What about the Green Forest King?”

“Here.”

Im So-Byeong trudged up from the rear.

“They say even the King of Salt hesitates to take ill-natured people, and seeing you, taoist, that doesn’t seem like nonsense.”

Chung Myung laughed at the crooked remark.

“You got beaten by Ho Ga-Myung and now you are taking it out on me.”

“W-who are you saying got beaten?”

“Or shall I say you were trounced?”

“Ugh…”

Im So-Byeong clenched his fist. Even if he pretended otherwise, his pride was clearly hurt. After trembling a moment, he took a deep breath and asked.

“What do you intend to do now?”

“What?”

“Given the situation, as you know we can’t stay holed up here forever—we have to get out. But everyone’s condition is like this…”

At those words everyone’s faces darkened.

They had barely caught their breath, but no one could pretend the situation was easy.

“What are we going to do?”

“…Yes.”

“You should ask the others, not me.”

“Yes?”

Im So-Byeong looked at Chung Myung as if to ask what he meant.

“Because the positions have changed.”

“…What do you mean by that?”

Chung Myung surveyed those standing before him.

Not as bad as Yu Yiseol, but their injuries were no joke. The marks of their brutal struggle remained on all their bodies.

“What about Jo Gul sahyung?”

“Over here…”

Namgung Do-Wei guided him. Chung Myung followed and silently stared at Jo Gul, who still hadn’t regained consciousness, as if memorizing every scar on his body.

A moment later, Chung Myung turned back to Im So-Byeong.

Im So-Byeong’s shoulders involuntarily twitched.

Chung Myung’s gaze was so cold it made him doubt if this was the same person he’d known.

No, it wasn’t just the chill. There was an uncanny dissonance, as if looking at the same person but seeing someone else.

“Sword.”

“…”

Baek Cheon handed over Chung Myung’s sword that he had been holding.

It was the sword he had kept to prevent Chung Myung from rampaging. He had intended never to hand it over until Chung Myung recovered.

Yet somehow he could not bring himself to disobey Chung Myung’s words now.

“Green Forest King.”

“Yes, Taoist.”

“Prepare while you buy me some time. We’ll break out of here in one go.”

Im So-Byeong nodded.

He was a strategist too. He knew that if they could only cross the Ten Thousand Great Mountains, they could move to the Yangtze River in one swift sweep.

Ho Ga-Myung had rallied all those he had to press tightly and formed a last-ditch stand in Guangdong. Now they would have to pay the price.

The problem was that with the current state of the Heavenly Friends Alliance and Southern Island Sect, even fully exploiting the terrain of the Ten Thousand Great Mountains would not make battle easy.

Furthermore, in gaining time to heal they had also given the enemy time to encircle them again.

Chung Myung knew this as well.

And Chung Myung’s words now implied he could resolve this situation himself.

“What are you planning to do…?”

Im So-Byeong asked cautiously. Chung Myung let slip a crazed smile.

“I’ll collect the debt.”

His twisted lips revealed white teeth in a chilling grin.

“They will learn what it means to set foot in this place.”

What he had once painfully learned, now it was time to make them understand.

Chapter 1345: I Will Go To Rescue Them (5)

On a moonless night near the new moon, the Ten Thousand Great Mountains were plunged into darkness so deep that one could hardly see an arm’s length ahead.

Within that rugged range, bright, ghostly lights shone like will-o’-wisps.

Step.

Ten Thousand People Clan swordsmen, blades in hand, scanned their surroundings with murderous, hostile eyes.

Rustle.

“Who goes there!”

No sooner had a faint sound come from the side than a Ten Thousand People Clan sword was swung without hesitation. The sword rushed toward the sound and struck down.

Crack!

The man who had thrown the sword rushed forward at once, but all he found was a rabbit twitching with its neck severed. His face contorted.

“…Damn.”

“Tsk tsk. What a great hunter you’ve become.”

The man snapped back sharply at the mocking voice behind him.

“Watch your mouth.”

“Don’t waste your strength for nothing, keep your eyes on the surroundings.”

He kicked aside the still-warm rabbit carcass in a fit of irritation, sheathed his sword at his hip, and stared at a mountain protruding from the darkness like a blade. The peaks stabbed the sky, truly a sight that made one call it a land defying heaven.

‘It is so ominous.’

The man scanned the area with anxious eyes.

A thick darkness had fallen and the silence was absolute. It might seem peaceful to some, but to those here it felt only eerie.

They were cautious even to make a sound. All the more so for those who knew the past that had unfolded on this land.

The man took a step with an irritated face.

“Ugh.”

A blunt pain suddenly shot through his foot. Looking down, a spear blade jutted up under his foot. It was so old it had rusted nearly to decay.

If one brushed away the soil around that blade, its owner’s bones would probably lie buried there.

“Damn.”

The spear’s shape resembled those used by the Evil Sect rather than the Justice faction. The man frowned slightly. He wasn’t about to feel pity for some unknown Evil Sect member who died a hundred years ago, but the thought still made him feel dirty.

“Are you hurt?”

“No. As if I’d be cut by some rusty old spear like this.”

Snap.

He stepped on the protruding spear to break it, then kicked aside the nearby underbrush.

As expected, swords typically used by the Justice faction lay scattered nearby.

If this had been an ordinary battlefield, locals would have long since picked them up, sold them, or melted them down for farming tools.

But this was the Ten Thousand Great Mountains, a land of death even common folk unconnected to the martial world avoided.

Thus, the traces of past wars remained undisturbed.

“Makes me sick.”

“Stop the pointless thoughts and watch around you. If you lose them, we’ll lose our heads.”

The man glared at the speaker, spat on the ground, and resumed walking.

“Damn it, can’t even rest.”

“Don’t even say that. Can’t you feel the atmosphere around us?”

“Damn it.”

He muttered another curse, hardly an incorrect sentiment.

“How many did they say died?”

“Damn, who’s counting… The Blood Sword Troops was wiped out, and countless others died. If you include the small fry who stood in their way, you could pile the corpses into a mountain.”

“…Serves them right.”

For so few, only around two hundred, to require such loss was surely unprecedented in Ten Thousand People Clan history. After paying that price and yet failing to annihilate the enemy, it’s no wonder the steady-faced soldiers had lost their composure.

If they let them slip away in this situation, it wouldn’t end with one or two lives lost. They had to find the enemy’s trail by any means.

“Maybe they’ve already left the range?”

“Don’t be ridiculous. At best they’re holed up in a cave. I’ve never heard of a cave hundreds of li long in my life.”

“Right?”

That meant the enemy was crouched somewhere around here.

Perhaps they were even beneath his feet, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, who had annihilated Gwi Tang and the Blood Sword Troops.

“…So you’re saying Mount Hua’s Sword Saint could jump out right in front of us?”

“What the hell! Don’t say such ominous things!”

The comrade who had taken his words calmly suddenly twisted into a look of horror.

For a moment, the man silenced under that intensity.

It was a strange thought: they were straining to find Mount Hua’s Sword Saint’s trail, yet at the same time desperately hoped he would never appear before them.

Because they were afraid.

Everyone had seen how he fought. No one wanted to cross blades with that fiend who was more like an Evil Sect demon than an Evil Sect man.

“…And isn’t that Plum Blossom Sword Ghost still human? He’s been that badly wounded, what way could he possibly have to get up and leave already?”

“Y-yeah, I suppose?”

“He wouldn’t be surprising even if he were dead. No, wait, he must be dead, for sure.”

The voice had neither strength nor conviction.

They prayed Mount Hua’s Sword Saint would be dead, yet a strange certainty that he couldn’t possibly be dead stirred fear.

“But Plum Blossom Sword Ghost? What’s that supposed to be?”

“Don’t you know? They’re calling Mount Hua’s Sword Saint that.”

“…What…”

“Honestly, does a dignified title like ‘Sword Saint’ suit that madman? The nickname’s already spread through the camp.”

The man nodded.

Indeed, Chung Myung was better called ‘Plum Blossom Sword Ghost’ than the dignified ‘Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.’ In fact, even ‘Plum Blossom Sword Ghost’ was inadequate for him.

“‘Plum Blossom Sword Ghost’ can rot. ‘Blood-Flower Demon Sword’ fits better.”

“…But he’s still from the Justice faction.”

“To hell with the Justice faction. What side of him looks like he’s from the Justice faction? Even the Demonic Cult wouldn’t be that vicious.”

“True.”

“No, the Demonic Cult is far worse.”

“What are you saying? The Demonic Cult guys…”

The man who was about to protest fell suddenly silent. He realized the voice had come from behind him.

Drip. Drip.

He heard the sound of a viscous liquid drop by his ear, freezing him to the bone. At the same time a smell too familiar, but now utterly unwelcome, wafted to his nostrils.

The scent of blood.

His shoulders trembled. He wanted to look away, to not turn his head and confirm that the horrible image in his mind was real.

But in urgent moments, instinct overrides thought. His body had already turned to identify who stood behind him.

The figure in black seemed to blend into the pitch-black night. All that stood out were a blood-stained sword and two eyes gleaming an icy blue.

And yet the man knew exactly who had appeared.

“Mo-Mount Hua’s Sword…”

Shreek.

Before he could finish the words, a searing pain ran through his neck. A chilling sensation snapped through his spine and vocal cords and then left his throat.

Hisss.

The sound of air escaping his throat hissed out. The man, unable even to scream, collapsed backward. Before his breath ceased, he saw his comrade’s head fly into the air.

The red spray of blood arched like a blossoming crimson flower. Seeing the cold gleam through it, the man thought one thing.

‘As I thought… the title is wrong…’

Before the thought finished, his body went cold.

Shreek.

Chung Myung, having dispatched another Ten Thousand People Clan swordsman, plunged his sword into the corpse’s chest.

Thud!

The heart was pierced and red blood stained the blade. Chung Myung, cloaking the sword’s gleam with blood instead of ash, melted back into the darkness, silent and impossibly swift.

Ssssh.

Moving like black water through the dark, Chung Myung slipped behind the Ten Thousand People Clan swordsmen who were advancing warily.

“What…”

Shreek!

Blood gushed in a steady stream from the half-slit throats. There was no time even to scream, only spasms and collapsing forward.

“Gurgle…”

They probably never knew how they died until their last breath.

Chung Myung’s gaze was ice-cold.

‘Not yet?’

It’s about time they discover him…

“W-Who’s there!”

At the cry, Chung Myung’s mouth twitched into a smile. Far off, Ten Thousand People Clan swordsmen looked at him with stunned faces.

He kicked off the ground and closed the distance in a single bound. He then cut off the leader’s head.

Crack!

With a bone-crunching sound, the Ten Thousand People Clan man’s head soared into the air. His face, still open-eyed, flashed pale in the faint moonlight before falling.

“Gasp!”

Shreek!

Chung Myung’s blade, having just severed the other throats, now met the last man’s neck.

Clack!

The man with the signal whistle froze with it in his mouth, eyes shaking as if seized by an earthquake. If he summoned the strength to blow, that sharp sword would split his neck without hesitation.

A razor-sharp gaze from the darkness watched him as if stalking.

Sweat poured from him in an instant. Then Mount Hua’s Sword Saint spoke briefly.

“Blow.”

It was an incomprehensible command, but there was no time to think. As he hesitated, Chung Myung’s sword began to pierce his skin.

“Can’t you hear me?”

Slice.

As the thin blade slashed his neck, a terrible sensation made his legs give way.

“Let go.”

He could think no more. There was no time. Trembling, he forced air into the whistle clenched in his hand.

Wheeeeeeeeet!

A high, piercing whistle rang through the dark sky. At that moment, Chung Myung’s mouth curled into a slight smile.

“That’s better.”

Shreek!

The man’s body collapsed. But the whistle had unmistakably revealed everyone’s location.

“This way!”

Shouts rose from all directions as chaos ensued. The corner of Chung Myung’s mouth twisted into a chilling grin.

❀ ❀ ❀

“The whistle sounded!”

“Run! Over there!”

“Yes!”

Everyone reacted to the sound and ran at full speed.

“Hurry!”

The underbrush trampled by the frantic men slowly rose again after they passed. As the racket faded and silence returned to the hills.

Under a large tree stump that had stood watch, a group cautiously revealed themselves.

Baek Cheon, the first to slip out, surveyed the area with tense eyes, then nodded and signaled.

“Let’s go.”

The revealed group numbered one hundred. As if of one body, they held their breath and began running northward, where the Ten Thousand People Clan had cleared the way.

Chapter 1346: Says He’ll Take That Man’s Life. (1)

“Enemy!”

“W-Where?”

The Ten Thousand Great Mountains, plunged in darkness, fell into chaos in an instant.

“This way!”

Those who heard the sharp voice hurriedly ran toward where the sound had come from.

“W-What the—!”

Soon they saw a body sprawled on the ground. Blood still gurgled from the throat, indicating he had been the one who cried out moments ago.

“He couldn’t have gone far! Search the area at once!”

It was both correct and wrong at the same time.

It’s accurate that he hadn’t gone far, but there was no need to search the vicinity; the person they sought was already surging up behind them.

Pa-aa-ang!

The sword tip ripped through the air and plunged into the backs of the Ten Thousand People Clan. The decisive stroke cut through sinew and nerve without hesitation.

“Kraaah!”

The dreadful scream echoed chillingly across the dark night sky.

“There!”

“Don’t let him get away!”

Dozens of Ten Thousand People Clan who had come running at the scream surged forward the moment they spotted Chung Myung holding a sword in the darkness.

Chung Myung glanced at them indifferently, expelled sword qi, and stomped the ground.

Paa-aat!

For a moment Chung Myung’s body seemed to elongate, and then he vanished from where he stood. Mount Hua’s distinctive black sword-robe and the heavy darkness intertwined, blurring his form in an instant.

“Over there! He went that way! Chase him now!”

“Where?!”

“I said over there, you idiots!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan shouted and pursued Chung Myung’s trail.

“T-There’s no sign!”

“Nonsense! Are you saying he shot up into the sky?!”

“B-But…!”

A warrior does not hunt solely with his eyes. They track a foe by presence, the flow of qi, faint sounds—by all those things.

But no matter how they sharpened their sense, they could not find his presence. It was as if he had truly vanished into thin air.

“Spread out! He couldn’t have gone far!”

Barring a specter, he couldn’t just vanish; he had to be nearby. Not far…

The hair on the man’s arms who shouted suddenly bristled.

He felt a sense that could freeze the soul, an uncanny chill like finely honed metal creeping up from his throat over his whole body.

‘W-When…’

Slash!

Stab!

The cold blade seared red as it was soaked by the hot blood gushing from the man’s neck.

“W-What the—!”

Startled by a groan behind them, the ones running ahead turned around. One who had been running behind them had already collapsed, foaming at the mouth, and Chung Myung’s figure was revealed behind him.

The panicked men wildly slashed with their swords as if in a seizure.

“Aaaah! Dieee!”

But if Chung Myung could be caught by chaotic, panicked swings, would the Ten Thousand People Clan have suffered so many casualties?

Kaa-ang! Ka-ang!

Chung Myung’s Hidden-Scent Plum Blossom Sword deflected the incoming blades and, like a starving viper, struck to tear into the enemy’s throat.

Kwa-deuk!

“Gurgle…”

Those whose throats were cut all collapsed with terror frozen in their eyes. Chung Myung had driven the sword deeper than usual and the blood flowing down thoroughly soaked it.

“There!”

“Sound the alarm, now!”

Beee-eeek!

A whistle blown by someone shrieked sharply like a scream. Chung Myung’s gaze deepened into darkness.

Bang!

An explosion cracked from the ground where he stood. Closing distance with a powerful step, Chung Myung swung swiftly and cut through the waists of the rushing men.

Paa-at!

Blood spurted from the severed faces.

“Bastarddd!”

Seeing their comrades split in front of them, the remaining men swung their swords in fear and rage.

Ka-kang!

Chung Myung slipped the blade falling from above and sent his sword to sever an enemy’s neck.

Before the split neck could even spurt blood, he kicked off the ground and launched himself backward.

“Don’t let him escape!”

Those who had rushed from all around shouted and pursued him.

At that moment, Chung Myung’s running form suddenly disappeared behind a ridge that rose like a blade.

“Follow him!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan also leapt forward to where Chung Myung had gone. But there was nothing to be seen.

Only a vast, suffocating heaviness of darkness remained.

“What…”

Nothing. Even when they instinctively spread their qi-sense, they detected nothing.

“Where did he go…!”

The man who had been screaming suddenly shut his mouth. His whole body went gooseflesh in an instant.

He tried to look up hastily, but before he could move, a sharp pain pierced his nape.

Puuuk!

A diagonally plunging sword pierced the man’s nape.

The blade severed vertebrae and pierced through his spine; his eyes bulged as if they might pop out. Not quite dead, indescribable pain gnawed his whole body. The man’s entire body convulsed and trembled.

“K-Kill him!”

Those nearby quickly swung their swords in defense. But before their blades could reach, Chung Myung’s sword, stuck through the body, scattered sword qi everywhere.

“Aaaaaah!”

“Kraah!”

The dying screams of those torn by the sword qi and the thick stench of blood billowed out. In an instant Chung Myung turned five into wraiths and melted back into the darkness.

“H-Here! This way!”

Those who arrived belatedly widened their eyes. All they could see were bodies cooling, mutilated by Chung Myung’s sword.

“T-That bastard…”

“There!”

Someone who spotted Chung Myung’s movement shouted. A few immediately ran after him without thinking.

“W-We too!”

Then a booming shout rang out behind them.

“Which direction is it!”

“Boss!”

It was Mo Hwi, the third leader of the Ghost Sickle Platform squad. His arrival brought a flash of hope to his men; pursuing Chung Myung among themselves had been a heavy burden.

“It’s the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, over there!”

“Follow me!”

Mo Hwi surged forward at once. Seeing him, the others, bolstered with courage, followed.

But the moment Mo Hwi ran over the rough terrain as if it were flat, his face froze. Surely a few had been trailing Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, yet there was no sign. The mountains were complex and steep, making visual confirmation impossible.

A wave of nausea-inducing terror surged through him, but Mo Hwi gritted his teeth and stepped on.

Thud! As he rounded the back of the ridge, the heavy smell of blood struck his nose.

“Ugh…”

Bodies of Ten Thousand People Clan lay scattered here and there. Perhaps the killings were recent—each time a chest rose and fell, hot blood gurgled from the gaping holes.

“That damned bastard…”

Mo Hwi’s face twisted in agony.

“Boss!”

“We can’t see his trail!”

“Don’t hesitate! He’s no ghost; where could he have gone? Split the squad in two. One group searches nearby, the others sweep the ridges!”

“Yes!”

Mo Hwi’s gaze fixed upward. If it was Chung Myung, he would use a ridge that jutted like a blade—especially in this deep darkness, perfect for concealment.

“Guh, guh-ruhk…”

A faint sound from below made Mo Hwi’s eyebrow twitch. Someone not fully beheaded was convulsing in pain.

“Tsk.”

Mo Hwi stepped forward and raised his blade—to grant a merciful death.

But the moment he lifted his blade to cut the subordinate’s throat, he noticed something odd. The man was desperately rolling his eyes to convey something—very, very urgently.

Paaa-aaaat!

At that moment a beam of light burst up through the subordinate’s belly and struck Mo Hwi.

“Aaaah!”

Mo Hwi twisted his body just enough to protect his vital points. He had sensed something strange in the subordinate’s desperate eyes.

But that did not mean he was safe.

Paa-aaat!

The corpse flipped and from beneath it the blood-splattered Chung Myung rose. Blades flashed in succession and sliced through both of Mo Hwi’s knees.

Slash! Slash!

Mo Hwi’s half-severed knees buckled and gave way.

“Aaaah!”

Even then Mo Hwi attempted to swing his blade—he was certainly fit to command a Ten Thousand People Clan division—but Chung Myung’s blade was ruthless and in an instant cut through his wrists and forearms.

Slash!

“Guh-ruk…”

Chung Myung’s sword then sliced through Mo Hwi’s sternum.

Mo Hwi, unable to hold himself, staggered as if to topple forward. Before he could, Chung Myung had moved behind him and grabbed a fistful of his hair.

Mo Hwi’s head was jerked forcibly back. The Dark Fragrant Plum Blossom Sword touched his exposed neck.

“Ugh… Uuugh…”

When he barely managed to turn his eyes to look behind, Mo Hwi saw Chung Myung’s eyes—cool and emitting a bluish radiance even in the thick darkness.

“Mo-Mount Hua…”

Pook!

“Aaaaaah!”

A harrowing, pitiful scream tore from Mo Hwi’s throat. That awful cry echoed like a horn calling allies and spread in all directions.

“Huuu… Huuuu…”

Though the man in his grip trembled helplessly in pain, Chung Myung’s gaze did not waver. He simply looked down at him with the same chilling cold as before.

Even while shaking with pain, Mo Hwi thought it strange. He was not afraid of death. But at the moment he met those eyes, a terror beyond death consumed him.

“Scream louder.”

“…”

“That will draw more of them. Idiots who don’t even know this will make their graves.”

“Y-You son of a—”

Kwa-deuk!

Chung Myung’s sword drove into Mo Hwi’s neck. In an instant it sliced muscle and vessels and, without hesitation, severed the cervical bone.

“Guh, guh-ruk…”

Froth of blood bubbled; the light left Mo Hwi’s eyes swiftly.

Without a second thought Chung Myung kicked Mo Hwi’s body aside and, sensing the footsteps of those converging from all around, let out a small, cold smile.

Thud.

Then he walked toward the enemies who hesitated nervously.

Those who had been hesitating backed away in pale fear as Chung Myung approached. He asked:

“Why the hesitation?”

His indifferent gaze swept over them once.

“Weren’t you the ones who came to kill me?”

“Y-You madman…”

Chung Myung’s gaze flicked toward the sky. It was pitch dark—just like the many nights he’d faced on this mountain long ago.

“I see you understand.”

The raised sword slanted across Chung Myung’s face.

“That the positions have been reversed.”

Chung Myung’s eyes, half-revealed from behind the sword’s face, emitted a chilling light.

“Let’s continue. There’s still much night left.”

Then, horrifying screams wracked with pain rang out like a signal.

Chapter 1347: Says He’ll Take That Man’s Life. (2)

“Over there! I heard a scream over there!”

“Hurry!”

Ten Thousand People Clan members ran at full speed.

“Leader! To the side! A scream came from the side!”

“Damn it! What the hell is going on? We’re going this way!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan members who had been running toward the front sharply changed direction and dashed off. Soon, a few groups passed by where they had just been, as if following.

And so, by the time the world was once again cloaked in deep silence.

“Let’s go.”

A group that had been hiding among the thick bushes leapt out.

“This way.”

“Yes!”

Baek Cheon and Im So-Byeong led the Heavenly Friends Alliance and Southern Island Sect survivors as they began sprinting across the land where the enemies had vanished.

“Soso.”

“Don’t worry, sasuk! I won’t let go even if I die.”

Tang Soso, carrying Yu Yiseol on her back, replied boldly. Baek Cheon nodded at her. Yoon Jong, carrying Jo Gul, also followed nimbly.

“What about ahead?”

“I’m watching.”

Baek Cheon drew on all his energy and spread his sense forward. What they had to do now was not to fight the enemy, but to have Chung Myung draw attention while they escaped the Ten-Thousand Great Mountains safely.

“This way, quickly!”

In an instant, Baek Cheon flashed his eyes, gave quick orders, and changed direction. Just after they hid in a nook of the towering ridge, a group of Ten Thousand People Clan members sped down the path they had just been running.

After they moved away, Baek Cheon led everyone and ran again.

“Green Forest King. How far is it?”

“The Ten‑Thousand Great Mountains span thousands of li horizontally, but not that far lengthwise. It won’t take even two hours!”

“Then let’s hurry!”

At that moment, the Southern Island Sect’s sect leader Geum Yang‑Baek came running from behind.

“Acting Sect Leader.”

“Yes, Sect leader.”

“A considerable number are heading toward the direction of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint! Is that all right?”

Deep concern shadowed his eyes.

At this rate, they might escape the Ten‑Thousand Great Mountains without a major engagement.

But that would mean the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint left behind would have to face all the Ten Thousand People Clan members alone, wouldn’t it?

The Southern Island Sect, whose majority of survivors were injured to varying degrees, might not have the right to speak, but Geum Yang‑Baek couldn’t ignore it. Southern Island Sect’s debt to the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint could no longer be expressed in words.

“The Mount Hua’s Sword Saint isn’t in good shape. Someone has to help…”

“No, don’t talk nonsense.”

It was Im So‑Byeong, not Baek Cheon, who cut him off coldly. Running beside Baek Cheon, he glared at Geum Yang‑Baek with a contorted face.

“Who is helping whom! Who is who!”

“Ah, no… but the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint’s injuries…”

“Why do you think he got injured in the first place? Because he was lugging a dead weight around, wasn’t he? Would he have suffered like that if he were alone?”

Geum Yang‑Baek sealed his lips tightly. He understood what the Green Forest King was trying to say.

It wasn’t wrong. The road from Southern Island Sect to this great mountain was extremely harsh, but if Chung Myung had tried to break through alone, it might not have been that difficult.

A few strong ones can’t easily fight perfectly against many weak opponents, but scattering those many weak opponents isn’t that hard.

A strong one with superior mobility can choose the time and place to fight. So they can swing their sword where and when they want until their opponent tires, then withdraw.

But in this battle Chung Myung met the enemies after having that mobility sealed. He couldn’t fall back because he was tired, nor flee because of injury.

It was like fighting with his body tightly bound in chains.

Conversely, because everyone put distance between themselves, for the first time Chung Myung had been freed from the shackles that bound him.

“People should know their place! What help are we offering? Not grabbing his ankle is helping him!”

Geum Yang‑Baek’s face twisted at the blatant contempt and scorn. But he also knew that the remark wasn’t entirely wrong.

Baek Cheon quietly opened his mouth to mediate the situation.

“First, focus on getting out of the mountain. He will come on his own.”

“…Understood, Acting Sect Leader.”

When Geum Yang‑Baek stepped back with a stern face, Baek Cheon let out a deep sigh.

The Green Forest King had clearly gone too far, but Baek Cheon didn’t want to blame him. The Green Forest King’s face was also hardened, unlike someone who had just scolded others.

His pride had been hurt as well.

That was understandable: Im So‑Byeong was the Lord of the Green Forest. A strategist once counted among the best in the land, now reduced to a burden, would find it hard to accept his situation calmly.

So he must have been furious at Geum Yang‑Baek for forcing him to explain the situation himself.

Baek Cheon exhaled a long breath.

‘Now is not the time for such thoughts.’

Everyone has their role at any time. What Baek Cheon had to do now was not to waste pride, but to lead the wounded and escape the mountain as quickly as possible.

“This way!”

“Yes!”

Sensing movement ahead, Baek Cheon nimbly changed direction. But this time his reaction was a bit late, and they spotted the rear of those who hadn’t had time to hide.

“Enemies!”

“Kill them!”

“Ugh.”

Baek Cheon and several others bit their lips and charged the enemies. The few Ten Thousand People Clan members were slaughtered in an instant.

“Guh!”

Seeing those collapsing with the sound of air escaping, Baek Cheon turned his head and gave orders.

“Moving together definitely draws too much attention. Scatter as we discussed earlier.”

“Yes!”

Namgung Do‑Wei, Tang Pae, and Hae Yeon nodded firmly. Those whose injuries weren’t severe had recovered considerably with just two days of rest. So leading the Southern Island Sect’s disciples respectively wouldn’t be a big problem.

“Rendezvous point as planned!”

“Understood, Acting Sect Leader.”

“Good luck!”

“Yes!”

Those who had been told in advance took their groups and scattered in all directions. Watching their backs, Baek Cheon looked at Yoon Jong and Tang Soso, who were guarding him, and said quietly.

“Let’s go too.”

“Yes, sasuk.”

Baek Cheon, leading the Southern Island Sect’s disciples, ran forward, then glanced back at the Ten‑Thousand Great Mountains shrouded in darkness.

‘Don’t be late, damn you.’

❀ ❀ ❀

“Unit Flowing Seven has been hit!”

“Location?”

“W‑well, the location can’t be determined right now!”

“What kind of bullshit are you spouting! You said they were attacked!”

“R‑right now everyone is rapidly moving from their assigned sectors. Exactly where they were hit…”

“That’s not an answer!”

Ho Ga‑Myung’s aide emitted a grim murderous aura at the reporter. Startled, the subordinate flattened himself, but that wouldn’t conjure an answer out of nothing.

The news that followed was equally stomach‑turning.

“Unit Five Men has been hit too!”

“The Third one Ghost Sword troops also suffered!”

“The enemy’s exact position cannot be determined at present! Our reporting lines are insufficient!”

No clear conclusion could be drawn even after putting together the nonstop yelling of the messengers.

All that could be gleaned from the reports was that the hastily organized men scattered across the Ten‑Thousand Great Mountains were being slaughtered here and there.

“C-commander.”

The aide called Ho Ga‑Myung in a trembling voice.

But Ho Ga‑Myung, having heard all the reports, did not react and only stared at the darkened mountain.

Swallowing dry saliva, the aide thought to call Ho Ga‑Myung again but finally fell silent. It wasn’t that Ho Ga‑Myung was unaware of the situation; he didn’t react because reacting would be useless.

Ho Ga‑Myung’s eyes were dim. His chapped lips parted and a hoarse voice leaked out.

“…It feels like we’ve stuck our heads into a tiger’s den.”

Facing the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint… no, that Plum Blossom Sword Ghost couldn’t be easy for scattered men. He knew that much. But the reports now were far beyond even his expectations.

It felt as if they weren’t chasing him, but he was luring everyone to where he wanted and hunting them one by one.

By now the Southern Island Sect’s main force was probably escaping the Ten Thousand Great Mountains. Ho Ga‑Myung wasn’t someone who couldn’t predict that.

But there was no way to catch them now. With communications collapsed and people charging the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, there was no way to recall them and issue new orders.

All that remained was the outcome of being thoroughly toyed with by the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint and losing the main force.

Since the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint had recovered and appeared faster than he expected, the result was almost predetermined.

‘From where did it go wrong?’

Should he have been more thorough? Once they lost the trail of that bastard, if they hadn’t rashly dispersed forces and had waited calmly, things might have been better.

No, even if not that, there would have been other ways.

But Ho Ga‑Myung thought such musings were meaningless.

The more finely tuned a plan, the more a small twist can render it useless. No matter how perfectly he planned, that Plum Blossom Sword Ghost could forcibly warp the plan.

All of this was the price of targeting an opponent they couldn’t handle.

“…Withdraw the troops.”

“C-commander!”

“We must reduce unnecessary sacrifices.”

The aides bit their lips until they bled.

Although they knew it was the right order, they couldn’t bring themselves to transmit it immediately. The moment they retreated, all plans would end in failure—and wouldn’t that mean Ho Ga‑Myung’s utter defeat?

They all knew what consequences this failure would bring.

“C-commander! We haven’t…”

“Don’t make me commit a sin against the Alliance lord any longer.”

In the end, the aides silently bowed their heads.

The sacrifice was far too great to claim the hundred people loss or the Southern Island Sect’s heads as achievement. The unauthorized operation had failed, so even Ho Ga‑Myung of the world couldn’t escape responsibility.

Ho Ga‑Myung clenched his eyes shut, about to let everything go.

Screeeeeech!

A strange sound was heard. At that moment his eyes snapped open.

A horn? No, it wasn’t a horn. It was the cry of a beast he well knew.

‘Could it be?’

He hastily raised his head. In the night sky without even starlight, a faint red glow hovered in the middle.

When Ho Ga‑Myung instinctively thrust his arm forward, the light hovering in the air solidified like lightning and settled onto it.

Flap.

It was a crimson hawk.

It flapped once and sat on his arm; Ho Ga‑Myung’s eyes trembled as he looked at the small hawk.

“A‑Alliance lord…”

The aides who recognized the hawk’s identity turned sallow.

They all knew that this small hawk with red and black feathers was Jang Ilso’s trusted beast, the one that carried only his orders.

Ho Ga‑Myung carefully opened the tube tied to the hawk’s foot and took out a letter. Even until he unfolded it, his hand trembled faintly.

While he read, everyone held their breath waiting for his reaction. But after reading the letter, Ho Ga‑Myung closed his eyes for a moment and showed no emotion.

At last, after suffocating anticipation, Ho Ga‑Myung opened his eyes again. Folding the letter, he spoke.

“Withdraw those fighting him to the second line.”

“C-commander, then…?”

“Continue the pursuit.”

“…Yes?”

Ho Ga‑Myung’s eyes reflected the cold light of the darkened mountain.

“The Alliance lord says he will take that bastard’s life.”

A strange heat of joy bloomed on his face.

Ho Ga‑Myung could hardly contain himself. The Mount Hua’s Sword Saint… no, one ought to call him the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost now. But…

‘If the Alliance lord steps in, everything changes.’

No matter how much of a Plum Blossom Sword Ghost he was, now he would have to accept the end of his fate.

From now on, the one he must face is none other than Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso.

Chapter 1348: Says He’ll Take That Man’s Life. (3)

“Stay still.”

Namgung Do-Wei slowly lowered the hand he had raised.

Southern Island’s disciples who saw the signal held their breath and tried to erase all sound.

Tadadadadak!

With swift footsteps, a group passed in front of the bushes where they hid.

“This way!”

At the shouted command, Southern Island’s disciples bowed lower and hid themselves. Their throats tightened with tension.

Fortunately, those who passed ahead did not discover their trail. Of course, that was to be expected.

“Aaaaaaaaagh!”

A desperate scream like that from afar makes the enemy’s presence unmistakable; who, out of fear there might be others nearby, would bother to search for more traces?

‘Taoist.’

Namgung Do-Wei forced himself not to look toward the direction of the scream. The way he could help Chung Myung now was to lead them away from here as quickly as possible.

“We will move.”

“Yes!”

Paat.

Namgung Do-Wei sprang forward, leading the way. His gaze was fixed on a high peak ahead.

‘From the highest peak, the fifth to the left.’

But their steps headed to the left side of the peaks. If everyone chose the same path, the effort of splitting the group would be wasted. They needed to pick different routes to avoid enemy attention and then regroup.

“What the hell…”

Namgung Do-Wei slashed without hesitation. The throat of a Ten Thousand People Clan member who had turned his head in surprise was pierced instantly.

“Gurgle…”

Having been pierced through the vocal cords, he couldn’t scream; he convulsed and collapsed. Namgung Do-Wei snatched him up before the thud, to shove him into a hidden corner where no one would notice.

“Huk…”

A bead of thick sweat ran down his brow. Though he had replenished some stamina, constantly watching for enemies while moving was harder than he thought.

Spreading one’s qi presence was ultimately an exercise of internal qi. Using light footwork skill while dispersing qi sense at the same time made him feel his spirit drained several times over.

‘Don’t complain.’

It was only for a short while. He would never admit to being exhausted after so little. Baek Cheon had led them for over this span, and Chung Myung had led them for days.

To say this was hard would not allow the proud Namgung Family’s pride.

“Let’s hurry!”

“Yes, Young lord!”

Namgung Do-Wei pushed off the ground again and sent the words with his mind.

’Please do not be late

Chapter 1349: Says He’ll Take That Man’s Life. (4)

“This way.”

Ho Ga-Myung spoke shortly. Yu Gong stared at him with a hardened face. For a moment, everyone’s gaze around them concentrated.

‘Damn it.’

It was the moment the hope—that by keeping as quiet as a dead mouse they might do nothing until this pursuit ended—was utterly shattered.

“Can’t you hear me?”

When Ho Ga-Myung urged, the atmosphere around them chilled accordingly. As if to say that if they ignored the commander’s words again, he’d cut their throats at once.

Yu Gong bit his lip and forcibly moved a foot that would not lift. Ho Ga-Myung’s calm, sinking gaze swept over Yu Gong.

“What do you think?”

“…What do you mean?”

“I won’t forgive you for beating around the bush. That’s not acceptable.”

“…”

“There’s one thing I want to confirm. Whether you’re willing to cooperate or not is going to help you.”

The tips of Yu Gong’s fingers trembled slightly.

Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze remained indifferent and cold, but Yu Gong standing before him couldn’t see it that way.

It felt as if that sunken gaze was saying to him: I already know everything, so don’t you dare try anything.

“Well?”

When Ho Ga-Myung asked again, Yu Gong’s lips moved. But words failed to come out and crumbled inside his mouth.

Ho Ga-Myung looked at Yu Gong with emotionless eyes for a long moment, then turned his head.

“I recall there was one more person, wasn’t there?”

At his words, everyone’s gaze turned to the person behind them.

“Ah…”

As all eyes suddenly turned to him, Go Hong, Yu Gong’s sajae, gave a twitching convulsion in the shoulder. The color drained from his face in an instant.

“Bring him here.”

“Yes.”

Members of the Ten Thousand People Clan grabbed Go Hong by both shoulders and dragged him before Ho Ga-Myung. Go Hong, his face ashen, looked at those holding him but couldn’t bring himself to resist and was dragged along helplessly.

Thud.

Those who had dragged Go Hong shoved him before Ho Ga-Myung. Go Hong, who had fallen and braced himself on the ground, forced his head up.

“I… I…”

The moment his eyes met Ho Ga-Myung’s, Yu Gong’s face turned so pale it went nearly blue.

“I will not force you.”

“…”

“But if you’re here, you must follow the Evil Sect’s way. Within the Evil Sect, the only way to preserve your life is to prove your usefulness.”

“I… I…”

Go Hong’s shoulders trembled. He couldn’t be unaware of what they wanted. He had been as quiet as a dead mouse, yet had kept vigilant about their movements the whole time.

What they wanted from him was to betray the sahyung of the Southern Island Sect.

The reason he had come this far was simply to survive. And to save the people of his hometown.

It wasn’t Go Hong who decided in that process that even the sahyung-sajae could be sold out. It was Yu Gong.

“So?”

His lips were parched. Spitting wetly, Go Hong opened his mouth in a trembling voice.

“W-what are you saying… I…”

“Can you prove the value of your life?”

Ho Ga-Myung’s intimidating gaze pressed down on Go Hong.

Go Hong’s gaze drifted somewhere without his control. It was an instinctive glance no firm will could suppress.

Ho Ga-Myung did not miss Go Hong’s instinctive struggle to survive.

“There’s something.”

Exposed by that brief glance, Go Hong’s eyes trembled as if an earthquake had struck. Ho Ga-Myung spoke slowly.

“Then it’s simple. There are only two options left. Cooperate and survive, or keep your loyalty and die with them.”

“…”

“Of course, not just you, but those remaining in the Southern Island Sect as well.”

“Ugh…”

Cold sweat began to soak his entire body.

Since being brought to Ho Ga-Myung, no physical harm had been done to him so far.

But now it seemed he’d rather be hacked all over his body. Compared to enduring words sharper than any blade, physical pain would be nothing.

“You’ve been given enough time. Decide.”

“I…”

Go Hong’s pale lips trembled.

The desire to live, and yet the will that he couldn’t betray those who had once been comrades.

Those two irreconcilable things mashed his head into a pulp.

“I… I…”

Go Hong’s eyes wandered aimlessly. So many gazes were still fixed only on him.

Ho Ga-Myung’s indifferent stare, the Ten Thousand People Clan’s hostile looks, and Yu Gong’s gaze that was impossible to read.

Suddenly his vision swam. The world spun, and a sick feeling rose in his stomach.

“I… I…”

When Go Hong ultimately couldn’t utter his decision, Ho Ga-Myung cut him off without remorse.

“This one has no use. Kill him.”

“Yes!”

No sooner had the command been given than the Ten Thousand People Clan members on either side drew their blades. Knowing this wasn’t just a threat, Go Hong widened his eyes and cried out desperately.

“W-wait, just a moment! I, I…!”

Whoosh!

But the blades swung by the Ten Thousand People Clan members flew inexorably toward his neck.

“Stop!”

At that moment, someone shouted loudly as if seized by a fit.

The blade that had flown in, carrying a cold gleam, stopped as it touched Go Hong’s neck.

Drip.

A stream of blood ran down from the split skin.

“I’ll tell you.”

The shout came from Yu Gong. He bit his lips so hard they went blue and squeezed his eyes shut.

“S-sahyung!”

Ho Ga-Myung’s blade-like gaze turned toward Yu Gong.

“I have no intention of giving you time.”

“No, sahyung—no, sahyung!”

As Go Hong urgently objected, Yu Gong opened his eyes and looked at him blankly. The cold gleam on the blade touching Go Hong’s neck began to deepen again.

Yu Gong finally pointed to a large tree standing ahead and spoke.

“There.”

His fingertips trembled slightly. It was a sight that showed how tangled his inner feelings were, but no one cared about his heart.

“Sahyung! Stop…!”

“There’s a pattern carved into that tree.”

Go Hong bit his lip until it bled. Ho Ga-Myung glanced at his reaction. From that alone, it didn’t seem Yu Gong was lying.

On the upper part of the large tree Yu Gong pointed to, there was a faint pattern carved that was hard to spot without looking carefully.

“What does it mean?”

“…It’s hard to explain in words.”

“Why?”

“Sahyung!”

Go Hong shouted with a pained expression. But Yu Gong, seeming resigned, laid out what he knew without hiding it. After all, deceiving Ho Ga-Myung would be impossible.

“That’s the sword mark of the seventh form among Southern Island Sect’s ‘Rinding the Wind Breaking the Waves’ sword techniques.”

“Hmm?”

“A sword… or rather, a blade will do.”

When Yu Gong reached out asking for a weapon, the Ten Thousand People Clan members looked toward Ho Ga-Myung as if seeking permission.

“Give it to him.”

“Yes.”

They handed over the blade they were holding. Yu Gong took the unfamiliar blade into his hand and exhaled briefly. Then he unfolded a sword form into the air without channeling internal qi.

“And that form ends like this.”

When the abbreviated form finished, the tip of the blade pointed in one direction. Everyone’s gaze followed it. The tip of the blade pointed toward a certain grove.

“Do you understand?”

“The direction the sword points when the form ends…”

Ho Ga-Myung nodded as if he understood.

“You used your head well.”

It was certainly logical.

No matter how intricate a code, it will eventually be cracked. And if you use a code easily understood by the opponent, it can cause unspeakable catastrophic damage.

But with this method, it’s hard to figure out the meaning of the code. Only a disciple trained in Southern Island Sect’s sword school could interpret it.

‘A prestigious sect is a prestigious sect indeed.’

Each troop would include those who could reliably recognize and interpret this code—people with an eye keen enough not to miss these signs even while moving swiftly.

Ho Ga-Myung, nodding lightly, asked again.

“The distance?”

“I don’t know.”

Yu Gong shook his head.

“Basically, this mark is used with ten li as the base, but that can change depending on circumstances. We don’t know how they agreed among themselves…”

“But you mean that if we proceed in this direction, the next mark will appear.”

“That’s right.”

“If not…”

Ho Ga-Myung stared at Yu Gong with cold eyes.

“We’ll encounter those ahead of them.”

The faint hostility that had lingered in Yu Gong’s eyes drained away at those words.

“…That will be so.”

“In other words, to find them, we need to keep you alive. Is that correct?”

“…”

“Huh, so that’s why you explained it so kindly.”

Ho Ga-Myung quietly nodded.

“Good.”

“…”

“However, I won’t allow stalling. You seem clever, so you can probably guess why I brought so many.”

“…I know.”

“Lead the way.”

Yu Gong silently nodded. He sighed and moved forward, when suddenly he noticed someone glaring at him. It was Go Hong. His eyes were full of bloodshot hatred.

‘Am I even the target of his resentment?’

Anyway, by coming this far he was already a traitor who had sold out his sect. What could he possibly be trying to protect now—willing to give his only remaining life for it?

If he had that spirit to begin with, he wouldn’t have come this far.

Seeing him only searching for someone to resent—neither the courage to risk his life nor the guts to betray his sect—filled Yu Gong with an indescribable disgust.

He had to survive.

Right now, that was the only goal left for Yu Gong.

All his pride as a Southern Island Sect’s disciple, even the path he once dreamed of—he had thrown them into the manure heap. All that remained were his life and the safety of the dependents left in Southern Island.

Even so, Yu Gong couldn’t bring himself to meet Go Hong’s eyes. He wanted to glare back at him with venom, but oddly, he looked away first.

“Move out.”

“…Yes.”

Watching Yu Gong grit his teeth and run forward, Ho Ga-Myung curled the corner of his mouth up.

“Let’s go.”

“Yes!”

The others followed Yu Gong and plunged into the dense forest.

‘Indeed, the move you prepared was deadly, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.’

But it was certain Chung Myung did not know either: we had a trump they wouldn’t expect prepared on our side as well.

“They wouldn’t expect a jackal to command a dog. Even someone like you in the world.”

Even if they did expect it, it wouldn’t matter. This battlefield no longer belonged to Ho Ga-Myung.

Even if he were to be struck down by that bastard again, the outcome wouldn’t change.

The flow of this battlefield was heading into the hands of the one who should rightfully seize everything.

A strange heat rippled in Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes.

He too wanted to confirm it. No, because of who he was, he wanted to confirm it all the more.

Mount Hua’s Sword Saint—no, the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost. He wanted to see how the Alliance lord would trample that overwhelming monster whom he had never even used a hand against.

When the Hegemony Lord, who had shown an inexplicable kindness to that Plum Blossom Sword Ghost all this time, finally draws the sword he had kept buried, what would happen then?

Chapter 1350: Says He’ll Take That Man’s Life. (5)

“Sasuk! What’s the matter?”

“Well… that’s…”

The faces of Mount Hua’s disciples were very serious.

They were looking at the pavilion that Hyun Jong currently used as his office and quarters. A short while ago, the senior figures of the Heavenly Friends Alliance had all gathered there with grim faces.

“Has something happened?”

“You are asking me? What would I know?”

Baek Sang sighed in frustration. With Baek Cheon absent, it was clear he was the eldest of the Baek disciples, but that didn’t mean he had heard anything special.

But the other disciples didn’t know Baek Sang’s situation, so questions poured out continuously.

Jong Hoe asked again with a stern face.

“Could it be… that something has happened to the sasuk and sahyungs who went to the Southern Island Sect?”

“You blabbermouth!”

In an instant, hostile glares flew from all sides. Stung by those looks, Jong Hoe shrank his neck.

“Ah, no, if that’s not the case then why is everyone like that…”

“The Four Sects Alliance might have moved, or those Nine Great Sects might have done some foolery again!”

“Frankly, the sahyungs probably haven’t even arrived in the Southern Island Sect yet; how could something be happening, you fool?”

“Shut it before I sew your mouth shut.”

Jong Hoe bowed his head deeply and Baek Sang sighed.

“For now, let’s wait. The Sect leader has gone in; once a conclusion is reached, he should give some notice, shouldn’t he?”

“…Yes, sasuk.”

But the Mount Hua disciples couldn’t relax their stern faces. Hyun Jong’s expression as he strode to his quarters had seemed far more serious than usual. Though they wanted to believe otherwise, that look alone made them sense that something had happened to those who had gone to the Southern Island Sect.

“Sasuk, what if…”

“No questions.”

“…Yes?”

Baek Sang said with a resolute face.

“I know what you’re trying to ask, but there’s no need to ask it. We have only one thing to do.”

“Yes. Sasuk!”

Soon a firm resolve settled on the faces of the other disciples.

Everyone’s gaze had shifted now — toward the far south.

The senior figures of the Heavenly Friends Alliance all wore hardened expressions.

“…Are you saying the Ten Thousand People Clan is pursuing them?”

“That is so.”

Meng So, who had fallen silent for a moment, spoke as if in pain.

“The Ten Thousand People Clan…”

Confusion flickered across Meng So’s face for a moment.

“Are you saying the Ten Thousand People Clan personally went out to strike the Southern Island Sect in the rear? In this situation?”

Hyun Jong nodded without speaking. But Meng So, still unsettled, asked again.

“And they began their attack precisely after the Heavenly Friends Alliance arrived in the Southern Island? Is that it?”

“It seems so.”

“Huh…”

He let out a hollow laugh that didn’t fit the situation. It was so absurd that only laughter came out.

A coincidence? No; this could not be explained as a coincidence.

Originally, the Heavenly Friends Alliance could not have reached Southern Island Sect so quickly. If they had gone by normal routes, it would have taken at least several months.

But those dispatched by the Heavenly Friends Alliance arrived unnaturally fast, and thanks to that they were able to rescue and lead them out before the Ten Thousand People Clan erased the Southern Island Sect.

“I thought they would strike the Southern Island Sect when the war started, but that assessment was wrong…”

“They knew it too, I suppose.”

Tang Gunak said in a grave voice. Meng So voiced his question.

“What do you mean?”

“That even if they attacked the Southern Island Sect, the Nine Great Sects would not move.”

Meng So couldn’t bring himself to answer.
